# سجلّ نتائج تدقيق المراحل 11 → 20 — **خامٌ، غير مؤكَّد**

> **اقرأ هذا أولاً، وإلّا أضرّ بك هذا الملفّ.**
>
> هذه **ادّعاءاتُ وكلاء**، لا نتائج مؤكَّدة. أربع جولات تدقيق ماتت كلّها عند مرحلة
> الكتابة (`write:*`) على حدّ الاستعمال، فلم يُكتب أيّ تقرير — وأُنقذت هذه الأسطر
> من سجلّات الوكلاء (`journal.jsonl`) قبل أن تُمحى.
>
> **والفرق بين الادّعاء والنتيجة ليس شكلياً:** في تدقيق المراحل 6→10، الذي اكتمل
> بمرحلته المعاكسة، **سقط ٢١ ادّعاءً من ٢٨** أمام التدقيق. فالنسبة المتوقّعة هنا
> أنّ أغلب ما تقرؤه أدناه **خطأ**. لا يُصلَح بندٌ من هذا الملفّ قبل أن يُقرَأ في
> الشيفرة ويُثبَت.
>
> **ما تحقّقتُ منه بيدي حتى الآن — بقراءة الشيفرة لا بتصديق الوكيل:**
>
> | البند | الحكم | المآل |
> |---|---|---|
> | الاستيلاء على صفّ الضيف برقم الهاتف (`guest-checkout.service.ts`، `auth.service.ts`) | **صحيح** — والشيفرة أكّدته سطراً بسطر | أُغلق في **ق-232** |
> | فجوة التسويات (`finance.service.ts:330`، `merchant-wallet.service.ts:91`) | **صحيح، وأسوأ ممّا وُصف**: كان السلوك الافتراضيّ لا احتمالاً نادراً | أُغلق في **ق-233** |
> | «`/api/health` ثابتٌ لا يلمس القاعدة فلا يكشف انقطاعاً» | **صحيح** — أثبته انقطاعٌ حقيقيّ في 2026-09-09: `/health` أجاب 200 بينما كلّ مسارٍ يلمس القاعدة أجاب 500 | مفتوح |
>
> **التالي المقترح للتحقّق** (أعلى احتمالاً بحكم تكرارها عبر وكلاء مستقلّين):
> حصّة الطلبات تُنفقها الطلبات الملغاة (`entitlement.service.ts:79`) · سقف الخطة
> يرفض المتسوّق عند آخر خطوةٍ بلا أن يُخبَر التاجر أنّ متجره توقّف
> (`storefront.service.ts:864`) · تغيير بريد المالك بلا إثبات ملكية ولا إنهاء
> جلسات (`owner-account.service.ts:71`).

---

## المراحل 11 → 15

**70 نتيجة** رفعها الوكلاء · **33** منها فقط بلغت التدقيق المعاكس (**14** سقطت · **19** صمدت). الباقي **لم يُدقَّق بعد** — وهذا هو الحدّ الذي يجب أن يُقرأ به هذا الملفّ كلّه.

### 11-15-01 · **عالية** · `.github/workflows/ci.yml:607`

**الادّعاء:** The PC-1 platform-contact suite has a package script and no CI step, so the owner-only wall on the platform's published contact channels and the owner's own sign-in-email change run on no pull request

**الأثر المزعوم:** PUT /platform/settings/contact rewrites the email, phone and WhatsApp number that every merchant on the platform is told to call, and POST /platform/account/email changes who can sign in as the owner. The only suite that proves a platform-staff session holding all nine permission codes is refused at that wall (403), that the owner's password hash survives an email change byte for byte, and that an address already held by a merchant returns 409 and not 500, is pc1 — and it runs on no PR and on no push to main or release. A regression in requirePlatformOwner on that route merges green: a staff member repoints the platform's support number at his own phone and the audit row is the only trace. The team's own rule (verify locally before CI, because Actions minutes are billed and exhausted) means nobody runs this by hand either.

**الدليل المزعوم:** package.json:78 defines "pc1:test": "node --import tsx phase1-backend/tests/pc1.platform-contact.integration.mjs". .github/workflows/ci.yml enumerates every suite as an explicit `run: pnpm <script>` step (ci.yml:215-607 for the integration suites, ci.yml:719-778 for the browser suites); a grep of ci.yml for `pc1` returns nothing — it is the only *:test script in package.json with no step. Nothing globs the directory either: the only two readdirSync calls over phase1-backend/tests are frozen-clock.guard.mjs:62 and docs-freshness.guard.mjs:137, and the latter filters to `.guard.mjs`. The routes left uncovered: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/platform.ts:244-250 (`app.put("/platform/settings/contact")`, wall at :246 `await requirePlatformOwner(request)`) and phase1-backend/src/http/routes/owner-security.ts:74-77 calling changeOwnerEmail, which writes the audit row at phase1-backend/src/services/owner-account.service.ts:76. Grep across phase1-backend for `platform/settings/contact` and `changeOwnerEmail` returns hits only in pc1.platform-contact.integration.mjs:160-217 and openapi.document.ts. The suite is substantive, not a stub: its header (pc1.platform-contact.integration.mjs:1-30) lists seven promises, and it TRUNCATEs 12 tables at :57-59. docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:210-296 documents this exact surface as the owner's lock-out recovery path.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add one step to the integration job beside the other suites, e.g. after ci.yml:607: `- name: "Integration — PC-1 platform contact and the owner's sign-in email"` / `run: pnpm pc1:test`. It needs no new service — it uses the same CODIQ_TEST_DATABASE_URL and already clears storefront_rate_limits, so it satisfies login:counter:guard as written.

### 11-15-02 · **عالية** · `.github/workflows/ci.yml:11`

**الادّعاء:** The `release` branch — the one production deploys from — has no branch protection at all, so any write-scoped credential can force-push straight to production without a PR, a review, or a required check

**الأثر المزعوم:** Phase 11's acceptance criterion was "a direct push to main is refused". That still holds for `main`, but ق-134 moved production off `main` on 2026-08-31 and the protection never moved with it. Today the branch that reaches every Iraqi merchant's live store, every shopper's phone number and address, and the COD settlement figures is completely unguarded: no required status check, no PR requirement, force-pushes allowed, deletion allowed, admins unrestricted. One stolen or mis-scoped GitHub token, one `gh auth` on a shared machine, one wrong `git push` — and arbitrary code is deploying to production with nothing to refuse it and no reviewable record, because a force-push also rewrites the deploy history that ق-134 calls "مؤرَّخ وقابل للمراجعة". The single control the owner believes is in place — Railway's «Wait for CI» — cannot save this: CI is defined by `.github/workflows/ci.yml` *in the pushed commit*, so a push that also empties that file produces a green `ci` and deploys.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:6-11` triggers CI on pushes to `main` and `release`, with the comment at :8-11 stating «ق-134: production deploys from `release` behind Railway's «Wait for CI»». 2. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:130` states it outright: «**الإنتاج مربوط بفرع `release`، لا بـ`main`.**»; :134 «دمج إلى `main` — **لا ينشر شيئاً**»; :135 the deploy act is a merge `main` → `release`; :136 claims the protection is «Wait for CI» مُفعَّل. 3. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:9` claims the pipeline's guarantee: «حماية `main` تلزم Pull Request وتجعل نجاح `ci / integration` شرطًا للحماية، بما في ذلك المسؤولون». 4. Live GitHub state, read via `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/branches/release/protection` → `404 {"message":"Branch not protected"}`; `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/rulesets` → `[]`; `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/branches/release` → `protected: false`, head `e0399311…`. The branch exists and is live. 5. Contrast `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/branches/main/protection` → `enforce_admins.enabled: true`, `allow_force_pushes: false`, `allow_deletions: false`, `required_status_checks.contexts: ["ci / integration"]`. The asymmetry is the defect: the protection was written for `main` before production moved to `release`, and `release` was never given any.

**العلاج المقترح:** Put protection on `release` at least as strong as `main`'s: required status check `ci / integration` (plus `ci / browser acceptance`, see the next finding), require a pull request, `enforce_admins: true`, `allow_force_pushes: false`, `allow_deletions: false`. A repository ruleset covering both `main` and `release` is better than two hand-kept branch rules, because it cannot drift apart the way these two did. Then correct `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:136` — «Wait for CI» is a deploy-ordering setting inside Railway, not a merge gate, and it must not be listed as the protection on the branch.

### 11-15-03 · **عالية** · `.github/workflows/ci.yml:648`

**الادّعاء:** `ci / browser acceptance` is declared BLOCKING in the workflow file but is not in main's required-status-check list, so all fifteen browser suites — including the one proving a shopper's session cookie is scoped to one store — can fail red and the PR still merges

**الأثر المزعوم:** The comment above this job says, in its own words, that a gate that cannot fail is not a gate, and records eleven green runs earned before the flip on 2026-09-04. The flip never happened where it counts. GitHub blocks a merge only on the contexts named in branch protection, and that list holds exactly one entry. So the acceptance contracts that no HTTP-level suite can hold — that the customer cookie for store A is never sent to store B, that a staff member's permission matrix is what the screen actually enforces, that platform staff cannot walk into the owner's area, that a spent password-reset link shows a dead-link page rather than a working form — are all free to go red and be merged. That is precisely the state the comment claims was ended, and the person who pays is the shopper whose session leaks across two shops on one phone, or the store_staff member who quietly gains a manager's screen.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:636-647` — «BLOCKING as of 2026-09-04… fifteen suites drive real screens… every one of those contracts could fail RED and still be merged… a gate that cannot fail is not a gate.» 2. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:648-650` — `browser-acceptance:` with `name: ci / browser acceptance`, and unlike `staging-healthcheck` (:617 `continue-on-error: true`) it carries no `continue-on-error`, which is the whole basis of the "blocking" claim. 3. The suites it holds: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:731-732` `pnpm phase9b:cookie` ("Browser — phase 9B cookie scope" — the per-store cookie path from `phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:29`), :739-740 `pnpm phase14:browser` (staff permissions acceptance), :754-755 `pnpm ps1:browser` (19-ب platform-staff lifecycle), :761-762 `pnpm pr1:browser` (password recovery end to end). 4. Live GitHub state: `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/branches/main/protection` → `required_status_checks.checks: [{context: "ci / integration", app_id: 15368}]` — one entry, and `ci / browser acceptance` is not it. `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/rulesets` → `[]`, so nothing else supplies the gate. 5. The context name is exact, not a guess: `gh api repos/phsaifaldin/CODIQ/commits/650ddd13…/check-runs` on main's head lists check runs named literally `ci / browser acceptance`, `ci / integration`, and `ci / staging healthcheck (environment probe, non-blocking)`. 6. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:22` still describes the job as «غير حاجبة مؤقتًا» under debt B-15 — so the workflow file and the deployment document give opposite answers, and the branch-protection setting agrees with the stale one.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add `ci / browser acceptance` to main's required status checks (and to `release`'s, once that branch is protected at all). Then update `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:22`, which still records the job as temporarily non-blocking under B-15 — one of the two documents is wrong today whichever way the setting goes, and the workflow comment is not the place a merge gate is configured.

### 11-15-04 · **عالية** · `.github/workflows/ci.yml:635`

**الادّعاء:** The only automated probe of a deployed environment hits /api/health, which is a hardcoded 200 — and /ready computes the true verdict and then throws it away

**الأثر المزعوم:** Phase 11's promise is that a merge reaches staging and that this is verified. The verification is a curl against the one endpoint in the codebase that is architecturally incapable of failing, and docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:126 and :146 say so twice in its own words («`/api/health` لا يكفي — لا يلمس قاعدة البيانات ويُعيد `ok` مهما كان حالها»). Meanwhile `/ready` already knows the truth — database, mail, and six config groups — and answers 200 no matter what it finds, so no automation anywhere (CI, a Railway healthcheck, an uptime service) can act on it; only a human reading JSON. The concrete cost is live right now: docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:99 records `CODIQ_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` as unset in production, which means the merchant's WhatsApp order message — the channel in Iraq — silently loses its tracking line, and password recovery answers «غير متاحة» to every merchant, owner and staff member. A merchant locked out of his own shop, with no email address and no second channel, is the person who pays for this; every automated check between that state and him is green.

**الدليل المزعوم:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:620-641 is the only job that touches a deployed environment: line 624 `continue-on-error: true`, lines 630-635 `curl --fail … https://staging.codiqos.com/api/health`, lines 636-641 the same path on the Railway origin. That path resolves through server/index.ts:81 (`app.use(API_MOUNT_LEGACY, codiqApi)`, api-version.ts:46 = `/api`) to phase1-backend/src/http/app.ts:60, which is a literal `response.status(200).json({ status: "ok", env })` — no database, no config, no branch that can return anything else. The endpoint that does know is app.ts:61-99: line 62 checks the database, line 97 embeds `config: readinessSummary()`, and phase1-backend/src/config/environment-report.ts:176-181 computes a real `ok` boolean from the six groups — but app.ts:73 hardcodes `response.status(200)`, so `config.ok === false` changes nothing an automated client can see. `pnpm env:check` (package.json:117), the script written for exactly this, appears nowhere in ci.yml — only its unit test does (ci.yml:512, `pnpm env:check:test`), which asserts the report function against a fake env object and says nothing about any real deployment. The consequence path for the live gap: environment-report.ts:94-99 names `publicBaseUrl` required-in-deployment; messaging-compliance.service.ts:120-124 `publicUrl()` returns null when the variable is blank; whatsapp-manual.service.ts:136 builds the tracking link from it and :145 drops the whole `{{رابط_التتبع}}` line when it is null; password-reset.service.ts:281 and :287 return null for the same reason.

**العلاج المقترح:** Point the staging probe at `/api/ready` instead of `/api/health` and make it read the body, not just the status: fail the step when `db !== "ok"` or `config.ok !== true`. That is a CI-only change and cannot take production down. Separately, have `/ready` answer 503 when `readinessSummary().ok` is false in a deployed environment (keeping the same body), so an uptime service or a platform healthcheck can act on the verdict the code already computes.

### 11-15-05 · **عالية** · `client/src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx:34`

**الادّعاء:** The app-wide crash screen shows an English sentence and a raw JavaScript stack trace to the shopper — on an Arabic, RTL, cash-on-delivery storefront

**الأثر المزعوم:** This is the last screen between any deploy and a real person, and it is written for a developer. A shopper in Baghdad who is one tap from confirming a COD order meets «An unexpected error occurred.», a grey box full of minified chunk names and function frames, and a button that says «Reload Page» — no Arabic, no «العودة إلى المتجر», no shop phone number, nothing he can act on. He does not reload; he closes the tab, and the merchant never learns the order existed. The same screen catches a merchant mid-order-call and a cashier at the till. It also publishes internal build structure (chunk paths, function names) to anyone who can make any render throw. Every other user-meeting string in this repository is policed for Arabic tone by `pnpm tone:test` (ci.yml:155); this screen is not Arabic at all and no guard notices, because the tone guard hunts feminine Arabic forms (copy-tone.guard.mjs:29-49) and has no opinion about English.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/App.tsx:195 wraps the entire router in `<ErrorBoundary>` — including `/store/:storeSlug/checkout` (App.tsx:172), `/store/:storeSlug/products/:productId` (App.tsx:170) and every merchant dashboard route (App.tsx:150-156). client/src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx:20 `getDerivedStateFromError` flips `hasError`; ErrorBoundary.tsx:25-55 then renders: line 34 `<h2>An unexpected error occurred.</h2>`, line 38 `{this.state.error?.stack}` inside a `<pre>`, line 51 `Reload Page`. No Arabic, no route out, no correlation id — even though every 5xx already carries one (`x-request-id`, per docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:166) and the merchant is told elsewhere to quote it. Grep across the repo for «An unexpected error occurred» and «Reload Page» returns only ErrorBoundary.tsx:34 and :51 — no browser suite ever renders this screen, so nothing in CI has ever looked at it. `pnpm template:guard` (ci.yml:107) scans client/src but only for `/__manus__/i`-family markers (template-residue.guard.mjs:43), so generator-era English copy passes it untouched.

**العلاج المقترح:** Rewrite the boundary in Arabic, masculine, RTL: a sentence that names what happened («صار خلل غير متوقع في الصفحة»), the request id if one is on the page, one primary action back to a working place (the store front for a storefront path, the dashboard home otherwise) and the store's own contact line where the storefront knows it. Delete `this.state.error?.stack` from the render entirely — send it to the console/log path, never the document. Then add the crash screen to a browser suite so it is exercised at least once, and extend the tone guard to fail on a user-facing English sentence in a rendered JSX text node.

### 11-15-06 · **عالية** · `client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:123`

**الادّعاء:** The store switcher's "الحالي" indicator is computed from the URL, not from the session, so a merchant whose URL and session disagree is locked onto a dead dashboard and the switcher disables the one row that would rescue him

**الأثر المزعوم:** The phase promised "a switcher listing his stores with a clear active indicator". The indicator exists but indicates the wrong thing: it marks whichever slug is in the address bar, not the store the session is bound to. Any merchant who keeps two store dashboards open in two tabs — the exact merchant this phase was built for — poisons tab 1 the moment he switches in tab 2: the shared `codiq_session` cookie now carries store B, tab 1's sidebar still names store A, marks A «الحالي», and every request tab 1 makes answers 403 STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED, surfacing to him as «ليست لديك صلاحية الوصول إلى هذه الطلبات.» (OrderWorkspace.tsx:145) — the platform telling a merchant he lacks permission on his own orders. The same trap springs on a bookmark: he bookmarks متجر الكرادة at /merchant/stores/karrada/dashboard, logs in the next morning (auth.service.ts:185 binds the session to his OLDEST active store), opens the bookmark, and lands on a dashboard where the switcher labels karrada «الحالي» and DISABLES its button (line 125), while every panel errors. His only escape is to switch to the other store and then switch back — two clicks he has no reason to guess. Nothing in the client detects the mismatch: no client-side handler for STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED exists anywhere in the merchant dashboard (only till-queue.ts:141 and DeleteConfirmSheet.tsx:220 name the code, neither reconciles the store).

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:195 derives the slug purely from the address bar: `const storeSlug = useMemo(() => location.match(/^\/merchant\/stores\/([^/]+)\/dashboard/)?.[1], [location]);` 2. MerchantDashboard.tsx:159 passes it in as `activeStoreSlug`. 3. MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:123 `const isCurrent = store.slug === activeStoreSlug;` — the ONLY definition of "current" in the switcher. 4. MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:125 `disabled={isCurrent || Boolean(busySlug)}` and :127 renders `الحالي` for that row; MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:68 `if (store.slug === activeStoreSlug || busySlug) return;` makes the click a no-op even if the button were enabled. 5. The server never tells the client which store the session holds: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:39-41 `GET /merchant/stores` returns only `listMerchantStores(claims.subjectId)`, and phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:39-46 selects `{ id, name, slug, isActive }` — `claims.storeId` is read at merchant.ts:40 and discarded. 6. The real binding lives only in the session row: identity.ts:194 puts `storeId` in the claims, identity.ts:281 verifies it against `merchant_sessions.store_id`. 7. The guard almost every store route uses refuses the mismatch: phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:225 `if (store.id !== claims.storeId || store.merchantId !== claims.subjectId)` → :226 `throw new HttpError(403, "STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED", …)`. 8. The cookie is a single global carrier, so the "old session stays valid for its own context" half of the promise is unreachable from a browser: http/shared.ts:44-51 `setWebSession` writes one `codiq_session` at `path: "/"`, and merchant.ts:49 overwrites it on every switch. 9. client/src/components/StoresManager.tsx:95 makes the same mistake in words, badging the URL-matched row «متجر الجلسة» — "the session's store" — while comparing against `activeStoreSlug` (StoresManager.tsx:343 ← MerchantDashboard.tsx:224, same URL-derived value).

**العلاج المقترح:** Return the session's own store with the list: at phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:41 respond `{ stores, activeStoreId: claims.storeId }` (the claim is already in hand at line 40). In MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:123 compute `isCurrent` from that id rather than from `activeStoreSlug`, and when the URL slug names a store that is NOT the session's, render it as switchable (not «الحالي», not disabled) — ideally auto-switching or offering a one-click «تبديل إلى هذا المتجر» banner instead of letting the merchant hit 403 on every panel. Apply the same change to the «متجر الجلسة» badge at StoresManager.tsx:95.

### 11-15-07 · **عالية** · `client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:123`

**الادّعاء:** The dashboard reads its store from the URL while the session reads it from one cookie, so after a switch the Back button leaves the merchant on a screen that says he has lost access to his own shop — and the switcher marks the wrong store «الحالي» and disables the one button that would fix it

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant with two shops — the entire population Phase 13 exists for — switches from shop A to shop B, then presses Back (or opens shop A in a second tab, or follows a link he sent himself). The URL now says shop A; the cookie says shop B. Every panel on the page fails: the orders workspace tells him «ليست لديك صلاحية الوصول إلى هذه الطلبات» and the products page shows the raw English «This merchant session is not authorized for the requested store.» The switcher in the sidebar reads the same URL, so it renders shop A as «الحالي» with its button disabled — the platform tells him he is standing in shop A, refuses him shop A's orders, and greys out the only control that would put him back in it. Nothing on screen names the real cause or offers a way out. A merchant who is packing today's cash-on-delivery orders concludes his account was cut off, stops working, and calls whoever sold him the platform. He is not wrong to: nothing he can see distinguishes this from being disabled.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. Switching replaces the session: POST /merchant/stores/switch mints a new token and writes it over the single cookie — phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:48-49. 2. There is exactly one such cookie per browser, `codiq_session` at path "/" — phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:44-52 — so a browser has exactly one store context, not one per tab. 3. The dashboard shell derives the store from the address bar alone: `location.match(/^\/merchant\/stores\/([^/]+)\/dashboard/)` — client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:195 — and hands that string to the switcher as `activeStoreSlug` (client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:159). 4. The switcher's active indicator is that string and nothing else: `const isCurrent = store.slug === activeStoreSlug` (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:123) and `disabled={isCurrent || Boolean(busySlug)}` (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:125). 5. Nothing in the payload could correct it: `listMerchantStores` selects only id/name/slug/isActive (phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:40-45) and the route returns just `{ stores }` (phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:39-42) — the session's own `claims.storeId` is read at line 40 and thrown away. 6. The server then refuses the mismatch: `if (store.id !== claims.storeId || store.merchantId !== claims.subjectId) throw new HttpError(403, "STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED", ...)` — phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:225-227. 7. What the merchant reads: client/src/components/OrderWorkspace.tsx:145 maps that code to «ليست لديك صلاحية الوصول إلى هذه الطلبات.» (a permission accusation, not the truth), while client/src/components/ProductCatalogManager.tsx:49-55 renders `error?.message` verbatim — the English sentence. 8. No shell-level recovery: the only navigation to /merchant/login in the whole merchant dashboard is the explicit sign-out button (client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:135-143).

**العلاج المقترح:** Return the session's store from GET /merchant/stores — `response.json({ stores, sessionStoreSlug })`, resolved from the `claims.storeId` already in hand at phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:40 — and make MerchantStoreSwitcher compute `isCurrent` from that field instead of `activeStoreSlug` (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:123), so the store the session is NOT on is never disabled. When the URL slug differs from `sessionStoreSlug`, render one Arabic line above the page — «جلستك الآن في متجر «{name}». بدّل إلى هذا المتجر للمتابعة.» — with a button that POSTs the switch for the URL's slug.

### 11-15-08 · **عالية** · `client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:52`

**الادّعاء:** When the merchant's 8-hour session lapses, the store switcher — mounted on every dashboard page — shows the server's English «The session token is invalid or expired.» beside a retry button that can never succeed, and nothing sends him to the login page

**الأثر المزعوم:** This is the ordinary morning. The merchant left the dashboard open overnight; the cookie's 8-hour life ran out. He clicks into orders and every panel fails at once. The first and most prominent failure is the sidebar switcher, which fetches on every mount: it renders a red box containing an English sentence and a «إعادة المحاولة» button that will produce the same English sentence forever, because retrying an expired session is not a recoverable condition. The merchant is Arabic-speaking and cash-on-delivery — English error text is not information, it is noise — and no screen tells him to sign in again. The platform already knows how to say this: the till says «انتهت الجلسة — سجّل الدخول» (client/src/pages/TillPage.tsx:723-726), the staff dashboard redirects on 401 (client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:103), the owner dashboard redirects on 401 and 403 (client/src/pages/OwnerDashboard.tsx:177-178). The merchant — the person whose money is on the line — is the one identity that gets neither. He loses the morning or calls support for something one sentence would have solved.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The switcher is in the sidebar of every merchant page (client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:159, mounted from both layout branches at :218 and :220) and fetches on mount (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:58-65 → :49). 2. An absent cookie answers 401 MISSING_SESSION «A session is required.» — phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:105 — and a lapsed or revoked one answers 401 INVALID_SESSION «The session token is invalid or expired.» / «The session is no longer valid.» — phase1-backend/src/auth/identity.ts:244 and :290. 3. `apiJson` treats 401 exactly like any other failure — no status branch at client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:17-22. 4. `messageForError` maps four store codes and has no case for MISSING_SESSION or INVALID_SESSION, so it falls through to `error.error?.message` — the English string — at client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:9-15. 5. That string lands in `loadingError` (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:52) and is rendered as a red alert whose only action is «إعادة المحاولة» calling the same dead endpoint (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:121). 6. The merchant dashboard has no 401 handling anywhere: grep for `status === 401` across client/src hits only StaffDashboard.tsx:103, PlatformStaffDashboard.tsx:210 and OwnerDashboard.tsx:177-178; client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx reaches /merchant/login only from the sign-out button at :142.

**العلاج المقترح:** In client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx's `apiJson` (line 17-22), branch on `response.status === 401` and route to /merchant/login. If a redirect out of an open dashboard is judged too abrupt, at minimum add to `messageForError` (line 9): `if (code === "MISSING_SESSION" || code === "INVALID_SESSION") return "انتهت جلستك. سجّل الدخول من جديد."` and replace the retry button with a link to /merchant/login when that code is the cause — the same treatment the till already gives at client/src/pages/TillPage.tsx:725.

### 11-15-09 · **عالية** · `client/src/components/ProductCard.tsx:42`

**الادّعاء:** The catalogue grid and the home rails add a product's DEFAULT variant blind — so a shopper who never chose a size gets one picked for him, and a card that says «متوفر» can add a variant that is sold out

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every multi-variant product (a shirt in S/M/L, a perfume in two sizes, a phone case in three colours) has one add-to-cart affordance on the store's front page and on the catalogue grid, and it is the primary one — the product page with the picker is a second tap away. Two costs, both borne in cash. (1) The shopper taps «أضف إلى السلة» on the grid, never sees a picker, and the server silently books the merchant's default variant. The cart line then hides the variant name, because StorefrontCartPage.tsx:42 renders `item.variant.name` only when `!item.variant.isDefault` — so nothing on any screen tells him which size he bought. He is delivered the wrong size cash-on-delivery, refuses at the door, and the merchant eats the courier round trip on an order that was never wrong on the shopper's side. (2) When the default variant is the sold-out one (size M always sells out first) and any other variant still has stock, the card computes `isAvailable = true`, shows no «نفد المخزون» ribbon and leaves the button enabled — then adds the sold-out default. The refusal arrives only at «تأكيد الطلب», after he has typed a full COD address. Both break the phase's promise of out-of-stock states on the catalogue and of a cart that shows availability.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/components/ProductCard.tsx:42 — `<Button disabled={adding || !product.isAvailable} onClick={() => onAdd(product.id)}>`: the callback receives the PRODUCT id only; the card's props (client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:62-74, `StorefrontProduct`) carry no variant list and no variant count, so the card cannot know the product has more than one. → client/src/pages/StorefrontCatalogPage.tsx:77 `const data = await storefrontApi.addCartItem(storeSlug, productId, 1);` and client/src/pages/StorefrontHomePage.tsx:118, the identical call for every home rail — neither passes a variantId. → client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:259 therefore posts `{ productId, quantity }` with no `variantId`. → phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:460-465, the else branch at :463 resolves `{ productId: requestedProductId, storeId, isDefault: true, isActive: true, product: { isActive: true } }` — the default variant, chosen by the merchant, with NO stock predicate anywhere in `addCartItem` (:451-480). Contrast client/src/pages/StorefrontProductPage.tsx:93, which does pass `selectedVariant?.id`. The availability mismatch: phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:380 sets the card's flag to `product.variants.some((variant) => isAvailable(variant.stockQuantity, reservedByVariant.get(variant.id)))` — ANY active variant — while the line just added is the default one specifically. The bill arrives at phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:933-939, where checkout recomputes `available = stockQuantity - reserved` per cart line and throws 422 INSUFFICIENT_AVAILABLE_STOCK. No test covers this path: phase1-backend/tests/phase20a.browser-acceptance.mjs:160 exercises the product-page picker (`variant-option-${redSku}`) and never the grid card.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add `variantCount` (or the variant ids) to the listing projection at phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:369-381, and in ProductCard.tsx:42 turn the button into a link to the product page when `variantCount > 1` — the shopper picks the variant where the picker already exists. Independently, make the card's availability flag mean the thing it triggers: when a single default variant is what will be added, `isAvailable` must be that variant's availability, not `some()` across all of them.

### 11-15-10 · **عالية** · `client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:219`

**الادّعاء:** The staff invite link the merchant is told to send omits the store id the staff login screen requires, so a new employee cannot sign in on day one

**الأثر المزعوم:** After creating an employee the merchant reads a green notice telling him to send three things — the link, the email, the temporary password — and a «نسخ» button that copies a URL with no store context in it. The staff member opens that link and meets a REQUIRED field labelled «معرّف المتجر» with the placeholder `store-...`: a slug neither of them has been shown anywhere on this screen, and which the merchant's own dashboard URL (/merchant/dashboard, App.tsx:156) does not display either. There is nothing on the login page telling him what to type or where to find it. This is not an edge case — it is EVERY staff member's first sign-in, on a phone, over WhatsApp, in the market this product is for. Whatever he guesses returns an English 404 (see the separate finding), so the failure teaches him nothing. The same dead end is reached again after a password reset: password-reset.service.ts:521 sends a staff member who just set a new password to that same slug-less /staff/login.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:97-112 creates the membership → client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:161-172 shows the fixed notice «أُنشئ الموظف … أرسل له رابط الدخول أدناه مع بريده وكلمة المرور المؤقتة» (three items, no store id) → client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:217-221 renders and copies `${window.location.origin}/staff/login` with no slug and no query string → client/src/App.tsx:142 registers `<Route path={"/staff/login"}>` with no path parameter → client/src/pages/DashboardLogin.tsx:112 reads the slug ONLY from the submitted form (`String(form.get("storeSlug") ?? "")`) — the file contains no read of location or search params → client/src/pages/DashboardLogin.tsx:148-152 renders `<Input name="storeSlug" required placeholder="store-..." />` under the label «معرّف المتجر». The comment at DashboardLogin.tsx:14-16 states the premise this depends on — «a staff member reaches his store through a link his merchant sent him, so the store IS his context» — which the link at StoreTeamManager.tsx:219 does not satisfy. Recovery repeats the dead end at phase1-backend/src/services/password-reset.service.ts:521 (`if (kind === "staff") return "/staff/login";`).

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the copied link carry the context: `${origin}/staff/login?store=${storeSlug}` (the component already holds `storeSlug`), and have DashboardLogin prefill and hide the «معرّف المتجر» field when that parameter is present, falling back to the visible field only for a bare visit. Add the slug to the success notice's list of what to send, and apply the same parameter to the staff branch of password-reset.service.ts:521.

### 11-15-11 · **عالية** · `client/src/config/navigation.ts:350`

**الادّعاء:** The Store Manager role holds all 15 permission codes but the staff shell renders screens for only 9 of them, so `settings.manage` opens one notifications page while the server admits it to coupons, storefront, payments, delivery pricing and till pairing — and the returns queue that every store_staff is authorized for has no door at all

**الأثر المزعوم:** This is the clause the phase existed for — «packaged roles ... from which the owner's, merchant's and staff sidebars are built». A merchant who hires a store manager to run the shop while he is at the wholesale market finds the manager cannot add a category, edit the shop's face, price delivery to Basra, switch a payment method on or off, pair a till, or clear the day's returns — even though the server answers 200 to every one of those requests from that same membership. The returns gap is the sharpest: a refused delivery at the door is the ordinary Iraqi COD return, `returns.ts` gates it on `orders.view`/`orders.update` which even the junior `store_staff` role holds by default, and there is no entry and no branch anywhere in the staff shell that reaches it. The merchant's two options are to do all of it himself at night, or to hand the manager his own merchant password — which also hands over billing, the subscription, store creation and every other store he owns. Phase 14 was built to remove exactly that choice, and in substance it did not.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:44 — `store_manager: PERMISSION_CODES` (all 15 codes at :13-35). phase1-backend/src/http/routes/team.ts:15 returns that resolved set to the shell. client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:119 `const entries = visibleStaffEntries(session.permissions)` filters client/src/config/navigation.ts:350-364, which carries exactly 10 entries covering 9 codes (orders.view :352, pos.sell :353, catalog.products.view :354, inventory.update :355, customers.view :356, catalog.trash.view :360, catalog.audit.view :361, team.manage :362, settings.manage :363). The branch table at client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:142-180 then maps `settings.manage` to ONE component — :160-161 `content = <NotificationCenter storeSlug={storeSlug} />` — and everything unmatched falls to the «غير متاحة» card at :173-179. Meanwhile the server admits `settings.manage` to: coupons phase1-backend/src/http/routes/marketing.ts:47,56,62,70,79; the storefront face phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront-config.ts:30-55; payment switches phase1-backend/src/http/routes/payment-methods.ts:15,21; per-governorate delivery pricing phase1-backend/src/http/routes/orders.ts:97,102; loyalty phase1-backend/src/http/routes/reviews-wishlist.ts:103,108; vacation mode phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:105,110; till pairing/revoke phase1-backend/src/http/routes/pos.ts:46,58. `catalog.categories.manage` (phase1-backend/src/http/routes/catalog.ts:52,53,57) has no entry and no branch — client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx mounts ProductCatalogManager only with mode "list" (:145) and "inventory" (:147), never "categories" (client/src/components/ProductCatalogManager.tsx:198). The returns queue phase1-backend/src/http/routes/returns.ts:44 (`orders.view`) and :50 (`orders.update`) is a separate mount in the merchant shell (client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:209-213 routes /orders/returns to ReturnsQueue) and has no counterpart in the staff shell; the staff order regex at client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:121-124 would read the segment "returns" as an order id. Nothing catches this: phase1-backend/tests/platform-nav.guard.mjs:76 applies the «every permission has a screen» rule to the PLATFORM matrix only, and the store-staff port at :105-117 checks solely the opposite direction (entries → branches).

**العلاج المقترح:** Extend rule 1 of phase1-backend/tests/platform-nav.guard.mjs to PERMISSION_CODES in staff-permissions.ts with an ACTS_NOT_SCREENS list for the act-codes (products.create/update/delete, categories.manage, orders.update, trash.restore), then add the missing staff entries and branches — returns, categories, and a settings hub that reaches the storefront/payments/shipping/coupons/loyalty/POS screens the merchant shell already renders — or move those codes into the exemption list with the reason they are unreachable by delegation.

### 11-15-12 · **عالية** · `client/src/main.tsx:24`

**الادّعاء:** The once-per-session guard on the stale-chunk auto-reload is cleared on every page load, so a failed chunk reloads the page forever instead of once

**الأثر المزعوم:** The comment two lines above says the flag exists «so a chunk that is genuinely gone for good cannot loop the page». It cannot do that: the flag is deleted at the top of every load, including the load the reload just caused. On the Iraqi mobile networks this handler was written for — the comment names them at main.tsx:9-12 — a lazy chunk fails for ordinary reasons, the page reloads, the chunk fails again, and the shopper sits in an endless reload cycle: the shell plus the entry chunk re-downloaded on metered data every few seconds, no message, no way to stop it but closing the tab. The same loop hits a merchant whose neighbourhood link is saturated while he is on the phone with a customer. The failure is silent where it must be loud: nothing is ever shown, so the person cannot tell the page from the network.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/main.tsx:15 registers the `vite:preloadError` listener. Inside it: line 18 reads `sessionStorage.getItem("codiq:preload-reload")` into `reloaded`; line 19 returns early only if it is `"1"`; line 20 writes `"1"`; line 22 calls `window.location.reload()`. Line 24, at module scope and unconditional, runs `sessionStorage.removeItem("codiq:preload-reload")` on every execution of main.tsx — which is every page load, the reloaded one included. Because the listener can only fire after module evaluation (a dynamic import failure happens later, on navigation), line 24 has always already cleared the flag by the time line 18 reads it, so `reloaded` is false on every pass and line 22 always fires. Grep for `codiq:preload-reload` across the repo returns exactly these two sites (main.tsx:16 and main.tsx:24) — nothing else writes or preserves it, and no test covers the second failure.

**العلاج المقترح:** Stop clearing the flag at load time. Either clear it only once the app has actually settled (`window.addEventListener("load", () => setTimeout(clear, 10_000))`), or key it by the failing asset URL so a second failure of the same URL falls through to a visible Arabic message instead of a reload. On the fall-through, show something the person can act on rather than a silent retry.

### 11-15-13 · **عالية** · `client/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailPage.tsx:172`

**الادّعاء:** A shopper who pays by wallet transfer is told to send the money and then loses the wallet number: it is rendered only on the checkout page, which becomes «سلتك فارغة» the moment the order exists

**الأثر المزعوم:** ق-209 lets a merchant switch on a manual wallet transfer (ZainCash/FastPay is how a good share of Iraqi shops take money before delivery). The shopper picks it, reads the wallet number on the checkout page, presses «تأكيد الطلب» — and is navigated straight to his order page, which shows him a status of «جديد», no payment method, no payment state and no wallet number. Going back to /checkout to re-read the number is impossible: the checkout consumed the cart, so that route now renders the empty-cart dead end. He has been asked for real money and the destination for it has disappeared from the product one tap after he agreed to send it. Best case he phones the shop; likely case he transfers to a number he half-remembers, or does not transfer at all and the merchant is left holding an order stuck in `awaiting_transfer` that he has to chase by phone. The information is already on the wire in both directions — `walletNumber` is in the public config the page has loaded, and `paymentStatus` is already returned by the customer order endpoint — nobody drew it.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. `walletNumber`, `holderName` and `instructions` appear in exactly one place in the whole client: client/src/pages/StorefrontCheckoutPage.tsx:310-316 (grep for `walletNumber` across client/src returns only that page, the merchant panel, and the type at client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:99). 2. On success, StorefrontCheckoutPage.tsx:182 sets a notice naming only the method — `تم إنشاء الطلب. حوّل المبلغ إلى ${method.displayName} وسيؤكد المتجر استلامه.` — and :183 navigates to `/store/{slug}/account/orders/{id}`. 3. That notice is wiped after 7 seconds: client/src/components/StorefrontShell.tsx:84-86. 4. The order page never renders payment: client/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailPage.tsx:172 draws items, money lines and the timeline with no payment section, and its `statusLabel` map at CustomerOrderDetailPage.tsx:11 has no entry for a transfer state — the shopper sees «جديد». 5. The client type drops the fields: `CustomerOrder` in client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:137 declares no `paymentMethodCode` and no `paymentStatus` — even though the server stores `paymentStatus: "awaiting_transfer"` at phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:993 and `customerOrder` (phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:1050) returns the whole row with a bare `include`, so both fields are already in the response body. 6. Returning to the checkout page cannot recover the number: StorefrontCheckoutPage.tsx:159 returns `EmptyState title="سلتك فارغة"` because the cart was converted by the checkout. 7. The merchant's side does show it — client/src/components/OrderWorkspace.tsx:664 renders «بانتظار التحويل» — so the two sides of the same order disagree about whether a payment is outstanding.

**العلاج المقترح:** On CustomerOrderDetailPage, when `order.paymentStatus === "awaiting_transfer"`, render a payment card above the items: the «بانتظار التحويل» badge, and the wallet number, holder name and instructions read from the `config.paymentMethods` entry matching `order.paymentMethodCode` (the provider already holds the public config). Add `paymentMethodCode` and `paymentStatus` to the `CustomerOrder` type in client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:137 — the server already sends both — and add a label for the transfer state to the `statusLabel` map.

### 11-15-14 · **عالية** · `client/src/pages/PasswordResetRequest.tsx:114`

**الادّعاء:** A shopper who registered by phone with no email has no password-recovery path at all, and the screen sends him to a merchant who has no button to help him

**الأثر المزعوم:** The storefront signs a shopper in by PHONE and marks email «(اختياري)» — most Iraqi shoppers will leave it blank. When such a shopper forgets his password, the only recovery door on the platform asks for an email address, `required`, with no phone alternative. The screen's own fallback tells him «تواصل مع المتجر لاستعادة حسابك» — but the merchant has no capability to reset a customer password anywhere in the product: the only merchant-side write to a customer is the block/unblock switch. He also cannot fall back to buying as a guest (his phone belongs to an account now) and cannot re-register (unique index refuses it). So a returning customer of the shop simply cannot buy there again — the merchant permanently loses a repeat COD customer, and neither of them is told why. The one message he does meet on the way, when he tries to register again with his own number, is in ENGLISH on an Arabic RTL sign-up form: «This phone is already registered in this store.» — no «سجّل الدخول», no recovery link, no Arabic.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. Registration makes email optional: client/src/pages/CustomerAuthPage.tsx:78 labels it «البريد الإلكتروني (اختياري)»; phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:255 stores `normalizeOptionalEmail(input.email)` (null when blank). 2. Sign-in is by phone: auth.service.ts:289 `findFirst({ storeId, phoneNormalized })`; a wrong password is 401 INVALID_CUSTOMER_CREDENTIALS at auth.service.ts:297. 3. «هل نسيت كلمة السر؟» at CustomerAuthPage.tsx:85 links to /store/{slug}/account/forgot-password. 4. That page (client/src/pages/PasswordResetRequest.tsx:114) renders exactly one field: `<Input name="email" type="email" required>`. There is no phone field. Its own hint at :108 says «إن لم تكن قد سجّلت بريداً إلكترونياً في هذا المتجر، تواصل مع المتجر لاستعادة حسابك.» 5. The server side is email-only too: phase1-backend/src/services/password-reset.service.ts:251 resolves a customer with `findFirst({ storeId, email, isActive: true })`. 6. The merchant cannot act on that advice. Grepping every write to `customer.passwordHash` in phase1-backend/src yields exactly three: auth.service.ts:255 (self-registration), auth.service.ts:281 (`setGuestPassword`, refused for a row that already has one — auth.service.ts:278), and password-reset.service.ts:595 (the email link). The merchant's only customer mutation is `setCustomerActive` in phase1-backend/src/services/customer-directory.service.ts:124. 7. He cannot buy as a guest either: guest-checkout.service.ts:52 refuses an account phone with 409 CUSTOMER_ACCOUNT_EXISTS. 8. He cannot re-register: auth.service.ts:260 only claims rows with `isGuest: true`, so his non-guest row is missed and auth.service.ts:268 hits `customers_store_phone_present_uq` (prisma/migrations/.../migration.sql:13) → P2002 → phase1-backend/src/http/errors.ts:201-206 returns `DUPLICATE_CUSTOMER_PHONE` with the English message «This phone is already registered in this store.» (the sibling merchant branch at errors.ts:205 is Arabic). 9. The client shows it verbatim: `DUPLICATE_CUSTOMER_PHONE` is absent from both the `ApiErrorCode` union (client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:1-51) and the Arabic map (storefront-api.ts:335-372), so `errorMessage` falls through to `error.message` at storefront-api.ts:373 and CustomerAuthPage.tsx:63 renders the English sentence.

**العلاج المقترح:** Two changes. (a) Give the storefront reset door a phone branch — an SMS code, or, until SMS exists, a merchant-side «إعادة تعيين كلمة مرور الزبون» action in the customer directory that issues a one-time code the merchant reads to the shopper on the phone he is already calling for COD; without one of these, remove the «تواصل مع المتجر» advice because it is a dead end. (b) Add `DUPLICATE_CUSTOMER_PHONE` to `ApiErrorCode` and to the `errorMessage` map in client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts with Arabic that names the way out — «هذا الرقم مسجّل في هذا المتجر — سجّل الدخول، أو استعد كلمة المرور» plus the two links — and translate errors.ts:206.

### 11-15-15 · **عالية** · `client/src/pages/TillPage.tsx:725`

**الادّعاء:** The till's session banner sends a staff cashier holding unsynced cash sales to /merchant/login, a door his credentials can never open

**الأثر المزعوم:** The till is the one screen a store_staff cashier uses all day (navigation.ts:353 gates «الكاشير» on pos.sell; pos.ts:84 serves ticket confirmation to any pos.sell holder). When his session dies mid-shift the queue pauses holding real, already-taken cash — the banner itself says «لمزامنة N مبيعات محفوظة». It then hands him a link to the MERCHANT door. He types his staff email and password there and gets «بيانات الدخول غير صحيحة. تحقق من البريد أو رقم الهاتف وكلمة المرور» (auth.service.ts:156) — a message that blames him for a typo when he is simply at the wrong building. He will retry it, ask the merchant for the merchant's own password, or close the tab. The unsynced sales are cash already in the drawer with no order behind them; the shop's stock and its takings are now out by however many tickets sat in that queue. Worse, SESSION_CODES (till-queue.ts:141) treats STAFF_ACCESS_REVOKED and STAFF_PERMISSION_DENIED as «انتهت الجلسة» too — so a cashier whose manager just disabled his membership is told to sign in again, and loginStaff refuses a disabled membership outright (auth.service.ts:239-243). For him no login anywhere drains the queue, and the screen never says so.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/config/navigation.ts:353 puts the till entry in the STAFF sidebar behind pos.sell → phase1-backend/src/http/routes/pos.ts:84 confirms `/merchant/stores/:storeSlug/pos/tickets` authorizes through requireStoreActor(request, "pos.sell"), which resolves a staff session at phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:249-258 → a flush refusal is classified at client/src/lib/till-queue.ts:141-144, whose SESSION_CODES list names STAFF_ACCESS_REVOKED and STAFF_PERMISSION_DENIED explicitly, i.e. the queue is written for a staff actor → client/src/lib/till-queue.ts:447-448 sets `this.halt = "session"` → client/src/pages/TillPage.tsx:723-726 renders the banner «انتهت الجلسة — سجّل الدخول لمزامنة …» whose Link href is the hardcoded "/merchant/login" → client/src/App.tsx:141 maps that to DashboardLogin kind="merchant" → client/src/pages/DashboardLogin.tsx:34 posts to /api/auth/merchant/login → phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:151-156 looks only in `prisma.merchant` and answers INVALID_MERCHANT_CREDENTIALS. The exit button eight lines above (TillPage.tsx:704-711) carries a comment saying the till «serves merchants AND staff … their dashboards are different routes» and uses history.back() for exactly this reason — the login link was left behind. For the revoked-membership case: phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:173-175 throws STAFF_ACCESS_REVOKED, and phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:241 requires `isActive: true` on the membership, so re-login returns 404 STAFF_STORE_CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND.

**العلاج المقترح:** Route the banner by who is actually signed in rather than by a constant: TillPage already knows the store slug, so link to `/staff/login` when the session that just failed was a staff one (the queue's `lastError.code` distinguishes MISSING_SESSION from STAFF_*), and to /merchant/login otherwise. For STAFF_ACCESS_REVOKED / STAFF_PERMISSION_DENIED specifically, stop calling it «انتهت الجلسة»: say the membership was stopped, that the saved sales are still on this device, and that the manager must re-enable the membership (or sign in himself on this device) to sync them — a sentence that names an action instead of an impossible one.

### 11-15-16 · **عالية** · `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:145`

**الادّعاء:** docs/DEPLOYMENT.md gives two contradictory answers to whether a merge into `release` migrates the production database, and no committed file settles it

**الأثر المزعوم:** This is the document phase 11 promised as the step-by-step commit-to-production description, and it is the page the owner will follow on launch day. Step 3 of «الإجراء عند الإطلاق» tells him the merge runs the migration; a later section states the pre-deploy command exists on staging only and that production was left untouched. If the second is the true one, the release merge boots a new build against the old schema, and every route touching a column added since the last manual migration answers 500 — for every merchant and every shopper — until someone notices and runs it by hand. Nothing in the repository can tell him which is true, and nothing in the repository can restore the setting if the dashboard field is ever cleared.

**الدليل المزعوم:** docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:145 (release procedure, step 3): «ادمجه. ينطلق البناء، ثم `pnpm phase1:db:migrate`، ثم التبديل.» — asserts build → migrate → switch happens on the merge. docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:204-208: «خدمة Railway في بيئة **staging** مضبوطة الآن بأمر ما قبل النشر `pnpm phase1:db:migrate` (المُهيأ في لوحة Railway على مستوى بيئة staging فقط) … بيئة production غير ممسوسة». Both sentences are live in the same file today. The repository cannot break the tie: a root listing shows no railway.json, nixpacks.toml, Procfile or Dockerfile (docker/ holds only backup.Dockerfile); package.json:10 `"start": "NODE_ENV=production node dist/index.js"` does not migrate; a grep of server/ (server/index.ts is the whole directory) for `migrate` returns no matches, so nothing migrates at boot. The single migrator is phase1-backend/scripts/migrate-deploy.ts, reachable only through package.json:15 `phase1:db:migrate`, and its only wiring is a dashboard field. ci.yml:5-11 runs CI on push to `release` (the «Wait for CI» gate) but has no deploy or migrate job of its own.

**العلاج المقترح:** Put the release path in the repository instead of a dashboard: commit a railway.json (or equivalent) carrying the pre-deploy `pnpm phase1:db:migrate` for both environments, or change package.json:10 to `pnpm phase1:db:migrate && node dist/index.js`. Then rewrite DEPLOYMENT.md:204-208 to quote that committed file, so §145 and §204 cannot disagree again.

### 11-15-17 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:267`

**الادّعاء:** registerCustomer claims an existing guest customer row by phone number with no proof of the phone, so anyone can set a password on a shopper the merchant already has on his books

**الأثر المزعوم:** A second, independent takeover door that survives any fix to the guest-checkout one. Every customer the merchant hand-adds from his directory is stored as a guest row (manual-provisioning.service.ts:137), as is every guest shopper (guest-checkout.service.ts:59). A stranger POSTs /store/{slug}/account/register with that person's number and a password of his choosing; the platform silently merges his registration into the victim's existing row — orders, addresses, loyalty points and prescriptions included — and hands him a session. The comment at auth.service.ts:257-259 describes this as a feature («a shopper who bought here as a GUEST and now registers with the same phone gets HIS row»), but nothing anywhere establishes that the registrant is that shopper. For a merchant this is worse than a leak: his own customer list, entered at the counter, is a list of takeable accounts. The victim gets no email, no SMS, no notice of any kind, and his own sessions are not revoked, so nothing on his screen changes.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:114 registers POST /store/:storeSlug/account/register, unauthenticated; :117 allows 8 attempts per IP per 10 minutes; :118 parses fullName/phone/password/email. 2) auth/auth.service.ts:255 builds `data` including `passwordHash: await createPasswordHash(input.password)` — the CALLER's password. 3) :260 `const guest = await prisma.customer.findFirst({ where: { storeId: store.id, phoneNormalized, isGuest: true }, select: { id: true } })` — the phone is the sole matching key and is never verified. 4) :267 `prisma.customer.update({ where: { id: guest.id }, data: { ...data, email: data.email ?? undefined, isGuest: false } })` writes the attacker's password hash and name onto the victim's row. 5) routes/storefront.ts:120-124 then mints a customer session, merges the caller's guest cart in and returns 201. 6) The rows reachable this way: services/manual-provisioning.service.ts:127-141 (`isGuest: true` at :137), called from http/routes/merchant.ts:72-73 whenever a merchant adds a customer to his directory; and services/guest-checkout.service.ts:59 for every guest purchase. 7) What the claimant then holds: storefront.service.ts:1031/1049 (order history and full order detail), :574 (addresses), reviews-wishlist.ts:94 (loyalty statement), routes/storefront.ts:232 (prescription image). 8) That the platform considers this fact sensitive is stated at auth.service.ts:290-294, where the login door pays a full bcrypt-12 compare against ABSENT_IDENTITY_HASH so a stranger cannot learn the same relationship by timing.

**العلاج المقترح:** Do not merge a phone-keyed row that carries history into a new registration without proof of possession of the number. Until an SMS code exists, auth.service.ts:260-267 should only claim a guest row that has no orders and no addresses; otherwise create nothing and answer the same generic «سجّل الدخول» the guest door gives, leaving the real owner to reach his row through a channel that proves he holds the phone.

### 11-15-18 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:260`

**الادّعاء:** Anyone who knows a shopper's mobile number can take over his guest row — his past orders, his home address and his phone — by typing that number into the store's public sign-up or guest-checkout form

**الأثر المزعوم:** In Iraq the phone number IS the identity, and it is the least secret thing a person has: it is on the shop's WhatsApp thread, on the courier's manifest, on a receipt taped to a box. A guest row is created for every shopper who buys without a password (the default: `guest_checkout_enabled` is on by default per ق-201), and that row accumulates his orders and the street address the courier used. Anyone who types that number into the store's ordinary «أنشئ حسابًا» form claims the row outright — sets his own password on it, and reads the victim's order history, recipient phones and full delivery addresses. The victim is simultaneously locked out forever: his row is no longer `isGuest`, so guest checkout now refuses his own phone (`CUSTOMER_ACCOUNT_EXISTS`) and he has no password. For a woman ordering to her home in Baghdad, that is her address handed to a stranger who only had to know her number. The register door costs 8 attempts per IP per 10 minutes (storefront.ts:117) — ample for a targeted takeover, and the checkout door's IP ceiling is deliberately 40 because a neighbourhood shares one IP (storefront.ts:180-182). Nothing anywhere verifies a customer phone: `Customer.phoneVerifiedAt` exists in prisma/schema.prisma and no code path in phase1-backend/src ever writes it.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. Victim buys as a guest: phase1-backend/src/services/guest-checkout.service.ts:59 creates `customer` with `isGuest: true, passwordHash: null` keyed on `phoneNormalized`; his checkout address is persisted onto that same customer id at phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:624, and the order at :993. 2. Attacker opens /store/{slug}/account/register (client/src/pages/CustomerAuthPage.tsx:51) and submits the victim's phone with his own name and password. 3. phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:118-119 → `registerCustomer`. 4. phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:260 looks up `prisma.customer.findFirst({ where: { storeId, phoneNormalized, isGuest: true } })` — matched by phone alone, no proof of possession — and line 267 UPDATES that row with the attacker's `passwordHash` and `isGuest: false`. 5. phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:120-121 mints and sets a customer session on the victim's row. 6. GET /store/{slug}/account/orders (storefront.ts:221 → storefront.service.ts:1031) returns the victim's orders; GET .../orders/:orderId (storefront.ts:222 → storefront.service.ts:1049-1052, a bare `include` with no `select`) returns `recipientPhone`, `shippingAddressLine1`, `shippingAddressLine2`, `customerName`, `customerNote`; GET .../account/addresses (storefront.ts:162 → storefront.service.ts:575) returns his saved addresses. 7. The victim is now locked out: phase1-backend/src/services/guest-checkout.service.ts:52 refuses his phone with 409 CUSTOMER_ACCOUNT_EXISTS «هذا الرقم مسجّل لدينا — سجّل الدخول لإتمام الطلب.», and he has no password to sign in with. 8. The guest-checkout door does the same without even needing the register form: storefront.ts:207 `guestCustomerFor` → guest-checkout.service.ts:51 finds any guest row by phone and :57 reuses it (overwriting `fullName` with the attacker's), then storefront.ts:208-209 sets the session BEFORE storefront.ts:210 runs the checkout — so even a 422 CART_EMPTY response still carries the victim's Set-Cookie. 9. No verification exists: `phoneVerifiedAt` in prisma/schema.prisma (Customer model, line ~306) is never written anywhere in phase1-backend/src.

**العلاج المقترح:** Stop treating an unverified phone as proof of possession of an existing row. Minimum: in `registerCustomer` (auth.service.ts:260) and `guestCustomerFor` (guest-checkout.service.ts:51), only reuse a guest row when the caller already holds a customer session on that exact row (the cookie issued when the guest checked out); otherwise create a fresh row or refuse. In `guestCheckout` (storefront.ts:208-209) issue the session AFTER `guestCheckout()` succeeds, not before, so a failed attempt hands out nothing. Longer term, an SMS one-time code on the phone is the only real fix — the `phoneVerifiedAt` column already exists for it.

### 11-15-19 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:49`

**الادّعاء:** Switching stores rebinds the one browser-wide `codiq_session` cookie, so the open till at the store you left starts answering 403 and its queued cash sales stop reaching the server

**الأثر المزعوم:** The merchant of a two-shop business rings COD sales at shop A's counter on a laptop that keeps the till page open (App.tsx:143-145 says so explicitly: «the till is a FULL-SCREEN counter surface, deliberately outside the dashboard shell — a cashier with a queue does not navigate»). Some of those tickets are sitting in IndexedDB because the 4G dropped for a minute. He then opens the dashboard in another tab of the same browser and switches to shop B — the ordinary act phase 13 exists for. From that instant every flush of shop A's queue is refused 403, the queue halts, and cash that has already crossed the counter never reaches store_orders, never decrements server stock, never enters the settlement ledger. Because server stock is untouched, shop A's storefront keeps selling units the till already sold — the one-stock-pool oversell rule 4 names, ending in a refused delivery at a door. The banner the cashier is shown says «انتهت الجلسة» and links to /merchant/login, which binds to the OLDEST active store (auth.service.ts:185 `const target = stores[0]!`), so following the instruction restores the wrong store for any till that is not on shop A and leaves the queue exactly as stuck. Nothing on the till re-reads the session, so nobody connects the cause to the other tab. Recovery is only by guessing to switch back.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. client/src/lib/till-queue.ts:484-490 — each queued ticket is POSTed with `credentials: "same-origin"`, i.e. authenticated by the single dashboard cookie, no device token. 2. phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:44-51 — `setWebSession` writes ONE cookie name, `codiq_session`, at `path: "/"`; a browser can therefore hold exactly one merchant session at a time. 3. phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:47-49 — the switch route resolves the target store, mints `createTypedSession("merchant", claims.subjectId, store.id)` and calls `setWebSession(response, accessToken)`, overwriting that one cookie (merchant.ts:61-62 does the same on store creation). Phase 13's promise that «the old session stays valid for its own context» holds for the token row but not for the browser, which no longer has it. 4. phase1-backend/src/http/routes/pos.ts:82-84 — `POST /merchant/stores/:storeSlug/pos/tickets` guards with `requireStoreActor(request, "pos.sell")`. 5. phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:224-227 — that guard resolves the store from the URL slug and throws `403 STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED` when `store.id !== claims.storeId`. 6. client/src/lib/till-queue.ts:141 lists STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED in `SESSION_CODES`; :144 classifies it "session"; :447-449 sets `this.halt = "session"` and breaks the flush loop; :416 makes every later `flush()` return immediately. New sales still enqueue durably (:243-301) but `triggerFlush` (:411) clears the halt, re-attempts, and re-raises it on the next 403. 7. client/src/pages/TillPage.tsx:723-726 — the halt renders «انتهت الجلسة» with a link to `/merchant/login`. 8. phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:171-185 — that login binds the new session to the oldest active store, not the till's store.

**العلاج المقترح:** Scope the merchant session cookie per store exactly as the storefront already scopes the customer one: `shared.ts:27-29` and `shared.ts:71-72` already define `customerCookieName(storeSlug)` and `storefrontCookiePath(storeSlug)` and use them in `setCustomerSession`. Give `setWebSession` the same shape for merchant sessions — `codiq_session_<slug>` at `path=/api/merchant/stores/<slug>` — and have `bearerToken` (shared.ts:15-21) prefer the slug-scoped cookie when the route carries `:storeSlug`, falling back to the unscoped one for the store-less merchant routes. Then a switch adds a session instead of replacing one, and phase 13's stated guarantee is true in a browser. If that is too large for now, the minimum stopgap is to stop misdiagnosing it: give STORE_BOUNDARY_DENIED its own verdict in `classifyHttp` and a banner that says the session moved to another store, with a button that switches back to `this.slug`, instead of «انتهت الجلسة» and a login link that binds to the oldest store.

### 11-15-20 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:208`

**الادّعاء:** POST /store/:slug/checkout/guest mints a customer session for any existing guest row matched on phone alone, so a stranger who knows a shopper's number reads his orders, addresses and prescriptions and can seize the row

**الأثر المزعوم:** Anyone who knows an Iraqi mobile number that has ever bought (or been entered) at a shop gets a logged-in session on that shopper's record. No password, no OTP, no cart, no order — one HTTP request. With the cookie he reads the shopper's whole order history (goods, totals, order numbers), his saved home addresses and delivery phone (storefront.service.ts:574), his loyalty balance, and — on a pharmacy store — his uploaded PRESCRIPTION IMAGE (routes/storefront.ts:232). He can then POST /account/password and convert the row into an account he owns, after which the real shopper is permanently locked out of his own number at that shop: guest-checkout.service.ts:52 now answers him «هذا الرقم مسجّل لدينا — سجّل الدخول لإتمام الطلب» for a password he never set. The same request also rewrites the victim's fullName and email on the row, so the password-reset channel becomes the attacker's mailbox. In a COD market the phone IS the identity — merchants keep these numbers in a WhatsApp thread and a POS till, and any leaked or guessed list turns straight into account takeovers. The victim is never notified. The ceiling is 40 attempts per IP per 10 minutes (shared.ts:118-132), i.e. thousands of numbers a day from one address.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:200 registers POST /store/:storeSlug/checkout/guest with NO session guard — only requireTrustedWriteOrigin (:201) and resolveStorefrontStore (:202). 2) :205 consumes the rate limit keyed on the caller's own cookie / IP. 3) :206 parses the body; guestCheckoutSchema (http/schemas/storefront.schema.ts:127-136) takes `phone` as z.unknown() straight from the caller. 4) :207 `const customer = await guestCustomerFor(store.id, body)`. 5) services/guest-checkout.service.ts:51 `prisma.customer.findFirst({ where: { storeId, phoneNormalized } })` — the ONLY input is the phone the caller typed. :52 refuses only when `!existing.isGuest`; a guest row is RETURNED. :57 `prisma.customer.update({ where: { id: existing.id }, data: { fullName, phone, ...(email ? { email } : {}) } })` — an anonymous caller has now overwritten another person's stored name and email. 6) Back at routes/storefront.ts:208 `createTypedSession("customer", customer.id)` and :209 `setCustomerSession(response, store.slug, accessToken)` → http/shared.ts:71-73 stages `Set-Cookie: codiq_customer_session_<slug>` immediately. 7) :210 `guestCheckout(...)` may throw — guest-checkout.service.ts:87 throws 422 CART_EMPTY when the attacker has no cart — but http/errors.ts:38 answers with `response.status(...).json(...)` and never clears the staged cookie, so the 422 response carries a valid session. 8) That cookie passes http/shared.ts:228-233: verifyTypedSession succeeds, the customer row is active and in this store, and `isGuest` is selected at :231 but gates nothing. 9) Therefore routes/storefront.ts:221 (customerOrders, storefront.service.ts:1031), :222 (customerOrder, storefront.service.ts:1049 — recipientPhone, shippingAddressLine1/2 included), :162 (listAddresses), :232 (customerPrescriptionFile, services/prescription.service.ts:95-99) and :215 (setGuestPassword, auth/auth.service.ts:276-283, which accepts precisely because isGuest is true) all answer for the victim's row. No phone-possession check exists anywhere on the platform: a grep of phase1-backend/src for otp/phoneVerified/verifyPhone finds only the owner and platform-staff TOTP files.

**العلاج المقترح:** This door must never return a row it did not create. In guest-checkout.service.ts:51-57, if a row already exists for that (storeId, phoneNormalized) — guest or not — refuse with the same generic «سجّل الدخول لإتمام الطلب» and mint nothing; only a freshly created row (:59) may be handed a session. And move `createTypedSession`/`setCustomerSession` (routes/storefront.ts:208-209) to AFTER `guestCheckout` returns successfully, so no failed request ever leaves a session cookie behind. Never let an unauthenticated body update an existing customer's fullName/email (:57).

### 11-15-21 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:129`

**الادّعاء:** The customer sign-in and registration doors are rate-limited per IP address with no refund on success, so honest shoppers behind one Iraqi carrier NAT lock each other out of the store they are trying to buy from

**الأثر المزعوم:** Twelve sign-ins per store per IP per ten minutes, and eight registrations — counted whether the password was right or wrong, with nothing given back when it was right. On an Iraqi mobile network a whole neighbourhood sits behind one public address (the codebase says exactly this, twice, in the comments cited below), so the thirteenth returning shopper of a busy evening on a store running an Instagram promotion is told «كثرت المحاولات. انتظر قليلًا ثم حاول مجددًا.» having done nothing wrong, and the ninth new shopper cannot create the account at all. There is no card on file and no receipt trail to pull an abandoned COD shopper back; he closes the tab. The platform already knows the fix and has applied it to its two LOWER-traffic doors and not to the shopper's: the merchant door and the staff door both get a limit of 30 AND a refund on success, while the customer door — the highest-volume login on the platform — gets 12 and none. This is the phase's «sign in by PHONE» clause kept in name and broken in substance.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:129 — `await limitStorefrontWrite(request, "customer_login", request.ip ?? "unknown", 12);` and :117 — `await limitStorefrontWrite(request, "customer_register", request.ip ?? "unknown", 8);`. Neither handler (:114-124 register, :126-137 login) calls any refund after the successful `registerCustomer` / `loginCustomer`; the only refund in the file is for checkout replays at :193. → phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:94-96 keys the bucket on `${request.params.storeSlug}:${rawKey}` with a 10-minute window — the raw key here is the bare IP, and phase1-backend/src/http/app.ts:46 sets `app.set("trust proxy", 1)`, so this really is the shopper's shared carrier address. → phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:246 throws 429 RATE_LIMITED once `attempt_count > limit`. → client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:349 renders that to the shopper as `RATE_LIMITED: "كثرت المحاولات. انتظر قليلًا ثم حاول مجددًا."`. The contrast is in the same repository: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/auth.ts:45-46 consumes `dashboard_login` at limit 30 for the merchant and :54 calls `refundStorefrontRateLimit(rateKey)` on success; :80-81 and :84 do the same for staff. The rule is written out at phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:190-195 — a budget «meant to slow guessing must not be spent by people who guessed right… one shared address — the ordinary case on Iraqi mobile networks — locks out an honest merchant after thirty of his neighbours sign in correctly» — and at auth.ts:49-53.

**العلاج المقترح:** Mirror the merchant door exactly on both storefront routes: hold the rate key, and after `loginCustomer` / `registerCustomer` returns successfully call `refundStorefrontRateLimit`. Better still, use `limitStorefrontWriteWithAddressCeiling` (shared.ts:118) so the tight bucket is keyed on the phone the caller typed and the wide, un-forgeable ceiling on the address — the shape the checkout route already uses.

### 11-15-22 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:227`

**الادّعاء:** The low-stock dedupe key embeds the exact stock quantity, so the second time a variant sells down to the same number the alert is silently swallowed — and it stays swallowed for 90 days

**الأثر المزعوم:** The merchant gets one low-stock warning per (variant, landing-quantity) pair per 90 days. In the most common Iraqi retail case — unit sales — the stock always crosses the threshold at exactly the threshold value, so he is warned once and then never again: every later restock-and-sell-down cycle on that variant is silent. He finds out he is at zero when a shopper's order cannot be fulfilled at the door, or when the till clamps a POS sale. The failure is completely invisible: the P2002 is caught and discarded, no row appears in the notifications log, no outcome is written, and the merchant's only other stock signal (the MD-3 daily digest, outbox-worker.ts:172-182) carries orders and revenue but nothing about stock. Phase 15's promised low_stock notification is therefore a one-shot per number rather than a standing alarm.

**الدليل المزعوم:** Every writer funnels into one helper: storefront COD completion at phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:1242, the till at phase1-backend/src/services/pos.service.ts:386 and :413, merchant inventory edits at phase1-backend/src/services/product-catalog.service.ts:384/:397/:505, and returns at phase1-backend/src/services/return-inspection.service.ts:216. In that helper, phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:212-214 computes the downward crossing (`wasLow = previousQuantity <= threshold`, `isLow = stockQuantity <= threshold`, return unless `!wasLow && isLow`) — this test alone already prevents spam while stock stays low. Line 227 then stamps `dedupeKey: `low_stock:${input.variantId}:${input.stockQuantity}``, i.e. keyed on the QUANTITY LANDED ON, not on the crossing episode. prisma/migrations/20260825120000_phase15_notification_outbox/migration.sql:35 makes that permanent: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX notification_outbox_dedupe ON notification_outbox(store_id, dedupe_key)`. Second episode: threshold 5, stock refilled to 40, sold down 6→5 — crossing test passes, insert raises P2002, and phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:231-234 catches `error.code === "P2002"` and `return`s with no row and no log. Nothing anywhere deletes or rewrites that key on restock (the only two occurrences of the literal `low_stock:` in the repo are line 227 itself and an assertion in phase1-backend/tests/phase20a.variants.integration.mjs:213). The only eventual release is the retention sweep — phase1-backend/src/services/retention-job.ts:111-116 deletes `status IN ('sent','skipped')` older than `RETENTION_DAYS.notificationOutboxSettled`, which phase1-backend/src/services/retention.ts sets to 90 days. The acceptance test asserts something strictly weaker than the criterion it claims: phase1-backend/tests/phase15.notifications.integration.ts:137-146 crosses first to quantity 2 (key `low_stock:v:2`), refills to 9, then crosses to quantity 3 (key `low_stock:v:3`) and asserts "a fresh crossing after refill fires again" — two DIFFERENT keys, so the repeat-at-the-same-quantity path is never exercised.
