**العلاج المقترح:** Key the dedupe on the crossing EPISODE rather than the resting quantity. Smallest correct change: replace the quantity segment with a marker that changes when the variant climbs back above its threshold — e.g. `low_stock:${variantId}:${lastRestockId}` using the id of the most recent upward inventory_movement, or drop the dedupe key from this call entirely and keep only the `!wasLow && isLow` crossing test plus a narrow guard against the concurrent double-insert (the race is the only thing the key was buying that the crossing test does not already buy).

### 11-15-23 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:231`

**الادّعاء:** enqueueLowStockIfCrossed swallows P2002 inside the caller's transaction, so a repeat low-stock crossing to a quantity already alerted permanently blocks the order completion, POS sale or write-off that triggered it

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant walks a delivered COD order to `completed` and gets a 500 — forever. Every retry hits the same wall, so the order can never leave `out_for_delivery`/`processing`: the stock decrement rolls back (phantom inventory that keeps overselling), no `inventory_movement` row, no `recordOrderCompletion` ledger entry, no loyalty points, and the courier has already taken the cash. The same swallow sits on the POS till (a cashier who cannot ring up an item) and on the RI-1 write-off. The trigger is ordinary trade, not a race: the dedupe key `low_stock:{variantId}:{stockQuantity}` carries no time component and the row survives 90 days (`retention.ts:34`), so the SECOND time a variant crosses down to the same number — sell out to 0, restock, sell out to 0 again — it collides.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) `order-lifecycle.service.ts:243` opens `prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {` (no isolation level, no retry wrapper). 2) `order-lifecycle.service.ts:256` flips the order to `completed`, `:259` calls `settleStorefrontReservations(tx, ...)`. 3) `storefront.service.ts:1238` decrements the variant, `:1240` writes the movement, `:1242` calls `enqueueLowStockIfCrossed(tx, ...)`. 4) `notification-outbox.service.ts:219-230` does `tx.notificationOutbox.create({ ... dedupeKey: `low_stock:${input.variantId}:${input.stockQuantity}` ... })`; the plain index `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX notification_outbox_dedupe ON notification_outbox(store_id, dedupe_key)` (prisma/migrations/20260825120000_phase15_notification_outbox/migration.sql:35) raises 23505 → P2002. 5) `notification-outbox.service.ts:231-234` catches P2002 and RETURNS, leaving the PostgreSQL transaction aborted. 6) The very next statement, `storefront.service.ts:1249` `tx.inventoryReservation.updateMany(...)`, dies with 25P02 and the whole transaction rolls back. The codebase already documents this exact failure and forbids the pattern: `loyalty.service.ts:298-303` — "`skipDuplicates`, NOT a try/catch on P2002. Catching a unique violation inside a PostgreSQL transaction does not work: the failed statement aborts the whole transaction, and every statement after it dies with 25P02. This runs inside the order-transition transaction, so a swallowed collision would have taken the entire completion down with it". Same defect on the other three callers, each with writes after it: `pos.service.ts:386` and `:413` (followed by `:420` `posTicketShortfall.create` and `:428` `posTicket.update`), `return-inspection.service.ts:216` (followed by `:233` `inventoryReservation.updateMany`), `product-catalog.service.ts:384/397/505`. `enqueueCourierDispatched` shows the authors knew: `notification-outbox.service.ts:150-153` says its pre-check exists "so the common repeat path never raises a constraint violation inside the caller's transaction" — and then names `enqueueLowStockIfCrossed`, which has only the broken second line, as its model. Untested: `phase15.notifications.integration.ts:137-146` crosses to 2, refills to 9, crosses to 3 — never twice to the same number.

**العلاج المقترح:** Replace the try/catch with `tx.notificationOutbox.createMany({ data: [ ... ], skipDuplicates: true })`, exactly as `loyalty.service.ts:304` does for the same reason — `skipDuplicates` becomes `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and never aborts the transaction. Separately make the key unique per crossing (append the movement or order id, or a crossing sequence) so a genuine second crossing at the same quantity still alerts the merchant instead of being silently suppressed.

### 11-15-24 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:304`

**الادّعاء:** Only the provider call is inside a try/catch — a row that fails to RENDER, or any transient DB error in the compliance gate, escapes processOutboxBatch, aborts the whole batch, and is never retried or dead-lettered despite the file claiming it is

**الأثر المزعوم:** One unrenderable row halts the entire platform's outbox on every pass, forever. The rows claimed alongside it in the same batch are stranded in `processing` for the full 5-minute stale window before they are even reclaimed, so throughput collapses from ~20 rows every 5 seconds to a partial batch every 5 minutes — a ~100x slowdown of every store's messaging at once. The message that pays for that delay is «طلب جديد», the trigger for the COD confirmation call the code itself calls the most expensive message on this platform to lose. The poison row never advances: attemptCount is never incremented, so OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS is never reached, it never becomes a dead letter, and the ق-188 health watcher's dead-letter alarm never fires. The same hole swallows any transient Neon error on the six unguarded queries each row performs.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:290 opens `for (const row of claimed)`. Inside it, line 304 `const message = renderMessage(await withStockForecast(row))` runs with no guard; renderMessage's `auth.password_reset` branch at line 129 calls `openSecret("password-reset-link", …)`, which throws by design on a wrong or withdrawn key (phase1-backend/src/auth/secret-box.ts, openSecret: 'A value that IS an envelope and fails to open is an ERROR, never a fallback'). Also unguarded: `notificationKind` (line 305), `operationalCaps`/`operationalSentToday` (line 347), `isUnsubscribed` (379), `marketingSentTodayToRecipient` (389), and every `prisma.notificationOutbox.update` (354, 374, 381, 390, 402, 411, 428, 438, 467, 474, 478) — all DB round-trips. The ONLY try/catch in the loop is lines 445-458, and it wraps exactly `provider.sendSms` / `provider.sendEmail`. So the throw propagates out of processOutboxBatch, past line 483, into `runJobPass("outbox", …).catch((error) => logger.error("job.failed", …))` at lines 498-505; the pass ends, and every row claimed at lines 283-286 but not yet updated stays `status: "processing"` with a claim token. Recovery is only lines 257-260, which requires `claimedAt < now - STALE_CLAIM_RECOVERY_MS` (line 44 = 5 minutes) and resets `nextAttemptAt: now` — so the poison row, still holding the oldest nextAttemptAt and operational priority 0 (orderBy at line 275), is re-claimed at the head of the very next batch and throws again. The retry/dead-letter ladder at lines 472-479 is never reached for it. The file asserts the opposite at lines 122-127: '`openSecret` throws, the worker's catch turns that into a retry and then a dead letter, which is the correct ending' — there is no such catch.

**العلاج المقترح:** Wrap the whole per-row body (from line 304 through the terminal update) in a try/catch that funnels an unexpected throw into the SAME ladder the provider failure uses at lines 472-479 — increment attemptCount, schedule the backoff, dead-letter at OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS with an outcome like `render_failed:${error.name}` — and `continue` to the next row so one bad row can never strand the rest of its batch.

### 11-15-25 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/services/finance.service.ts:330`

**الادّعاء:** A settlement whose period starts after the previous settlement's end is accepted, and every ledger entry in the gap is never paid and never shown as pending — permanently, with no in-app repair

**الأثر المزعوم:** The merchant is silently short-paid for every order completed inside the gap, and the platform never books its commission on them. Nobody sees a wrong number; they see a smaller one. The default UI range makes the gap the ORDINARY path, not an edge: the owner settles a merchant mid-month (say 20 Sept), and the next time he opens the Finance Center in October the form's default range is [1 Oct, today] — so the eleven days between 20 Sept and 1 Oct are settled by nothing. The merchant's «المستحق لك» then drops those days too, because merchantWallet counts only entries strictly after the LATEST period_to. Both rows are immutable at the database level, and createSettlement refuses any later attempt to cover the gap because that attempt's periodFrom would be earlier than the newest period_to. For a cash-on-delivery platform whose entire revenue is a commission on completed orders, this is money that leaves the books in both directions and can never be recovered through the product.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/components/FinanceCenter.tsx:47 initialises the screen's `from` to the 1st of the CURRENT UTC month (`new Date(Date.UTC(now.getUTCFullYear(), now.getUTCMonth(), 1))`) — it is never seeded from the merchant's last settlement. client/src/components/FinanceCenter.tsx:75 posts that same value as the settlement period: `periodFrom: `${from}T00:00:00Z`, periodTo: `${to}T23:59:59Z``. Server side, phase1-backend/src/services/finance.service.ts:305 resolves the period (settlementPeriod at :170-174 only clamps a FUTURE `to` down to now); :325-329 reads `previous` as the settlement with the greatest period_to; :330 is the only guard — `if (previous && from < previous.periodTo) throw 409 SETTLEMENT_PERIOD_OVERLAP` — which fires only on overlap and passes anything strictly LATER. :333-336 then sums exactly `ledgerEntry where createdAt >= from AND createdAt <= to`, and :338-353 writes the immutable snapshot with `periodTo = to`. The wallet mirrors the same window from the other side: phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-wallet.service.ts:85-91 takes `from = lastSettlement.periodTo` (orderBy periodTo desc) and :96 counts pending as `createdAt > from`, so an entry with createdAt <= period_to that no settlement covered is invisible there as well. Nothing else catches it: prisma/migrations/20260904142000_rt_money_settlement_no_overlap/migration.sql:46-47 adds `EXCLUDE USING gist (merchant_id WITH =, tstzrange(period_from, period_to) WITH &&)` — an exclusion constraint forbids OVERLAP only, never a gap — and prisma/migrations/20260825190000_phase17_finance_domains_registration/migration.sql:88 installs the `settlements_immutable` trigger, so the row cannot be corrected. The repair path is closed by the same line that caused it: settling [20 Sept, 1 Oct] afterwards has `from < previous.periodTo` and is refused at finance.service.ts:330.

**العلاج المقترح:** Stop taking `periodFrom` from the caller. In createSettlement, derive it: `const from = previous ? previous.periodTo : (body.periodFrom ?? firstEntryCreatedAt)`, and refuse a client-supplied periodFrom that is not equal to `previous.periodTo` with a message naming the true start — the same 409 already exists for the overlap direction, it simply needs the gap direction too. While there, align the two range dialects that currently disagree: the ledger query at finance.service.ts:334 is closed `[from, to]` (`gte`/`lte`) while the exclusion constraint and merchant-wallet.service.ts:96 treat the range as half-open `[from, to)` (`gt`), so an entry landing exactly on a boundary instant is counted in both adjacent settlements; make the ledger query `gte: from, lt: to`.

### 11-15-26 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-home.service.ts:82`

**الادّعاء:** `GET /merchant/stores/:storeSlug/dashboard/home` is guarded by `orders.view` but returns the merchant's platform-wide wallet balance and last settlement, so any counter cashier reads the owner's money across all his stores

**الأثر المزعوم:** `store_staff` — the lowest store role, the counter cashier and the packer — holds `orders.view` by default. One GET on his own store's home endpoint returns `wallet.pendingNetIqd` (everything the platform currently owes the merchant, summed over EVERY store he owns, not just this one), `wallet.pendingOrders`, and `wallet.lastSettlement` with the exact net amount and date of the merchant's last payout. The platform's own routing says staff must not have this: the dedicated reads `GET /merchant/finance/wallet` and `GET /merchant/finance/settlements` are behind `requireMerchantSession` (platform.ts:194-202), which admits the merchant identity only — no store permission code opens them, and none exists for finance in the store matrix at all. The codebase applies the opposite standard one file over: the delete-confirmation sheet strips its order counts from a reader who lacks `orders.view` precisely because «a permission that is denied and changes nothing empties the team screen of its meaning» (delete-preview.service.ts:51-69). Here a permission that was never granted is served anyway. For a multi-store merchant it is worse than a privacy leak: a manager hired for one shop learns the takings of the other two.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) `store_staff` holds `orders.view` in the approved matrix: phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:45. 2) The route admits any store actor with that one code and hands the service the MERCHANT id: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant-home.ts:13 (`const { store } = await requireStoreActor(request, "orders.view");`) and :14 (`merchantHome(store.id, store.merchantId, …)`). 3) The aggregate calls the wallet unconditionally — no actor kind is passed in and none is checked: phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-home.service.ts:69 (signature takes `storeId, merchantId` only) and :82 (`merchantWallet(merchantId, now)` inside the `Promise.all`). 4) It ships in the response body: merchant-home.service.ts:121-122, whose own comment reads «The balance is the MERCHANT's, across his stores — one wallet, one payout». 5) The wallet really is cross-store and merchant-scoped: phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-wallet.service.ts:96 (`where: { store: { merchantId }, createdAt: … }`), :85-89 (last settlement by `merchantId`), :100 (`settlement.aggregate({ where: { merchantId } })`). 6) The merchant-only contrast: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/platform.ts:194-196 and :198-202 both open with `await requireMerchantSession(request)`. 7) The client proves these are the figures on screen: client/src/components/MerchantHome.tsx:157 renders «الرصيد (دينار عراقي)» from `home.wallet.pendingNetIqd` and :244 renders the last settlement amount.

**العلاج المقترح:** Thread the actor into the aggregate and omit the key rather than filter it in the client. In merchant-home.ts:13 destructure `{ store, actor }` and pass `actor.kind` to `merchantHome`; in merchant-home.service.ts:69-102 call `merchantWallet` only when `actor.kind === "merchant"` and leave `wallet` out of the returned object otherwise (merchant-home.service.ts:121-122), so an unauthorized reader gets an absent key rather than a zero — the same «absent, never invented» rule `deletePreview` follows at delete-preview.service.ts:116. `MerchantHome.tsx` is mounted only by the merchant shell, so no client change is required; type `wallet` as optional there for safety.

### 11-15-27 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:107`

**الادّعاء:** A store staff member's password is chosen by the merchant, is never marked provisional, and the staff dashboard has no screen to change it — so the employer keeps a permanent working key to the employee's identity and the store audit log attributes his acts to the employee

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every store staff account on the platform is created by the merchant (or by a store_manager) typing `temporaryPassword` at `POST /merchant/stores/:storeSlug/team`. Nothing makes it temporary and the employee has no way to replace it from his own dashboard, so the person who typed it can sign in as the employee at `/auth/staff/stores/:slug/login` for the life of the account. Because every store write records the ACTOR, not the device, the audit log, the order history, the order notes, the inventory movements and the POS ticket are then stamped with the employee's name. In a cash-on-delivery shop this is the difference between a till shortfall the shortfall queue attributes to the manager who caused it and one it attributes to the cashier who did not: `POST /merchant/stores/:storeSlug/pos/tickets` (pos.ts:82-90) books the sale under `{kind:"staff", id: actor.id}`, and `pos/shortfalls` is where the missing cash is reviewed. The platform already decided this exact question the other way for merchants — ق-218 (staff-permissions.ts:108-138) gates a merchant out of every surface until he replaces a password a platform actor chose, arguing that «a password somebody else chose opens nothing but the door that replaces it». The phase-14 identity, the only one of the five whose password is ALWAYS typed by someone else, is the one the rule was never applied to.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) `createStoreStaff` hashes the caller-supplied password straight into the new Staff row and marks nothing: phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:107 (`const passwordHash = await createPasswordHash(input.temporaryPassword);`) and :109 (`tx.staff.create({ data: { email, passwordHash, displayName: … } })`). 2) There is no column to mark it with: `model Staff` has id/email/passwordHash/displayName/isActive/timestamps only — prisma/schema.prisma:948-959 — while `Merchant` carries `passwordSetByProvisionerAt` at prisma/schema.prisma:51. 3) No gate reads one: the merchant branch of `resolveStoreActor` calls `assertMerchantOwnsHisPassword` at phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:246, but `requireStoreStaff` (staff-permissions.ts:155-196) checks membership, isActive and merchant status and never asks about the credential. 4) The sign-in tells him nothing: `loginStaff` returns `{ accessToken, redirectTo }` only — phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:244-247 — whereas `loginMerchant` computes `mustChangePassword` and redirects to the security screen at auth.service.ts:190-197. 5) He cannot fix it himself from his shell: `staffNavEntries` has home/orders/till/products/inventory/customers/trash/activity/team/settings and no account or security entry — client/src/config/navigation.ts:350-364 — and `StaffDashboard.tsx`'s section switch (client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:141-180) mounts no `AccountSecurityPanel`, which is imported only by MerchantDashboard.tsx:34, OwnerDashboard.tsx:28 and PlatformStaffDashboard.tsx:15. The API route exists (`POST /auth/password`, account-security.ts:22-29, and `delegateNameFor("staff")` is supported in password.service.ts:71-105) but nothing in the product leads him to it. 6) The impersonation is recorded as the employee: `actorLabelFor` resolves a staff actor from `staff.displayName/email` at phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/audit-log.service.ts:51-52.

**العلاج المقترح:** Apply ق-218 to the identity phase 14 created. Add `password_set_by_provisioner_at` to `staff`, set it in `createStoreStaff` (staff.service.ts:109) beside the hash, clear it inside the same transaction as the new hash in `changeOwnPassword` (password.service.ts:112-114 already does exactly this for `claims.identityKind === "merchant"` — extend the branch to `"staff"`), and assert it in `requireStoreStaff` (staff-permissions.ts:155-196) the way the merchant branch does at :246, leaving `POST /auth/password` outside the gate as it already is. Then give the staff shell the one screen it lacks: a `{ id: "security", permission: null }` entry in `staffNavEntries` rendering `<AccountSecurityPanel view="password" />`, and have `loginStaff` return `mustChangePassword` + a `redirectTo` pointing at it, mirroring auth.service.ts:190-197.

### 11-15-28 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/services/store-bootstrap.service.ts:40`

**الادّعاء:** Every store is created with the notification master switch off, and nothing in the merchant's day ever turns it on — so a new shop's first COD orders raise no «طلب جديد» to the merchant and no «تأكيد طلبك» to the shopper, and no row is even written to prove it

**الأثر المزعوم:** This costs the merchant the single most expensive message on the platform, on the day it matters most. In an Iraqi COD shop the «طلب جديد» mail is the trigger for the confirmation phone call; without it the merchant learns about an order only when he next opens the dashboard, and the shopper — who reached the shop by phone and has no order-confirmation mail either — has no proof his order was received. It is silent by construction: because the guard returns BEFORE `notificationOutbox.create`, there is not even a `skipped` row in the notification log to hint that something was suppressed. Two strings actively tell him the opposite: the store-creation card promises «ينشأ المتجر بأساساته كاملة (رابط عام، توصيل، إشعارات)» (client/src/components/StoresManager.tsx:142) and the empty notification log promises «لا توجد إشعارات بعد؛ ستظهر هنا فور وقوع أول حدث» (client/src/components/NotificationCenter.tsx:279) — a sentence that is false for as long as the switch is off. The platform already knows how to warn about exactly this: AbandonedCartsPanel.tsx:128 renders «مفتاح الإشعارات الرئيسي للمتجر معطل — لن تُرسل أي رسالة حتى تفعيله من «الإعدادات ← الإشعارات».» — but only on the marketing panel, which a merchant who never sells by abandoned-cart recovery never opens.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. `createStoreFoundation` writes the settings row disabled: `notificationSettings: { create: { provider: null, apiKey: null, senderId: null, isEnabled: false } }` — phase1-backend/src/services/store-bootstrap.service.ts:40. 2. `storeNotificationPolicy` maps that column to `enabled: store.notificationSettings?.isEnabled === true` — phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:77. 3. `enqueueOrderCreated` returns before writing anything: `if (!policy || !policy.enabled) return;` — notification-outbox.service.ts:95, so neither the merchant row (:98) nor the customer row (:101) is created. The same guard kills status changes (:128), «مندوبك في الطريق» (:162) and low stock (:216). 4. The only writer that can flip it is the merchant's own checkbox: notification-settings.service.ts:171 `...(body.isEnabled !== undefined ? { isEnabled: body.isEnabled } : {})`, driven by NotificationCenter.tsx:190 `data-testid="notif-enable"`. Grepping `notificationSettings.(update|upsert)` across phase1-backend/src finds only that call, abandoned-cart.service.ts:101 (`create: { storeId, isEnabled: false, ... }`) and messaging-caps.service.ts:61 — none enables it. 5. The onboarding path that is supposed to walk him through setup has five steps and none is notifications: `first_product`, `product_image`, `delivery_enabled`, `order_preview`, `store_published` — merchant-onboarding.service.ts:53-59 — and the card hides itself for good once those five are done (`visible: !store.onboardingDismissedAt && completedCount < steps.length`, :69). 6. The acceptance suite only ever runs the feature in a state the product never produces: it asserts the default is off (phase1-backend/tests/phase15.notifications.integration.ts:63) and then turns it on by hand with a PUT at :69 before any of the enqueue assertions run.

**العلاج المقترح:** Two lines of defence, either of which closes it. (a) Ship the operational trio on: `isEnabled: true` at store-bootstrap.service.ts:40. The master switch was designed to gate an external provider the merchant configures (`provider`/`apiKey`/`senderId` beside it), and that provider is platform-level today — there is nothing for the merchant to configure, so the off default protects nothing and only loses his orders. (b) If it must stay off, make it discoverable: add a sixth onboarding step keyed on `notificationSettings.isEnabled` with `href` to the notifications tab, and change NotificationCenter.tsx:279 to say the truth when the switch is off — e.g. «الإشعارات معطّلة لهذا المتجر، فلا يُسجَّل أي حدث. فعّلها من المفتاح أعلاه.» — instead of promising rows that can never arrive. Also drop «إشعارات» from the StoresManager.tsx:142 list of ready foundations, or make it true.

### 11-15-29 · **عالية** · `phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:266`

**الادّعاء:** Two concurrent "add to cart" taps from a guest each mint their own cart and their own cookie, so one shopper's item lands in a cart he can never reach again

**الأثر المزعوم:** A guest browsing the home rails or the catalogue taps «أضف إلى السلة» on product A and, a beat later, on product B. Both cards stay enabled (ProductCard.tsx:42 disables only the card whose id equals `adding`), so on a normal Iraqi mobile connection the two POSTs overlap. Neither request carries a guest cookie yet — the cookie only exists after a successful add — so each mints a fresh random token and creates its own `shopping_carts` row. Both answer 200 with «تمت إضافة المنتج إلى السلة», the browser keeps whichever `Set-Cookie` arrived last, and the other cart plus its item is unreachable until it expires 7 days later. The shopper opens the cart, sees one of the two products, and either re-adds it or leaves. With COD there is no saved card and no receipt pulling him back — a cart that silently drops what he just added is a closed tab. The signed-in variant of the same race is louder but no better: the partial unique index fires and the shopper is told, in English, «An account with this identity already exists in this permitted scope» for pressing an add-to-cart button.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/pages/StorefrontCatalogPage.tsx:39 holds `adding` as a single product id and :124 passes `adding={adding === product.id}`; ProductRail.tsx:36 does the same for the home-page rails; ProductCard.tsx:42 therefore disables only the tapped card, leaving every other add button live while a request is in flight. Each request reaches phase1-backend/src/http/routes/storefront.ts:92, which calls `addCartItem(store.id, { customerId, guestToken: guestCartToken(request) }, body)` — and guestCartToken reads a cookie that is only ever written at storefront.ts:93, `if (result.issuedGuestToken) setGuestCartCookie(...)`, i.e. after a successful add. In phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:263 `tokenHash` is undefined, :264 leaves `cart` null, :265 `expireGuestCartIfNeeded(null)` returns null, and :266-269 runs `randomGuestToken()` then `prisma.shoppingCart.create({ ... sessionTokenHash: hash(issuedGuestToken) ... })` — outside any transaction, with a freshly random identity per call. The only guard the database has is prisma/migrations/20260818011500_storefront_customer_carts_reservations_9a/migration.sql:52, `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX shopping_carts_active_guest_token_uq ON shopping_carts(store_id, session_token_hash) WHERE status='active' AND owner_kind='guest'` — two different random tokens never collide, so both inserts commit. For a signed-in shopper the same read-then-create at storefront.service.ts:259-260 does collide, on migration.sql:51 `shopping_carts_active_customer_uq`; the resulting P2002 is not caught by `addCartItem` (no retry wrapper) and falls through every branch of phase1-backend/src/http/errors.ts:181-208 to :209, `DUPLICATE_IDENTITY` 409.

**العلاج المقترح:** Mint the guest cart identity once, before any write: set the guest cookie in `GET /store/:storeSlug/cart` (storefront.ts:76-81) when it is absent, so every subsequent add carries a token and `activeCartForContext` binds the cart to the token it was given instead of inventing one. Then wrap the two `shoppingCart.create` calls (storefront.service.ts:260 and :268) in the existing `withSerializableRetry` (phase1-backend/src/db/serializable-retry.ts:64, which already treats P2002 as retryable), so the loser of the race re-reads the winner's cart rather than creating a second one or answering 409.

### 11-15-30 · متوسطة · `client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:135`

**الادّعاء:** The sidebar's inline «إنشاء متجر جديد» form creates a store whose public link can never be changed, that can never be deleted, and that permanently spends one of the merchant's 3 plan store slots — and it is the one of the two creation surfaces that warns about none of it

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant opening his second branch clicks «إنشاء متجر جديد» in the sidebar — the nearest path, and the one the phase's own browser test drives. He types a store name in Arabic and improvises a Latin link under a small hint that mentions only characters and length. He mistypes it, or picks something he regrets a week later. He then discovers three things nobody told him: the link is his shop's permanent public address (only the name can ever be changed), there is no way to delete the store, and the dead store counts against his plan forever — on the free plan that is one of three, so two careless attempts leave him with a single usable slot and two ghost shops he cannot remove. In Iraq that link is what he prints on the shop's sign and sends on WhatsApp; getting it wrong is not cosmetic. The dedicated creation page does warn him («الرابط هوية عامة ولا يتغير لاحقاً»); the sidebar form, which is on every page, does not. Worse, the same click silently moves his whole session to the new empty store while he was mid-work in the old one.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The inline form's only guidance is «أحرف إنجليزية صغيرة وأرقام وشرطات، من 3 إلى 50 حرفاً.» — client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:135, inside the form at :132-138 — with no word about permanence, deletion or the plan. 2. The dedicated page does say it: «الرابط هوية عامة ولا يتغير لاحقاً.» — client/src/components/StoresManager.tsx:150. 3. The slug is in fact permanent: the only update route takes `name` alone (phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:95-100) and `renameMerchantStore` writes `data: { name }` (phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:63,70). 4. There is no store deletion at all — no `app.delete("/merchant/stores/:storeSlug")` anywhere under phase1-backend/src/http/routes/, and `model Store` has no soft-delete column (prisma/schema.prisma:155-173). 5. The dead store keeps spending the quota: `max_stores` usage is `client.store.count({ where: { merchantId } })` with no isActive or deleted filter (phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:68), checked at phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:121, against a seeded limit of 3 on free and 5 on growth (prisma/migrations/20260825150000_phase16_plans_entitlements/migration.sql:67,72). 6. The session moves on success and the form never says so: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:61-63 mints a session for the new store and overwrites the cookie, and the client just follows `redirectTo` (client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:99-101).

**العلاج المقترح:** Give the inline form the same sentence the dedicated page carries and finish it: «الرابط هو العنوان العام لمتجرك ولا يتغير بعد الإنشاء، ولا يمكن حذف المتجر لاحقاً — ويُحتسب ضمن عدد المتاجر في خطتك.» Add a confirm step that shows the final `/store/<slug>` verbatim before the POST, and say plainly that the session will move to the new store.

### 11-15-31 · متوسطة · `client/src/components/ProductCard.tsx:42`

**الادّعاء:** «أضف إلى السلة» on the catalog grid and the home rails posts no variantId, so a multi-variant product is silently added in its default size — or refused as «العنصر المطلوب غير موجود» while it sits in stock on screen

**الأثر المزعوم:** The card is the only add-to-cart the shopper meets on the two busiest storefront surfaces, and it shows a price RANGE for a multi-variant product — so the shopper can see there are options, and the button adds one for him without asking which. He discovers the size or colour he did not choose in the cart, or, for COD, at the door: a wrong-size shirt is a refused delivery, and the merchant eats the courier fee in both directions with no card to charge back. Two sharper failures ride on the same line. (a) A product is listed as available when ANY active variant has stock, but the quick-add resolves the DEFAULT variant, which may be the out-of-stock one — the cart accepts it (add-to-cart never checks stock) and checkout refuses it at the last step, which is precisely the moment ق-221's own comment says a COD shopper closes the tab. (b) If the merchant deactivated the default variant while other variants stay live — the variant toggle permits it, nothing protects the default's `isActive` — the quick-add 404s and the shopper is told «العنصر المطلوب غير موجود أو غير متاح.» about a product he is looking at, in stock. And that refusal is drawn in the same soft-accent `role="status"` notice bar as «تمت إضافة المنتج إلى السلة.», so on a 390px phone a failed add is visually indistinguishable from a successful one and self-dismisses after 7 seconds.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. client/src/components/ProductCard.tsx:42 — `onAdd(product.id)`; the card holds no variant data at all, and :40 shows `priceLabel(product)`, which renders `min–max` when the variants differ (ProductCard.tsx:19-24). 2. client/src/pages/StorefrontCatalogPage.tsx:77 — `storefrontApi.addCartItem(storeSlug, productId, 1)` with no fourth argument; the home rails do the same at client/src/pages/StorefrontHomePage.tsx:118 (ProductRail passes the card's `onAdd` straight through, client/src/components/ProductRail.tsx:36). 3. Server resolution: phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:463 — with no `variantId`, `findFirst({ productId, storeId, isDefault: true, isActive: true, product: { isActive: true } })`. No stock is consulted anywhere in `addCartItem` (storefront.service.ts:451-480). 4. Availability on the card is any-variant: phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:380 — `isAvailable: product.variants.some(...)` — while the list payload carries no per-variant data (storefront.service.ts:354 selects only `id, priceIqd, stockQuantity`, and the response at :369-381 exposes neither). 5. A deactivated default is reachable: the list query filters `variants: { where: { isActive: true } }` (storefront.service.ts:354), and phase1-backend/src/services/product-catalog.service.ts:490-493 lets `updateVariant` set `isActive: false` on any variant including the default — only DELETION of the default is guarded (product-catalog.service.ts:524). The `findFirst` at storefront.service.ts:463 then returns null → 404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND (storefront.service.ts:465). 6. The refusal is styled as success: StorefrontCatalogPage.tsx:82 and StorefrontHomePage.tsx:123 both push the error through `setNotice(errorMessage(caught))` — the same setter as the success string at StorefrontCatalogPage.tsx:79 — and client/src/components/StorefrontShell.tsx:208 renders `notice` in one `role="status"` bar on `bg-[var(--storefront-accent-soft)]`, auto-cleared after 7s (StorefrontShell.tsx:84-86). 7. The product page proves the intended behaviour: client/src/pages/StorefrontProductPage.tsx:145-152 renders a variant picker that disables unavailable variants, :27 preselects an AVAILABLE one, and :93 sends the chosen `selectedVariant.id`.

**العلاج المقترح:** Carry per-variant data into the list payload (`variants: [{ id, name, priceIqd, isAvailable }]` from storefront.service.ts:369-381) and make the card's button open the variant chooser — or link to the product page — whenever the product has more than one active variant, adding directly only for single-variant products. Fall back safely on the server: when no `variantId` is given and no active default exists, resolve the first active variant instead of 404-ing (storefront.service.ts:463). Separately, give failures their own channel: render add-to-cart errors as `role="alert"` in a danger-toned bar rather than in the same accent-soft success notice (StorefrontShell.tsx:208).

### 11-15-32 · متوسطة · `client/src/components/StorefrontProvider.tsx:54`

**الادّعاء:** When GET /store/:slug/public-config fails, the store's front page and the guest checkout page spin forever with no error and no retry — the same happens for an unknown or platform-disabled store slug

**الأثر المزعوم:** `config` is the gate on both of the storefront's two most valuable screens, and the one fetch that populates it swallows its own failure. A single dropped request on a 3G connection — the ordinary condition of this market — leaves the shopper looking at «جارٍ تحميل المتجر…» on the shop's front page, or «جارٍ تجهيز الطلب…» on checkout, permanently: no ErrorState, no retry button, no second attempt, and no reason for him to guess that a manual reload would help. The shell above it renders normally, so the page looks alive and merely stuck. The same code path is what a shopper meets on a mistyped slug or on a store the platform has disabled: `resolveStorefrontStore` answers 404 and the shopper gets an eternal spinner under a header titled with the raw slug instead of «this store does not exist». This is the phase's «Loading/Empty/Error states on every screen» clause broken on the two screens where it is worth the most — StorefrontHomePage is the only storefront page with no ErrorState at all.

**الدليل المزعوم:** client/src/components/StorefrontProvider.tsx:52-54 — `storefrontApi.publicConfig(slug).then(...).catch(() => { if (!cancelled) initStorefrontPixels({}); })`: the catch initialises the pixels module and returns; `config` is never set and there is no error state and no retry in the context (its shape at :6-14 has no config error field). → client/src/pages/StorefrontHomePage.tsx:127 `if (!config || banners === null) return <LoadingState label="جارٍ تحميل المتجر…" />;` — `banners` does resolve, via its own `.catch(() => setBanners([]))` at :99, so `config === null` alone holds this branch forever. → client/src/pages/StorefrontCheckoutPage.tsx:154 `if (storefrontLoading || (!customer && !config)) return <LoadingState label="جارٍ تجهيز الطلب…" />;` — the same permanent branch for any shopper who is not signed in, i.e. every guest, who is the default buyer (prisma/schema.prisma:192, `guestCheckoutEnabled Boolean @default(true)`). → client/src/components/StorefrontShell.tsx:109 renders the whole shell regardless and :77 titles it `config?.name ?? slug`, so nothing above signals the failure. For the disabled/unknown store: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/marketing.ts:178 calls `resolveStorefrontStore` first, which throws 404 STORE_NOT_FOUND at phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:224 when the store or its merchant is inactive. Coverage confirms the gap — StorefrontHomePage.tsx is the only Storefront*/Customer* page that imports LoadingState without ErrorState.

**العلاج المقترح:** Track the config fetch's failure in the provider (a `configError` beside `config`) and render `<ErrorState message={…} retry={…} />` in place of the two LoadingState branches at StorefrontHomePage.tsx:127 and StorefrontCheckoutPage.tsx:154. Give the 404 its own branch: a store that does not resolve should say so, not load.

### 11-15-34 · متوسطة · `client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:145`

**الادّعاء:** The staff shell gates the sidebar by permission but hands the permitted screens no permission set, so a default store_staff sees تعديل / نسخ / إزالة on every product though the role holds none of catalog.products.create/update/delete

**الأثر المزعوم:** The acceptance criterion is «a menu item without permission neither renders nor works»; it is honoured for the menu and abandoned one level in. The ordinary shop assistant — the default `store_staff` role, which by design may look at the catalogue and touch stock but not change products — opens المنتجات and every product card offers him Edit, Duplicate and Remove. Tapping «إزالة» opens the delete confirmation sheet, which fetches a preview and tells him what will be removed and that he has 12 days to undo it; confirming answers 403. «تعديل» lets him retype a price and lose the edit on save. The same shape hits a member granted `customers.view` so he can look up a phone number: the directory shows him a «حظر» button whose endpoint needs `settings.manage`. Nothing is destroyed — the resolver holds — but the phase's own view/create/update/delete split has no effect on what the employee is shown, and the screen he uses most teaches him daily that the buttons lie. The shell already knows how to do this correctly and does it in exactly one place.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:45 — `store_staff: ["catalog.products.view", "inventory.update", "orders.view", "orders.update", "pos.sell"]`, so no create/update/delete. client/src/config/navigation.ts:354 gates the products entry on `catalog.products.view`, so it renders for every store_staff. client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:144-145 then mounts `<ProductCatalogManager storeSlug={storeSlug} mode="list" />` with no permission input — compare :155, where TrashBin alone receives `permissions={session.permissions}` (client/src/components/TrashBin.tsx:140 accepts it and :183 filters its tabs with it). client/src/components/ProductCatalogManager.tsx:68 takes only `{ storeSlug, mode }`, and :203 renders on every card «تعديل» (→ PATCH, phase1-backend/src/http/routes/catalog.ts:70 requires catalog.products.update), «نسخ» (→ POST .../duplicate, catalog.ts:114-116 requires catalog.products.create), «إزالة» (→ client/src/components/DeleteConfirmSheet.tsx:340 preview then :363 DELETE, catalog.ts:71 requires catalog.products.delete) and «المتغيرات» (catalog.ts:85-88). Same pattern at client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:151 → client/src/components/CustomersDirectory.tsx:43 (no permissions prop), whose :153 «حظر» button posts the PATCH at :93-94 that phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:79-81 guards with `settings.manage`, and at :142-143 → client/src/components/OrderWorkspace.tsx:672, which renders status transitions with no gate on `orders.update`.

**العلاج المقترح:** Thread `session.permissions` into ProductCatalogManager, CustomersDirectory and OrderWorkspace the way client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:155 already threads it into TrashBin, and gate each control on the code its endpoint requires (تعديل → catalog.products.update, نسخ → catalog.products.create, إزالة → catalog.products.delete, حظر → settings.manage). The merchant shell passes the full set, so nothing changes there.

### 11-15-35 · متوسطة · `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:207`

**الادّعاء:** No deploy configuration is versioned in the repository, and DEPLOYMENT.md contradicts itself about whether a production release runs migrations at all

**الأثر المزعوم:** The release procedure at DEPLOYMENT.md:145 tells the owner that merging `main` into `release` runs the build, then `pnpm phase1:db:migrate`, then the cutover. Sixty lines later the same document says the pre-deploy migrate command is configured «على مستوى بيئة staging فقط» and that production «غير ممسوسة». Both cannot be true, and the repository cannot settle it: there is no railway.json, railway.toml, nixpacks.toml, Procfile or Dockerfile for the API service anywhere in the tree — the only Dockerfile is docker/backup.Dockerfile, for the six-hourly dump. If the pessimistic reading is the true one, the first release carrying a migration boots new code against the old schema, and every query touching a new column throws for as long as it takes a human to notice — the storefront's product page and checkout 500 for every shopper on every store, on the launch day the whole plan is aimed at. Nothing rolls it back, because the one automated probe (finding above) still answers ok. The owner is asked to promote to production on the strength of a document that disagrees with itself about the single most dangerous step.

**الدليل المزعوم:** package.json:15 defines `phase1:db:migrate` as `node --import tsx phase1-backend/scripts/migrate-deploy.ts && pnpm secrets:encrypt`; phase1-backend/scripts/migrate-deploy.ts:41-49 is what actually runs `prisma migrate deploy`. The only invocation anywhere in the repository's automation is .github/workflows/ci.yml:89, against the throwaway CI PostgreSQL declared at ci.yml:37-55 — never against a deployed database. A file-type search across the tree for railway.json / railway.toml / nixpacks.toml / Procfile / Dockerfile / fly.toml / render.yaml returns one hit, docker/backup.Dockerfile, whose own header (line 4-5) confirms that every other service «builds with Railpack and no image definition» — i.e. the API's build and pre-deploy commands live only in dashboard fields. docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:145 states the release runs `pnpm phase1:db:migrate`; docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:204-208 states the pre-deploy command is set on staging only and that production is untouched. The same document, at :150-160, records that a previous production incident came from exactly this class of thing — a deploy setting that lived only in a dashboard and pointed at the wrong Neon branch.

**العلاج المقترح:** Put the deploy configuration in the repository: a railway.json (or the equivalent for whichever builder each service uses) carrying the build command, the start command and `preDeployCommand: pnpm phase1:db:migrate` for both the staging and the production service, so the migration step is diffable and reviewable rather than a dashboard field nobody can inspect. Then delete whichever of DEPLOYMENT.md:145 or :204-208 is false, and make the release checklist assert the deploy log's `[migrate]` line (migrate-deploy.ts:33-38 prints the host it used) before the cutover is trusted.

### 11-15-36 · متوسطة · `package.json:78`

**الادّعاء:** `pc1:test` is the only test suite in package.json that CI never runs, and it is the sole proof that a platform-staff member holding all nine permission codes cannot read or write the platform's own contact channels

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every other suite and guard in `package.json` appears in the workflow; this one does not. What it alone asserts is an identity-boundary property, not a cosmetic one: that `/platform/settings/contact` is owner-only against a *fully privileged* platform-staff session — the 19-ب identity that legitimately holds every permission code the platform grants — and that four separate refusals leave `platform_settings` untouched. Change that route's guard from `requirePlatformOwner` to any staff-permissioned guard and CI stays green, all 100-plus other steps included. The blast radius is the address and WhatsApp number every visitor and every merchant is told to use to reach Codiq: a staff member who could rewrite them redirects the platform's entire inbound support channel to himself, and the audit row would name him only after the fact.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. `package.json:78` — `"pc1:test": "node --import tsx phase1-backend/tests/pc1.platform-contact.integration.mjs"`. 2. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` contains no `pc1` step. Diffing every `pnpm <script>` invocation in the workflow against `package.json`'s script list leaves exactly one suite unreferenced — `pc1:test` — the rest of the remainder being tools and entry points (`dev`, `start`, `owner:create`, `backup:run`, `env:check`, `mail:probe`, `demo:seed`, `openapi:generate`, `theme:*`, `pg6:seed`, `perf:evidence`, `secrets:encrypt`, `postinstall`, `format`, `preview`, `phase1:start`, `phase15:worker`, `backup:restore`). 3. What goes unrun: `phase1-backend/tests/pc1.platform-contact.integration.mjs:164-166` — `assert.equal(staffAttempt.payload.error.code, "IDENTITY_BOUNDARY_DENIED", "PC-1: a supervisor with ALL NINE permission codes is still refused — no code grants the platform's own channels")`, plus the read refusal at :166 and `assert.equal((await contactRow("platform.contact.email")).rowCount, 0, …)` at :168. 4. The property still holds in the code today — `phase1-backend/src/http/routes/platform.ts:240-243` (`GET`) and :244-250 (`PUT`) both call `await requirePlatformOwner(request)` — so this is an unguarded invariant rather than a live hole. Nothing in CI would notice it changing. 5. The suite is CI-shaped and would run unmodified: it takes its database through `assertSafeTestDatabaseUrl(process.env.CODIQ_TEST_DATABASE_URL)` at `phase1-backend/tests/pc1.platform-contact.integration.mjs:40-41`, the same helper every suite in the integration job uses.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add a `- name: "Integration — PC-1 the platform's own contact channels" / run: pnpm pc1:test` step to the `integration` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Separately, the mechanism that let one suite go unwired deserves a ratchet of its own: a guard that fails when a `*:test` / `*:guard` script exists in `package.json` with no step referencing it — the same idiom `surface.guard.mjs` already uses for routes with no caller.

### 11-15-37 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:236`

**الادّعاء:** The staff sign-in door answers in English while the merchant door beside it was fully Arabised, and the page's Arabic fallback for those codes is unreachable

**الأثر المزعوم:** A store employee in Iraq who mistypes his password reads «Invalid staff credentials.» — a Latin-script sentence, left-to-right, inside an otherwise fully Arabic RTL page. If he mistypes the store id instead (which, per the finding above, he is being asked to guess) he reads «This staff account has no active membership in the requested store.» He is the least likely person on this platform to read English: the merchant may have some, a shop assistant paid by the day often has none. He cannot tell «wrong password» from «wrong store» from «your manager stopped you», so he calls the merchant, who calls support, for a mistyped character. The same two doors' merchant equivalents were translated — including a full actionable sentence for the no-active-store case — so this is not a pending translation phase, it is the staff door being skipped. The page even carries the correct Arabic for INVALID_STAFF_CREDENTIALS and it can never render.
