(code ? FALLBACK_MESSAGES[code] : null) ?? …`, so a present server message always wins → client/src/pages/DashboardLogin.tsx:74 defines `INVALID_STAFF_CREDENTIALS: "بيانات الدخول غير صحيحة."`, which the line above makes dead code, and FALLBACK_MESSAGES (DashboardLogin.tsx:71-79) has no entry at all for STAFF_STORE_CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND. Contrast the merchant door in the same file: phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:144, :156, :160 and :177 are all Arabic, and :177 («حسابك سليم، لكن لا يوجد متجر نشط مرتبط به حالياً. تواصل مع إدارة المنصة لإعادة تفعيل متجرك.») is precisely the shape the staff 404 needs and lacks.

**العلاج المقترح:** Translate the two staff-door messages in auth.service.ts:236 and :243 the way the merchant door already is, and give the 404 an action rather than a statement — e.g. «معرّف المتجر غير صحيح أو عضويتك في هذا المتجر موقوفة. تأكد من المعرّف الذي أرسله لك مديرك، أو اطلب منه إعادة تفعيل عضويتك.» Add STAFF_STORE_CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND to FALLBACK_MESSAGES so a message-less answer still lands in Arabic.

### 11-15-38 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:237`

**الادّعاء:** `requireMerchantSession` never reads `merchant.isActive`, so a merchant session that outlives a suspension keeps eight merchant routes — and the Phase 13 switch route lets it renew itself forever

**الأثر المزعوم:** ق-225 built suspension as the platform's only trust-and-safety lever in a cash-on-delivery market — "a shop that takes orders and never delivers, a merchant whose account is stolen" (phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-suspension.service.ts:16-17). Suspension is supposed to be terminal: it revokes every session and deactivates every store. It is not, for the eight routes behind this guard. A merchant session created in the window of the suspension transaction survives revocation, and because this guard never asks whether the merchant is still active, that session keeps: reading his wallet, settlement history and finance ledger (`GET /merchant/finance/wallet|settlements|report`, `GET /merchant/subscription`), filing upgrade requests, listing his stores — and, the one that writes permanent public state, CREATING NEW STORES. Each new store permanently occupies a globally-unique public slug (`slug @unique`, and no rename path exists anywhere in the codebase), and every one of them flips live the moment the platform ever un-suspends him, because `setMerchantSuspension` resumes with a blanket `store.updateMany({ where: { merchantId }, data: { isActive: true } })`. Worse, the foothold is self-renewing rather than time-boxed: the newly created store is active, so `POST /merchant/stores/switch` accepts it and mints a fresh 8-hour session on demand, indefinitely. He cannot log in again (auth.service.ts:166 refuses `!isActive`) and he cannot reach store-scoped routes (both other guards refuse him) — so this guard is the entire remaining hole, and it silently widens the day anyone adds a ninth route behind it.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The guard: phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:237-241 — `verifyTypedSession(bearerToken(request), "merchant")` then `assertMerchantOwnsHisPassword(claims.subjectId)` then `return claims`. No read of `merchant.isActive`. 2. Both sibling guards DO read it, and the codebase argues at length that they must: phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:250-252 (`requireMerchantStore` → 403 MERCHANT_DISABLED) and phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:240-242, whose ق-192 comment states the rule this violates — "a route's choice of guard must never change who may act". 3. The eight routes with no other check: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:40 (`GET /merchant/stores`), :45 (`POST /merchant/stores/switch`), :58 (`POST /merchant/stores`); phase1-backend/src/http/routes/platform.ts:171, :175, :195, :199, :308. 4. The surviving session: phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-suspension.service.ts:114-130 — inside one `prisma.$transaction`, `merchant.update` (line 115), `store.updateMany` (116), then `merchantSession.updateMany({ where: { merchantId, revokedAt: null } })` (125). Under READ COMMITTED that `updateMany` only sees session rows committed when the statement began; a concurrent `POST /merchant/stores/switch` inserts its row via `prisma.merchantSession.create` outside that transaction (phase1-backend/src/auth/identity.ts:205, called from routes/merchant.ts:48), so a row committing after that statement is never revoked. 5. That row then verifies forever: phase1-backend/src/auth/identity.ts:281 checks only `{ id, merchantId, storeId, tokenHash, revokedAt: null, expiresAt: { gt: now } }`. 6. Store creation with a suspended merchant: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:56-64 → `createMerchantStore` (phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:115-129) reads name, slug, plan limit and slug uniqueness — never `merchant.isActive` — and the row is born active (prisma/schema.prisma:162 `isActive Boolean @default(true)`). 7. The renewal loop: `merchantStoreForSwitch` (phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:52-55) requires `{ merchantId, slug, isActive: true }`, which the just-created store satisfies, and routes/merchant.ts:48 mints a new 8-hour session from it. 8. Login is not the way back in (phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:166-168 refuses `!merchant.isActive`), which is exactly why the surviving session is the whole finding.

**العلاج المقترح:** Read the merchant's live state in `requireMerchantSession` and refuse with the same 403 MERCHANT_DISABLED the other two guards raise. It costs no extra query: `assertMerchantOwnsHisPassword` (phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:131-138) already does `prisma.merchant.findUnique` for `passwordSetByProvisionerAt` — add `isActive: true` to that select and have `requireMerchantSession` check both from the one row, the same way `resolveStoreBySlug` (staff-permissions.ts:146) already carries both columns on one read.

### 11-15-39 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:227`

**الادّعاء:** The low-stock dedupe key is the stock QUANTITY, so the second time a variant crosses its threshold at the same number the alert is swallowed by a silent P2002 catch — and the key stays taken for 90 days

**الأثر المزعوم:** A shop that sells one unit at a time crosses its threshold at exactly the threshold value every single time: with `lowStockThreshold: 3`, the crossing is always 4→3 and the key is always `low_stock:<variantId>:3`. So the merchant gets ONE low-stock alert for that variant, ever — restock to 20, sell down to 3 again next week, and nothing is sent, nothing is logged, and no failure is recorded anywhere. Over a 90-day retention window a fast SKU can burn every reachable crossing value (3, 2, 1, 0) inside the first month, after which the variant is permanently silent. This is the merchant's one push signal about stock; he has a low-stock panel on his dashboard (client/src/components/MerchantHome.tsx:211), but the whole point of the alert is that he is at the counter or in a WhatsApp thread and not looking at the dashboard. The crossing guard at :212-214 already does the anti-spam job the comment claims for the dedupe key — «repeated writes while the variant stays low do not spam the merchant» is enforced by `wasLow`, not by the key — so keying on quantity buys nothing and costs the repeat alert.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The key is built from the post-sale quantity: `dedupeKey: \`low_stock:${input.variantId}:${input.stockQuantity}\`` — notification-outbox.service.ts:227. 2. It is globally unique per store with no time component: `@@unique([storeId, dedupeKey], map: "notification_outbox_dedupe")` — prisma/schema.prisma:1155. 3. A collision is swallowed with no trace: `catch (error) { if (error instanceof Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError && error.code === "P2002") return; throw error; }` — notification-outbox.service.ts:231-234. No row, no log line, no `lastOutcome`. 4. The crossing itself already fires at most once per downward crossing: `const wasLow = input.previousQuantity <= input.lowStockThreshold; const isLow = input.stockQuantity <= input.lowStockThreshold; if (wasLow || !isLow) return;` — :212-214. 5. The rows that hold the key live 90 days: `notificationOutboxSettled: 90` (`notification_outbox` rows that reached `sent` or `skipped`) — phase1-backend/src/services/retention.ts:34, swept by retention-job.ts:109-113. 6. Real unit-sale call sites that produce the repeating crossing: storefront.service.ts:1242 (`previousQuantity: before.stockQuantity, stockQuantity: before.stockQuantity - reservation.quantity`, on COD completion) and pos.service.ts:386 (`previousQuantity: variant.stockQuantity, stockQuantity: variant.stockQuantity - quantity`, at the till). 7. The acceptance test steps around the defect rather than catching it: it crosses first to 2 (phase15.notifications.integration.ts:137), refills to 9 (:142) and then crosses to 3 (:144) — a different quantity, so a different key — and asserts «a fresh crossing after refill fires again» (:146). Had the second crossing landed on 2, as a one-unit-at-a-time shop's always does, the count would still be 1 and the assertion would fail.

**العلاج المقترح:** Key the crossing, not the quantity. The `wasLow`/`isLow` check is the anti-spam rule; the dedupe key only has to stop a double-insert race for ONE crossing. Use something that identifies this crossing event — e.g. the `inventoryMovement` id already created beside it at storefront.service.ts:1240 / pos.service.ts:383, or at minimum add the date: `low_stock:${variantId}:${stockQuantity}:${now.toISOString().slice(0,10)}`. Passing the movement id (or a per-variant crossing counter) through `enqueueLowStockIfCrossed` keeps the race guard intact while letting next week's crossing through, and it makes the failing case above assertable: refill, cross again at the SAME quantity, expect two rows.

### 11-15-40 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:467`

**الادّعاء:** The outbox stamps a claim token on every row but never checks it when marking the row sent, so a batch that outlives the 5-minute stale-claim window is delivered twice

**الأثر المزعوم:** A shopper receives «تأكيد طلبك» twice, a merchant receives «طلب جديد» twice, and — worst — a password-reset mail is sent a second time from a row whose sealed link was already delivered. The daily caps that protect the platform's sending domain also double-count, because the second send stamps `sentAt` on a row the first send already counted. Two workers are not exotic here: every composite server process spawns its own worker child, so during Railway's normal deploy overlap (new container up, old one still serving) two full worker processes poll the same queue, and this is precisely when the second condition — an abrupt shutdown mid-batch — is also met.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:44 sets `STALE_CLAIM_RECOVERY_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000`; :257-260 every pass first returns any row `status: 'processing'` with `claimedAt` older than that window to `retry_scheduled`. :282-286 mints `claimToken` and claims up to 20 rows in one updateMany, stamping the SAME `claimedAt: now` on all of them; :287 reads them back. :290-481 then walks those rows one at a time, and each row can cost a full provider round trip capped at 20 seconds — phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-provider.ts:171 `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(20_000)` — so a batch of 20 timing-out sends runs ~6.7 minutes, past the 5-minute window, while its untouched tail still carries `claimedAt` from minute zero. Worker B's :257-260 releases that tail, :283 re-claims it, and delivers it. Worker A then reaches the same rows and delivers them again, because its terminal writes name the row by id alone with no claim-token predicate: :467 `update({ where: { id: row.id }, data: { status: 'sent', ... } })`, and the same at :474 and :478. The second reachable path is shutdown: server/index.ts:21-43 spawns one worker child per server process; phase1-backend/src/notifications/worker-entry.ts:79-91 `shutdown()` calls `worker.stop()` — which at outbox-worker.ts:507 only does `clearInterval(timer)` and does not await the pass in flight — then `prisma.$disconnect()` and `process.exit(0)`, so a row already accepted by the provider but not yet written back stays `processing` and is re-sent 5 minutes later.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the token load-bearing: change the three terminal writes (outbox-worker.ts:467, :474, :478) and the gate writes at :292/:297/:354/:374/:381/:390/:402/:411/:428/:438 from `update({ where: { id: row.id } })` to `updateMany({ where: { id: row.id, claimToken } })`, and skip the row when `count !== 1` — a row whose token no longer matches was taken from this worker and must not be sent or restamped. Additionally, have `startOutboxWorker` expose the in-flight promise so `worker-entry.ts:shutdown` can await the current pass before `$disconnect()`.

### 11-15-41 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:291`

**الادّعاء:** When no email provider is configured the worker marks every claimed row terminally `skipped` instead of leaving it queued, and the loss is invisible: no log line, no health alert, and the evidence is deleted after 90 days

**الأثر المزعوم:** The provider is resolved once at worker boot. Any window where the mail key is absent — a first deploy before CODIQ_RESEND_API_KEY is set, a variable dropped or renamed, a worker started ahead of its env — permanently destroys every notification the platform produced in that window at a rate of 20 rows every 5 seconds. Adding the key later re-sends nothing: `skipped` is terminal, so the «طلب جديد» rows for orders placed during the gap are gone and the merchant never learns those orders exist. Phase 15 promised retry-with-backoff-then-dead-letter for a send that does not happen; this is a third terminal state that gets neither. Worse, it is silent in all three places an operator would look: the pass logs nothing, the health watcher reports the queue healthy (it drained — to nowhere), and the retention sweep deletes the skipped rows on the same 90-day clock as successes, so the record of what was lost disappears too.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/notifications/worker-entry.ts:27 calls `resolveNotificationProvider()` ONCE at import and line 29 hands the result to `startOutboxWorker(provider, …)`; phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-provider.ts:346-362 returns null when demo mode is on or when neither `httpMailSettingsFromEnv` nor `smtpSettingsFromEnv` yields settings. Every 5 seconds startOutboxWorker (outbox-worker.ts:493-508) calls processOutboxBatch(provider); at line 291-295 `if (!provider)` writes `{ status: "skipped", lastOutcome: "no_provider_configured" }` and increments `result.skipped`. `skipped` is absent from CLAIMABLE_STATUSES (line 45 = `["pending", "retry_scheduled"]`), so the row can never be claimed again; attemptCount is left at 0, so the ladder at lines 472-479 never applies. Invisibility, three ways: (1) line 500 logs only `if (result.sent > 0 || result.deadLettered > 0 || result.retried > 0)` — `skipped` and `deferred` are omitted, so a pass that skips 20 rows emits no line at all; (2) phase1-backend/src/services/platform-health.service.ts:67 defines HEALTH_ALERT_KINDS as only `outbox_dead_letters` and `outbox_stalled`, counted at line 97 (`status: "dead_letter"`) and line 100 (`status IN ('pending','retry_scheduled')` past due) — a queue emptied into `skipped` matches neither, so the platform reports healthy while delivering nothing; (3) phase1-backend/src/services/retention-job.ts:114 deletes `status IN ('sent','skipped')` older than `RETENTION_DAYS.notificationOutboxSettled` = 90 days (phase1-backend/src/services/retention.ts).

**العلاج المقترح:** Treat a missing provider as a transient condition, not a verdict on the message: leave the row claimable by scheduling it (`status: "retry_scheduled"`, `nextAttemptAt` a few minutes out, `lastOutcome: "no_provider_configured"`) without incrementing attemptCount, exactly as the cap deferral at lines 353-363 does — or simply skip claiming at all when `provider` is null. At minimum, add `result.skipped`/`result.deferred` to the log condition on line 500 and a `skipped` counter to HEALTH_ALERT_KINDS so a platform delivering nothing cannot report itself healthy.

### 11-15-42 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:93`

**الادّعاء:** The order-status-change email interpolates the raw English status enum, so an Arabic-speaking shopper is told «تغيرت حالة الطلب … من new إلى out_for_delivery»

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every order.status_changed message this platform sends — to the shopper and to the merchant alike — contains two untranslated English identifiers in the middle of an Arabic sentence. In a product whose language is Arabic, for a market where the shopper is a phone-first Arabic speaker who may not read Latin script at all, the one line the message exists to convey is the one line he cannot read. It is not a missing translation somewhere in the codebase: five client surfaces already carry the Arabic map, so the shopper who taps the tracking link sees «خرج للتوصيل» while the email about the same event says `out_for_delivery`. The `out_for_delivery` transition makes the contrast plain, because CD-1b's «مندوبك في الطريق» notice fires from the same transaction in correct Arabic — the shopper receives both, one readable and one not.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/order-lifecycle.service.ts:11 defines ORDER_STATUSES as the bare identifiers `["new", "processing", "preparation", "out_for_delivery", "completed", "cancelled", "rejected", "returned"]`. Inside the transition transaction, line 247 takes `const fromStatus = orderStatus(current.status)` (that raw string) and line 262 passes `fromStatus, toStatus` to enqueueOrderStatusChanged. phase1-backend/src/notifications/notification-outbox.service.ts:129 stores them verbatim: `const payload = { orderNumber, fromStatus: input.fromStatus, toStatus: input.toStatus, storeName }`, and lines 132/135 write both the merchant and the customer row from that same payload with no per-audience wording. phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:91-94 renders it: `body: `تغيرت حالة الطلب ${orderNumber} في ${storeName} من ${String(payload.fromStatus ?? "")} إلى ${String(payload.toStatus ?? "")}.`` — no lookup table, no branch on recipientKind. That body reaches the provider unchanged at line 455. The translation exists everywhere except here: grepping phase1-backend/src for «قيد التوصيل» / «قيد التجهيز» returns nothing, while the client holds five copies of the map — client/src/components/OrderWorkspace.tsx:72, client/src/pages/StorefrontTrackOrderPage.tsx:18, client/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailPage.tsx:11, client/src/pages/CustomerOrdersPage.tsx:10, client/src/components/MerchantHome.tsx:33. Compare the neighbouring branch at outbox-worker.ts:96-104, which renders the same transition's courier notice in full Arabic.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add an Arabic status map beside renderMessage (mirroring client/src/pages/StorefrontTrackOrderPage.tsx:18) and interpolate it: `من ${STATUS_AR[fromStatus] ?? fromStatus} إلى ${STATUS_AR[toStatus] ?? toStatus}` — falling back to the raw code only for a status the map has not caught up with, so a new status degrades to today's behaviour instead of an empty string.

### 11-15-43 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:304`

**الادّعاء:** A notification whose message cannot be rendered escapes the worker's only try/catch: it aborts the whole batch, is never retried and never dead-lettered, and re-poisons the queue every 5 minutes forever while both health alarms stay silent.

**الأثر المزعوم:** The one message on this platform that a locked-out merchant depends on — «استعادة كلمة السر في Codiq» — is the one message whose render can throw, and a throw there is unrecoverable and invisible. Trigger: rotate CODIQ_ENCRYPTION_KEY without setting CODIQ_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS. secret-box.ts:53-58 documents that rotation as the routine procedure, and the reset rows queued at that moment (each one a person who just pressed «نسيت كلمة السر») become permanent poison pills. He never gets a link, sees no error, and there is no support desk above the platform. Nobody is told either: the row never reaches `dead_letter`, so evaluateHealth's dead-letter alarm cannot fire, and its nextAttemptAt is rewritten to `now` on every recovery cycle so it is never the 15 minutes overdue the stall alarm needs — platform-health.service.ts:96-101 is structurally blind to this state. The damage is not confined to that row: every pass that claims it dies at it, so the up-to-19 other rows claimed in the same batch — «طلب جديد», the trigger for the COD confirmation call — are left in `processing` and wait out the full 5-minute stale window before anyone retries them, and this recurs every cycle for the life of the deployment. retention.ts:33-36 sweeps only `sent`, `skipped` and `dead_letter`, so a poison row never leaves the queue by any path. This also falsifies the phase-15 acceptance claim: «the worker processes and retries on an induced failure» holds only for a failure induced at the provider.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The claim: processOutboxBatch claims a batch of up to 20 rows with a token-stamped updateMany (outbox-worker.ts:283-286) and reads them back (outbox-worker.ts:287). Each claimed row is now status `processing` with claimedAt = now. 2. The unprotected stage: for each row the message is rendered at outbox-worker.ts:304 — `const message = renderMessage(await withStockForecast(row));` — with no try around it. The only try/catch in the loop is outbox-worker.ts:445-458, and it wraps the provider call alone. 3. renderMessage can throw. Its `auth.password_reset` branch calls `openSecret("password-reset-link", String(payload.resetUrlSealed ?? ""))` at outbox-worker.ts:129, and openSecret throws on an undeclared key id (secret-box.ts:252-258) and on a failed GCM tag (secret-box.ts:266-274). A second latent site sits in the `trial.ending` branch: outbox-worker.ts:153-154 does `new Date(String(payload.endsAt)).toISOString()`, and an Invalid Date is truthy, so a bad `endsAt` throws RangeError there too. 4. The throw leaves processOutboxBatch entirely and is swallowed by the loop's catch at outbox-worker.ts:504 (`logger.error("job.failed", …)`). No `notificationOutbox.update` runs for that row, nor for any row the loop had not yet reached. 5. Nothing repairs it: the stale sweep at outbox-worker.ts:257-260 sets status `retry_scheduled`, nextAttemptAt = now, lastOutcome `stale_claim_recovered` — and deliberately does not touch attemptCount, so the row can never reach OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS at outbox-worker.ts:473 and can never become `dead_letter`. It is re-claimed on the next pass (ordering at outbox-worker.ts:275 puts nextAttemptAt = now at the head) and throws again. 6. The alarms cannot see it: platform-health.service.ts:96-98 counts only `status: "dead_letter"`; platform-health.service.ts:99-101 counts only `status in (pending, retry_scheduled)` with nextAttemptAt older than 15 minutes. The poison row is `processing` for ~5 minutes, then `retry_scheduled` with nextAttemptAt = now for ~5 seconds. Neither predicate ever matches. 7. The code asserts the opposite of its own behaviour at outbox-worker.ts:120-126: «openSecret throws, the worker's catch turns that into a retry and then a dead letter, which is the correct ending». There is no such catch.

**العلاج المقترح:** Put the render/compliance stage inside the same failure ladder as the send. Wrap outbox-worker.ts:304 (and the rest of the pre-send block) in a try that produces `outcome = { ok: false, outcome: \`render_failed:${error.name}\` }` and falls through to the existing attemptCount/backoff/dead-letter branch at outbox-worker.ts:472-480 — one row's bad payload then costs one row, ends at `dead_letter` after OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS, and becomes visible to evaluateHealth instead of silently cycling. Separately, make the loop body per-row fault-isolated so no single row can abort a batch that has already claimed nineteen others.

### 11-15-44 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:304`

**الادّعاء:** Message rendering and classification run outside the worker's only try/catch, so one row that throws aborts the whole batch, never increments attemptCount, never dead-letters, and re-poisons every later pass — halting notifications for every store

**الأثر المزعوم:** One unrenderable row stops the platform's entire messaging spine: no order confirmations, no «طلب جديد» merchant alerts (the trigger for the COD confirmation call), no password-reset links — for every merchant on the platform, indefinitely, with only a `job.failed` line to show for it. Rows claimed alongside the poison row in the same batch are stranded in `processing` for 5 minutes per pass. The retry/dead-letter ladder cannot end it because the poison row's `attemptCount` is never touched. The module's own comment asserts the opposite outcome, so nobody would look here.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) `outbox-worker.ts:290` `for (const row of claimed) {` — the ONLY try/catch in the loop is `:445-458`, wrapping the provider call alone. 2) `:304` `const message = renderMessage(await withStockForecast(row));` and `:305` `const kind = await notificationKind(row.eventType);` sit before it, unguarded. 3) `renderMessage` at `:129` calls `openSecret("password-reset-link", ...)`, which throws by design — `secret-box.ts:253-258` throws `no key with id ... is declared` when the sealing key is neither CURRENT nor PREVIOUS (an encryption-key rotation without `CODIQ_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS`), and `:273` throws when the tag does not verify. `:154` `endsAt.toISOString()` on a `trial.ending` payload whose `endsAt` does not parse throws RangeError the same way. 4) The throw escapes `processOutboxBatch` entirely; `startOutboxWorker:498-505` only logs it (`.catch((error) => logger.error("job.failed", ...))`). 5) The row is left `status='processing'` with its claim token; `:257-260` recovers it after `STALE_CLAIM_RECOVERY_MS` (5 min) to `retry_scheduled` with `nextAttemptAt: now` and — deliberately — no `attemptCount` change, so `:473` `attemptCount >= OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS` can never fire and `:275`'s `nextAttemptAt: "asc"` ordering puts it back at the head of the next batch. 6) `outbox-worker.ts:122-127` claims the opposite: "`openSecret` throws, the worker's catch turns that into a retry and then a dead letter, which is the correct ending".

**العلاج المقترح:** Move the `try` up to the top of the loop body so it encloses `withStockForecast`, `renderMessage`, `notificationKind` and the compliance gate — a render or classification failure then joins the same `attemptCount`/backoff/dead-letter ladder a provider failure gets. Also increment `attemptCount` in the stale-claim recovery at `:257-260` so a row that repeatedly kills the worker eventually reaches `dead_letter` instead of looping forever.

### 11-15-45 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:467`

**الادّعاء:** The worker stamps a claim token but never checks it on write-back, so a send that outlives the 5-minute stale-claim window is re-sent by a second worker and the first worker's update then clobbers the result

**الأثر المزعوم:** The shopper receives the same «تأكيد طلبك» (or password-reset link) twice, and the row is left describing a state that never happened: the late writer flips a `sent` row back to `retry_scheduled` while leaving `sentAt` populated, so the merchant's notification log shows a send time on a message it reports as unsent, the row is re-claimed and sent a third time, and the store/recipient daily counters (which filter on `status: "sent"`) under-count what actually reached the inbox. The module header at `:3-4` states this cannot happen.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) `:283-286` claims rows with `updateMany({ where: { id: { in: ... }, status: { in: CLAIMABLE_STATUSES }, nextAttemptAt: { lte: now } }, data: { status: "processing", claimToken, claimedAt: now } })` — atomic and token-stamped, as advertised. 2) Every terminal write-back drops the token from the predicate: `:292, :297, :355, :374, :381, :390, :402, :412, :428, :438, :467, :474, :478` all use `where: { id: row.id }` only. 3) `:257-260` returns any row held in `processing` longer than `STALE_CLAIM_RECOVERY_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000` (`:44`) to the queue. 4) A send can exceed that window on the SMTP transport: `notification-provider.ts:290-294` builds the nodemailer transport with only `host`, `tls`, `port`, `secure` and `auth` — no `socketTimeout`, so nodemailer's 10-minute default applies (the HTTP provider is safe, `:171` `AbortSignal.timeout(20_000)`), and the `dns.lookup` at `:289` has no deadline either. 5) Two workers co-exist in normal operation: `server/index.ts:21-43` spawns one worker child per HTTP server process and respawns it on exit, so any rolling deploy or overlapping restart runs two. Worker A claims row R at T0 and hangs in `sendMail`; worker B's sweep at T0+5min re-queues and re-claims R, sends it, writes `sent`; A's socket times out at T0+10min and `:478` writes `status: "retry_scheduled", attemptCount: row.attemptCount + 1` over B's result using the stale `row` it read at `:287`.

**العلاج المقترح:** Gate every terminal write on the claim: `updateMany({ where: { id: row.id, claimToken }, data: ... })` and treat `count === 0` as "this claim was revoked — write nothing". Also set an explicit `socketTimeout`/`connectionTimeout` on the nodemailer transport at `notification-provider.ts:290` comfortably below `STALE_CLAIM_RECOVERY_MS`, matching the 20s deadline the HTTP provider already carries.

### 11-15-46 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:93`

**الادّعاء:** The shopper's Arabic order-status email interpolates the raw English status enum — «تغيرت حالة الطلب 1023 في متجر النور من preparation إلى out_for_delivery» — while every screen in the product translates the same values into Arabic

**الأثر المزعوم:** This is the message an Iraqi shopper meets in his inbox, and the two words that carry all the meaning are in a language he did not choose and a shape no human writes: `out_for_delivery`, `preparation`, `new`. It reads as a system dump, not as his shop talking to him — and on the one transition he actually cares about it arrives side by side with a message that gets it right, because `preparation → out_for_delivery` fires BOTH this row and the polished CD-1b «مندوبك في الطريق — الطلب 1023 … خرج طلبك من متجر النور مع مندوب التوصيل» (outbox-worker.ts:96-104). The contrast is the proof the platform can say it in Arabic. It is also sent for transitions that mean nothing to a shopper at all (`new → processing`), so what he gets is a noisy line of English tokens with no tracking link on it.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The renderer interpolates the payload values verbatim: `return { subject: \`تحديث حالة الطلب ${orderNumber}\`, body: \`تغيرت حالة الطلب ${orderNumber} في ${storeName} من ${String(payload.fromStatus ?? "")} إلى ${String(payload.toStatus ?? "")}.\` };` — phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:93. 2. The payload holds raw enum strings, untranslated: `const payload = { orderNumber: input.orderNumber, fromStatus: input.fromStatus, toStatus: input.toStatus, storeName: policy.storeName };` — notification-outbox.service.ts:129, and the customer row is created from it at :135. 3. Those inputs are the machine statuses: `export const ORDER_STATUSES = ["new", "processing", "preparation", "out_for_delivery", "completed", "cancelled", "rejected", "returned"]` — order-lifecycle.service.ts:11, handed straight in by `transitionOrderStatus`: `await enqueueOrderStatusChanged(tx, { ..., fromStatus, toStatus, customerEmail: current.customerEmail, actorKind: actor.kind })` — order-lifecycle.service.ts:262, where `fromStatus = orderStatus(current.status)` (:247) and `toStatus` is the validated enum (:248). 4. The Arabic labels exist in at least six places on the client, including the two pages the same shopper sees: `const statusLabel: Record<string, string> = { new: "جديد", processing: "قيد المعالجة", preparation: "قيد التجهيز", out_for_delivery: "خرج للتوصيل", completed: "مكتمل", ... }` — client/src/pages/StorefrontTrackOrderPage.tsx:18 and client/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailPage.tsx:11 (also MerchantHome.tsx:33, OrderWorkspace.tsx:72, MerchantAnalytics.tsx:38, MerchantCommandPalette.tsx:39). 5. Nothing pins the wording: grepping the test tree for «تغيرت حالة الطلب» returns no hits, so the body has never been asserted.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add the label map next to `renderMessage` and use it in the `order.status_changed` branch, falling back to the raw token so an unmapped status still renders: `const AR_STATUS: Record<string,string> = { new: "جديد", processing: "قيد المعالجة", preparation: "قيد التجهيز", out_for_delivery: "خرج للتوصيل", completed: "مكتمل", cancelled: "ملغي", rejected: "مرفوض", returned: "مرتجع" };` then `من ${AR_STATUS[from] ?? from} إلى ${AR_STATUS[to] ?? to}`. Translate at RENDER time, not at enqueue time, so the 90 days of rows already in `notification_outbox` are fixed too. While there, hang the tracking link on it with the existing helper — `linkLine("تابع حالة طلبك من هنا:", payload.trackingUrl)` (outbox-worker.ts:75) — which `order.courier_dispatched` already uses.

### 11-15-47 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:291`

**الادّعاء:** When no mail provider is configured the worker marks every claimed row `skipped` — a terminal state with no requeue path anywhere — so a boot without the mail key destroys the queue instead of holding it, and the health watcher cannot see it

**الأثر المزعوم:** The provider is resolved once, at worker boot. If `CODIQ_RESEND_API_KEY`/`CODIQ_MAIL_FROM` are missing or mistyped on a deploy, the loop keeps running and drains the queue at 20 rows every 5 seconds straight into `skipped` — every «طلب جديد», every «تأكيد طلبك», every «مندوبك في الطريق», every password-reset link, permanently. Fixing the variable an hour later brings back nothing: no route, job or script re-queues a skipped row, and the rows are deleted at 90 days. This is the module's own rule — «DEFERRED, NEVER DROPPED … a cap that silently discards is worse than no cap, because the merchant sees a send that did not happen» (messaging-compliance.service.ts:17-19) — broken by the one branch above the caps. And it is invisible where it must be loud: the platform-owner health watcher counts only `dead_letter` and overdue `pending`/`retry_scheduled`, and a burned queue is neither, so nobody is woken; the merchant's only signal is a «تخطي» badge on a log page he has no reason to open.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. Terminal skip, before any other gate: `if (!provider) { await prisma.notificationOutbox.update({ where: { id: row.id }, data: { status: "skipped", lastOutcome: "no_provider_configured", claimToken: null, claimedAt: null } }); result.skipped += 1; continue; }` — phase1-backend/src/notifications/outbox-worker.ts:291-295. `attemptCount` and `nextAttemptAt` are untouched and `skipped` is not in `CLAIMABLE_STATUSES = ["pending", "retry_scheduled"]` (:45), so the claim query at :273-274 can never see the row again. 2. The provider is a boot-time constant: `const provider = resolveNotificationProvider();` then `startOutboxWorker(provider, ...)` — worker-entry.ts:27-29; it returns null when neither `httpMailSettingsFromEnv` nor `smtpSettingsFromEnv` finds its variables (notification-provider.ts:357-361). 3. No requeue exists: grepping `outbox|notificationOutbox` across phase1-backend/src/http returns no matches, and the only service reader is `listNotificationLog` — notification-settings.service.ts:194, a read. 4. The owner is not told: `evaluateHealth` counts `status: "dead_letter"` in the last 24h and `status: { in: ["pending", "retry_scheduled"] }` past due — platform-health.service.ts:95-102 — and `HEALTH_ALERT_KINDS = ["outbox_dead_letters", "outbox_stalled"]` (:67). A queue draining into `skipped` is not stalled and produces no dead letters. 5. The evidence is then swept: `notificationOutboxSettled: 90` covers rows that reached `sent` or `skipped` — retention.ts:34.

**العلاج المقترح:** Treat a missing provider the way the caps treat a full one: hold, don't discard. Replace the terminal update with a short deferral that leaves the retry budget alone — `data: { status: "retry_scheduled", nextAttemptAt: new Date(now.valueOf() + 60_000), lastOutcome: "no_provider_configured", claimToken: null, claimedAt: null }` — so the rows are still there when the variable is fixed, and `outbox_stalled` fires at platform-health.service.ts:111 after 15 minutes, which is exactly the alert this case needs. Keep the terminal skip only for the deliberate case: `demoModeFromEnv` returns null on purpose (notification-provider.ts:351) and demo-mode.integration.mjs:389 asserts `skipped/no_provider_configured`, so branch on demo mode rather than on `provider === null`.

### 11-15-48 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/notifications/restock-scanner.ts:104`

**الادّعاء:** A shopper who re-arms «نبّهني عند التوفر» on a wishlist item he already owns is never notified again, and the restock scanner retries his stuck row every 10 minutes for 90 days

**الأثر المزعوم:** The shopper taps the toggle, the storefront shows it on, and no alert ever comes — the one feature whose entire promise is "we will tell you". It self-heals only when retention hard-deletes the old outbox row at 90 days. Meanwhile every stuck wishlist row costs one doomed transaction per scanner pass (144/day each), and these rows accumulate permanently because the candidate set only drains when a message is actually enqueued — the exact failure the module header at `restock-scanner.ts:70-73` says it excluded phone-only customers to avoid.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) `restock-scanner.ts:97-113` inserts with `dedupeKey: `back_in_stock:${item.id}`` where `item.id` is the WISHLIST ROW id, and `:114` clears `notifyOnRestock` in the same `prisma.$transaction` opened at `:96`. 2) The wishlist row is never deleted, only unflagged. 3) `wishlist.service.ts:92-103`: `addToWishlist` on an existing (storeId, customerId, variantId) does `prisma.wishlistItem.update({ where: { id: existing.id }, data: { notifyOnRestock: ... } })` — it re-arms the SAME row id. 4) `client/src/pages/CustomerWishlistPage.tsx:59-61` wires a checkbox (`checked={item.notifyOnRestock}`, `:116`) straight to `addToWishlist`, so re-arming in place is the only UI path there is. 5) Next restock: the row matches the candidate filter at `restock-scanner.ts:52-86`, the create at `:97` violates `notification_outbox_dedupe` (prisma/migrations/20260825120000_phase15_notification_outbox/migration.sql:35), the transaction rolls back so `notifyOnRestock` is NOT cleared, and `:119` counts it as `duplicates` with no per-item log. 6) `retention.ts:34` `notificationOutboxSettled: 90` is the only thing that ever breaks the loop. The header at `:22-26` claims the row-id key "is what lets him ask AGAIN: a new row has a new id, so removing the item and re-adding it works" — true only for delete-then-add, which the UI never does.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the key name the ASK, not the row: add an `armedAt` (or `notifyRequestedAt`) column set by `addToWishlist` whenever it flips `notifyOnRestock` to true, and key on `back_in_stock:{wishlistItemId}:{armedAt.toISOString()}`. Alternatively clear the flag in its own committed statement before the enqueue so a collision cannot leave the candidate armed forever.

### 11-15-49 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:121`

**الادّعاء:** The per-merchant store cap is a check-then-insert with no lock, so concurrent POST /merchant/stores requests all read the same count and every one of them succeeds past the plan limit

**الأثر المزعوم:** The phase promised a per-merchant store cap, "later replaced by plan limits", and `assertWithinPlanLimit` carries a comment asserting the guarantee it does not deliver — "Runs inside the caller's transaction when one is provided so the check and the write see the same snapshot". Under PostgreSQL's default READ COMMITTED isolation two concurrent transactions do NOT see each other's uncommitted inserts, so N parallel creation requests from one merchant each count the same starting number of stores, each pass the limit test, and each commit a store. A merchant on the free plan (3 stores) can hold 5 or 8 by firing the same request from a browser console a few times in parallel; nothing downstream re-checks, because the cap exists only in this application-level count — there is no unique index, partial index or trigger on the store table that bounds stores-per-merchant. Every extra store is a full storefront with its own catalogue, its own COD orders and its own share of the image quota, taken without the plan upgrade that is supposed to pay for it. The same defect rides `max_products`, `max_staff` and `max_image_storage_mb` through the same function, so the whole entitlement ceiling is advisory under concurrency.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:119 opens `prisma.$transaction(async (transaction) => {` with NO `isolationLevel` option. 2. Line 121 `await assertWithinPlanLimit(transaction, merchantId, "max_stores");` 3. phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:113 `const usage = await merchantUsage(client, merchantId);` → entitlement.service.ts:68 `client.store.count({ where: { merchantId } })` — a plain count, no `FOR UPDATE`, no advisory lock. 4. entitlement.service.ts:114 `if (usage[feature] + increment > limit)` is the entire ceiling. 5. Back in merchant-store.service.ts:126 the insert happens: `createStoreFoundation({ merchantId, name, slug, defaultSubdomain: slug }, transaction)` → store-bootstrap.service.ts:28 `client.store.create({…})`. Nothing between the count and the insert serializes the two. 6. phase1-backend/src/db/prisma.ts:7 `export const prisma = new PrismaClient();` — no transaction options are configured globally, so the default isolation applies. 7. The comment claiming the snapshot guarantee is at entitlement.service.ts:107-109. 8. The uniqueness that IS enforced in the database is only on identity, not on count: prisma/schema.prisma:159 `slug String @unique` and :161 `defaultSubdomain String @unique`. 9. The suite only tests the sequential path — phase1-backend/tests/phase13.store-switcher.integration.ts:136-153 creates store #3, then store #4, one after the other, and asserts 403 PLAN_LIMIT_EXCEEDED; it never fires two creations at once, so the acceptance test is weaker than the criterion it stands for.

**العلاج المقترح:** Serialize the count and the insert on the merchant row inside the same transaction. The cheapest correct change is a per-merchant advisory lock taken at the top of the transaction in `createMerchantStore` before line 121 — `await transaction.$executeRaw`SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(${merchantId}, 0))`` — which the platform already uses for the category-cycle guard; alternatively pass `{ isolationLevel: "Serializable" }` to the `$transaction` at merchant-store.service.ts:119 and retry on 40001. Either way, correct the false snapshot claim in the comment at entitlement.service.ts:107-109.

### 11-15-50 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:121`

**الادّعاء:** `createMerchantStore` checks `max_stores` with a count-then-insert inside a READ COMMITTED transaction, so N concurrent creates all pass and the plan cap is bypassed by N stores

**الأثر المزعوم:** Phase 13's cap — "a temporary per-merchant store cap later replaced by plan limits" — is the thing this route exists to enforce, and it is defeated by a burst. A merchant one below his ceiling (free plan = 3 stores, sitting at 2) who fires 50 parallel `POST /merchant/stores` with 50 distinct slugs ends up with 52 stores: every request reads `stores = 2`, every request computes `2 + 1 <= 3` and passes, every request inserts. Nothing else stops the burst — the route carries no rate limit of any kind, unlike every storefront write. The cost is not one extra row: each store is a full public storefront with a globally-unique, permanent slug (`slug @unique`, no rename path), so this is also the cheapest bulk slug-squat on the platform, and each `createStoreFoundation` call writes ~20 extra rows (one per governorate delivery rate, plus payment method and notification settings), so 50 requests amplify into ~1000 inserts. On a platform whose plan limits are the upgrade lever (B-08), the cap that gates the upgrade is the cap that does not hold.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:56-64 — `POST /merchant/stores`: `requireTrustedWriteOrigin`, `requireMerchantSession`, parse, `createMerchantStore`. No `limitStorefrontWrite`, no `limitUpload`, no ceiling of any kind. 2. phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:119-128 — `prisma.$transaction(async (transaction) => { await assertWithinPlanLimit(transaction, merchantId, "max_stores"); ... createStoreFoundation(...) })`, called with NO `isolationLevel` option, so it runs at the Postgres default READ COMMITTED. 3. phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:110-121 — `assertWithinPlanLimit` reads the plan, then `merchantUsage`, then compares `usage[feature] + increment > limit`. The usage read is phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:68, `client.store.count({ where: { merchantId } })` — a plain count, no `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, and no lock is taken on the merchant or subscription row anywhere in the transaction. Concurrent transactions therefore all see the same pre-burst count and all pass. 4. The slug half of the same check-then-act IS safe and shows what is missing here: merchant-store.service.ts:122-125 pre-checks `store.findUnique({ where: { slug } })`, but the database backs it with `slug @unique` (prisma/schema.prisma:159) and a lost race surfaces as a clean 409 via phase1-backend/src/http/errors.ts:200. There is no equivalent database constraint on the per-merchant store COUNT, so nothing catches the lost race. 5. The codebase already owns the tool it did not use here: `withSerializableRetry` at phase1-backend/src/db/serializable-retry.ts:64, applied to the two paths where a count-then-write actually matters today — phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:561 and :1008, and phase1-backend/src/services/pos.service.ts:431, all with `isolationLevel: Prisma.TransactionIsolationLevel.Serializable`.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the store-creation transaction serialize on the merchant. The smallest correct change is to run it at `Prisma.TransactionIsolationLevel.Serializable` under `withSerializableRetry` (phase1-backend/src/db/serializable-retry.ts:64), exactly as storefront.service.ts:561 and pos.service.ts:431 do — the count and the insert then conflict and the loser retries and is refused with the real PLAN_LIMIT_EXCEEDED. A cheaper alternative that does not change isolation: take an advisory lock on the merchant id (`SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(merchantId, 0))`) as the first statement inside the transaction at merchant-store.service.ts:120, the same idiom the category-cycle guard already uses per store.

### 11-15-51 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:122`

**الادّعاء:** Store creation commits before the session is minted and has no replay branch, so a lost response makes the retry answer 409 «الرابط مستخدم» about the merchant's own store — and he can never delete the one he just made

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant on an Iraqi mobile connection submits «إنشاء المتجر». The transaction commits the store, and the response is lost (dropped connection, or `createTypedSession` failing on the separate write that follows the commit). He sees a failure, retries with the same slug, and is answered 409 with «رابط المتجر مستخدم بالفعل. اختر رابطاً مختلفاً.» — a sentence that tells him someone else owns it. He obeys, picks `matjari-2`, and now owns two stores, one of them a ghost. On the free plan that is 2 of his 3 forever: there is no store delete route on any surface (merchant, staff or platform owner), no store deactivate, and the slug is deliberately immutable (merchant-store.service.ts:62, «the slug is a public identity and never changes here»), so neither he nor the platform owner can undo it, and the platform-global slug he wanted is burned by his own dead row.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. phase1-backend/src/http/routes/merchant.ts:60 — `createMerchantStore` returns after its transaction has committed; :61 `createTypedSession(...)` and :62 `setWebSession(...)` are separate writes outside it, so a failure there answers 500 with the store already persisted. 2. phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:119-128 — inside the transaction, :122 `transaction.store.findUnique({ where: { slug }, select: { id: true } })` selects the id ONLY, so the code cannot tell whether the occupying store belongs to this merchant; :123-125 throws 409 DUPLICATE_STORE_SLUG unconditionally. 3. client/src/components/StoresManager.tsx:125-135 — on any failure the form only calls `setFormError`; there is no idempotency key, no re-check of `GET /merchant/stores`, and the button re-enables for an identical resubmission. :22 hard-codes the 409 into «رابط المتجر مستخدم بالفعل. اختر رابطاً مختلفاً.» 4. `app.delete(` across phase1-backend/src/http/routes matches coupons, categories, products, variants, team, banners, logo, addresses, wishlist, reviews, cart items, platform staff and sessions — no store; `store.delete` appears nowhere in phase1-backend/src. Store `isActive` is written only by registration.service.ts:97/:137 and merchant-suspension.service.ts:116, all merchant-wide. 5. The codebase already treats this exact hazard as real elsewhere: phase1-backend/src/http/shared.ts:136-146 describes the idempotent checkout REPLAY built for «a shopper on a 3G connection who taps «تأكيد الطلب», loses the response, and taps again».

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the retry a replay instead of a lie. At merchant-store.service.ts:122 select `merchantId` alongside `id`; when the occupying store is this merchant's own, return it as the result of the call rather than throwing — the route then mints the session for it and answers 200 with the same body, so the second submit lands the merchant on the store he already created. Keep the 409 only when the slug belongs to another merchant.

### 11-15-52 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:30`

**الادّعاء:** A refused store link always blames the characters, even when every character was legal and the real rule broken was length or a leading/trailing dash — and the hint that states the length rule is itself wrong

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant transliterating an Iraqi shop name writes something like `matjar-al-noor-lil-adawat-al-manzilia-wa-al-kahrabaiya` (53 characters), or leaves the trailing dash he was still typing. Every character he used is a lowercase Latin letter, a digit or a dash — exactly what the form asked for — and the platform answers «استخدم أحرفاً إنجليزية صغيرة وأرقاماً وشرطات فقط في رابط المتجر.» He reads it, sees that his characters are already lowercase Latin, and retypes the same thing; the message will never tell him the rule he actually broke. The one place the length appears is a grey hint that is itself untrue: it says «من 3 إلى 50 حرفاً» while the pattern accepts a single character and refuses two. This is the platform's own rule being charged to the merchant's typing.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The pattern is `/^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]{1,48}[a-z0-9])?$/` — phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:8 — which encodes three separate rules: the character set, a total length of 1 or 3-50 (the optional group makes 2 impossible and 1 legal), and no leading or trailing dash. 2. All three failures produce one message about characters only: `throw new HttpError(422, "INVALID_STORE_SLUG", "يجب أن يكون رابط المتجر بأحرف إنجليزية صغيرة وأرقام وشرطات فقط.")` — phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:29-30. 3. The merchant usually never reaches the server, because both clients pre-check with the identical pattern and an equally character-only sentence: client/src/components/StoresManager.tsx:18 and :122 («استخدم أحرفاً إنجليزية صغيرة وأرقاماً وشرطات فقط في رابط المتجر.»), client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:7 and :92, plus the same text mapped for the server code at StoresManager.tsx:23 and MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:12. 4. The only statement of the length rule is the hint at client/src/components/StoresManager.tsx:150 and client/src/components/MerchantStoreSwitcher.tsx:135, «من 3 إلى 50 حرفاً», which contradicts the pattern's acceptance of a 1-character slug.

**العلاج المقترح:** Split the refusal by cause in `normalizedStoreSlug` (phase1-backend/src/services/merchant-store.service.ts:28-37) and mirror it in both client pre-checks: length → «رابط المتجر من 3 إلى 50 حرفاً.», edges → «لا يبدأ رابط المتجر ولا ينتهي بشرطة.», character set → the existing sentence. Change the pattern's minimum to a real 3 (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{1,48}[a-z0-9]$`) so the hint the merchant reads is true.

### 11-15-53 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/return-request.service.ts:208`

**الادّعاء:** Approving a return runs the order transition and the request flip as two separate transactions with no compare-and-swap, so a failure between them strands a request that can never be approved again

**الأثر المزعوم:** `transitionOrderStatus` commits the whole of the return — the order moves to `returned`, the commission ledger entry is reversed, loyalty points are clawed back and refunded, and the goods are queued into the merchant's inspection list. Only then does a second, independent transaction mark the return request `approved`. If that second transaction does not commit — a pool timeout, a dropped connection, a worker restart, or the audit insert inside it failing — the request stays `requested` while the money has already moved. The merchant reopens «طلبات الإرجاع», still sees the request open, and taps «موافقة» again: the order is now `returned`, its version has advanced to exactly what the fresh read reports, so the version check passes and the state machine refuses the terminal edge with a raw English 422 «returned cannot transition to returned». That request can never be approved. His only remaining button is «رفض», which requires a reason note the customer reads — writing a rejection, with an explanation, onto a return the shop already accepted, refunded and physically took back. The customer's returns page meanwhile says «قيد المراجعة» forever on an order that was returned days ago.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/return-request.service.ts:189-192 reads the request and the order outside any transaction; :194 checks `request.status !== "requested"` on that unlocked snapshot. :201-205 then calls `transitionOrderStatus(storeId, actor, request.orderId, { toStatus: "returned", expectedVersion: request.order.version, ... })`, whose own transaction commits everything: phase1-backend/src/services/order-lifecycle.service.ts:256 flips the order and bumps `version`, :259 `settleStorefrontReservations` stamps the reservations `pending_inspection`, :303 `recordOrderReturn` reverses the ledger, :306 `reverseLoyaltyForReturn` moves the points. Control returns to return-request.service.ts:208, which opens a SECOND `prisma.$transaction` and at :209 writes `tx.returnRequest.update({ where: { id: request.id }, data: { status: ... } })` — keyed on id alone, with no `status: "requested"` predicate and no affected-count assertion, and with `recordAuditEvent` (:220) sharing its fate. When that transaction is lost, the retry dies at order-lifecycle.service.ts:248 because :22 declares `returned: []`, so `transitions["returned"].includes("returned")` is false. The correct idiom already exists two files away: phase1-backend/src/services/return-inspection.service.ts:163-175 does the guarded `updateMany` on the disposition first and throws 409 when `flipped.count !== 1`.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the decision re-entrant and guarded. Before :201, treat `request.order.status === "returned"` as "the transition for this request already committed" and skip `transitionOrderStatus` instead of calling it into a terminal edge; and replace the bare `update` at :209 with `updateMany({ where: { id: request.id, storeId, status: "requested" }, data: {...} })` plus an assertion that the count is 1, exactly as `resolveReturnInspection` does — so a second attempt closes the row rather than throwing 422, and two concurrent deciders cannot both write a decision.

### 11-15-54 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:107`

**الادّعاء:** The merchant types his employee's password and nothing ever makes it the employee's: the staff shell has no security screen, no flag marks the credential provisional, and the audit log still names the employee for everything done with it

**الأثر المزعوم:** Phase 14 promised «an independent staff identity». The session layer is independent; the credential is not. The merchant chooses `temporaryPassword` at creation, nothing marks it as somebody else's choice, and the staff shell is the only one of the five identities with no way to change it from inside — the employee's sole route is the email reset link on the login page, which needs a mail provider to be configured and an inbox the employee actually reads. So the shop owner keeps a working key to his cashier's identity indefinitely, and every order transition, till ticket and inventory movement made with it is written to the audit log as the cashier — phase 14's own suite asserts precisely that attribution. When 50,000 IQD is missing from the till on Thursday, the activity log names a person whose password his employer also knows, and it cannot settle the question. The platform already reasoned this through for merchants and built the fix; it was not extended to staff, whose identity is additionally global — an existing Staff row is attached by email to any store whose merchant types that address, so the password one merchant chose keeps working wherever that identity is later added.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:107-111 hashes the merchant-supplied password and creates the Staff row with no provisioner mark; contrast phase1-backend/src/services/manual-provisioning.service.ts:42, which stamps `passwordSetByProvisionerAt` on a merchant created the same way, and phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:131-138 + :246, where `assertMerchantOwnsHisPassword` refuses every merchant surface until he replaces it — `requireStoreStaff` (staff-permissions.ts:155-196) has no equivalent call. The change path exists server-side for staff: phase1-backend/src/services/password.service.ts:38-46 returns the `staff` delegate and phase1-backend/src/http/routes/account-security.ts:22 is identity-generic. It is only the surface that is missing: client/src/config/navigation.ts:350-364 has no account/security entry, and the branch table at client/src/pages/StaffDashboard.tsx:141-180 never renders AccountSecurityPanel — compare client/src/pages/OwnerDashboard.tsx:251, client/src/pages/MerchantDashboard.tsx:224 and client/src/pages/PlatformStaffDashboard.tsx:281, which all do. The attribution that this undermines is asserted at phase1-backend/tests/phase14.staff-permissions.integration.ts:267-271 (`actorKind === "staff"`, `actorId === basicStaffId`). The identity is global: phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:92-101 looks Staff up by email across the whole platform and attaches a membership to the existing row.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add a `passwordSetByProvisionerAt` column to `staff`, set it in staff.service.ts:109, clear it in password.service.ts:112-114 alongside the merchant branch, and refuse staff surfaces from `requireStoreStaff` with the same named 403 the merchant gets — then add an «الأمان» entry to staffNavEntries and a branch in StaffDashboard.tsx rendering `<AccountSecurityPanel view="password" />`, which is the one action that must stay reachable while the refusal stands.

### 11-15-55 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:92`

**الادّعاء:** `POST /merchant/stores/:storeSlug/team` answers 201-with-the-person's-name for an email that is already a staff account and 400 for one that is not, giving any merchant a free, unlimited oracle for «is this address a CODIQ staff member» plus that member's real display name

**الأثر المزعوم:** `prisma.staff.findUnique({ where: { email } })` is global — `Staff.email` is unique across the whole platform, not per store. A merchant probing with `{ email, roleCode }` and no `displayName` gets two perfectly distinguishable answers: 400 INVALID_DISPLAY_NAME when the address is not a staff account (nothing written, no plan seat spent, so the probe is free and repeatable), and 201 when it is — carrying that person's `displayName` and global `isActive`, and silently attaching him to the prober's store. There is no rate limit on the route. This re-opens by the front door exactly the question the sign-in door was hardened to refuse: `loginStaff` runs an unconditional bcrypt compare against `ABSENT_IDENTITY_HASH` because «a sweep over candidate addresses at this door used to sort «is a staff member on CODIQ» from «is not» in one request each, and hand that list to a password-guessing run». A merchant account is cheap, and pairing this oracle with finding #1 (a credential the employer chose and the employee cannot change) turns a list of confirmed staff addresses into a targeted list.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) The route requires only `team.manage` on the caller's own store and applies no ceiling: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/team.ts:22-26. 2) The lookup is platform-wide: phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:92; `Staff.email` is `@unique` at prisma/schema.prisma:950. 3) Positive branch — 201 plus the membership payload: staff.service.ts:94-102, whose `membershipSelect` carries `staff: { select: { id, email, displayName, isActive } }` at staff.service.ts:23. 4) Negative branch — the displayName check runs only AFTER the lookup, so it fires exclusively for an unknown address: staff.service.ts:104-106. Nothing is written on that path, so a negative probe costs neither a row nor a plan seat (`assertWithinPlanLimit` at :91 charges only when a seat is actually taken on the positive branch). 5) The defence this bypasses: phase1-backend/src/auth/auth.service.ts:230-237 with the reasoning at :231-234, and the shared constant's rationale at phase1-backend/src/auth/identity.ts:148-171. 6) The HTTP schema does not close it either — `displayName` and `temporaryPassword` are `.optional()` at phase1-backend/src/http/schemas/team.schema.ts:36-37, by design (D14-06), which is what makes the two branches distinguishable.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make both branches indistinguishable to a prober and stop returning a stranger's name. Require an explicit opt-in field (e.g. `linkExistingStaff: true`) in `createStoreStaphSchema` for the second-membership case, and in `createStoreStaff` validate `displayName`/`temporaryPassword` BEFORE the `findUnique` at staff.service.ts:92 whenever that flag is absent — so a probe without the flag gets the same 400 whether or not the address exists. On the linking path, return only `{ id, email }` for the pre-existing identity instead of the full `membershipSelect` payload (staff.service.ts:101), drop the `created: "staff" | "membership"` discriminator from the response, and add a per-actor ceiling on the route the way `limitUpload` is used at catalog.ts:47.

### 11-15-56 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:170`

**الادّعاء:** The permission-override PUT is a blind whole-set replace from a panel that never re-syncs, so one manager's save silently reinstates a permission another manager had denied — with no audit record that it happened

A second person holding `team.manage` — the owning merchant on his phone, another store_manager, or the same person in a second tab — had the colleague's «الصلاحيات» panel already open. He toggles one unrelated permission and saves. His PUT carries the override set as it looked when he opened the panel, the server deletes every override row and writes his set, and the deny is gone. The response shows the reinstated permission list as if it were correct, and because override changes are not an audited entity (AUDIT_ENTITY_TYPES / AUDIT_ACTIONS, phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/constants.ts:50 and :53-60 have no permission entry) nothing in «سجل النشاط» records that a restriction was lifted or by whom. The platform-staff mirror of this exact function DOES log it (recordPlatformAuditEvent with action "staff_overrides_changed", phase1-backend/src/services/platform-staff.service.ts:184-191), so the store side is the only one where the loss is both silent and untraceable. The staff member keeps working with a permission the store believes it took away.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:155 reads the membership (requireMembershipInStore, :116) with a plain SELECT, then :170-180 opens a transaction that unconditionally runs `tx.staffMembershipPermissionOverride.deleteMany({ where: { membershipId } })` (:171) followed by `createMany` of the request payload (:173) and `permissionVersion: { increment: 1 }` (:177). Nothing compares the caller's view of the row against the stored one. The route that reaches it, phase1-backend/src/http/routes/team.ts:33-38, reads no precondition, and the body schema phase1-backend/src/http/schemas/team.schema.ts:46-59 has exactly one field, `overrides` — no version, no If-Match. The compare-and-swap token already exists and is already on the wire: `permissionVersion` is returned in every membership payload at phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:40 and is bumped by this very function at :177. On the client, client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:73-74 seeds the editor with `const initial = new Map(membership.overrides.map(...)); const [draft, setDraft] = useState(initial)` — `useState` ignores every later value of `initial`, so the draft is frozen at mount. The row's wrapper carries `key={membership.id}` (:228) and the panel is shown while `expanded === membership.id` (:277), so the editor stays mounted across refreshes: `load()` (:127-137) is called on mount (:139), after every action (:148) and by the manual «تحديث» button (:211), and each of those replaces `team` with fresh server data while leaving `draft` untouched. The save button then posts the whole frozen draft: `onSave(Array.from(draft.entries()).map(([permissionCode, effect]) => ({ permissionCode, effect })))` (:109) → `PUT ${teamUrl}/${membership.id}/overrides` (:281). resolvePermissionSet (phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:66-71) then resolves live from whatever rows survived, so the reinstated permission is effective on the colleague's very next request. The codebase already recognises this hazard elsewhere and answers it — see the ORDER_TAGS_CONFLICT 409 «جرى تعديل وسوم هذا الطلب في جلسة أخرى في اللحظة نفسها» at phase1-backend/src/http/errors.ts:162 and :194 — but not here.

On the client, send `membership.permissionVersion` with the save and key the OverridesEditor on `${membership.id}:${membership.permissionVersion}` so a refreshed panel re-seeds its draft instead of holding a stale one.

### 11-15-57 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:136`

**الادّعاء:** updateStoreMembership writes by primary key alone, so a concurrent «إزالة من الفريق» turns a re-activation into a CHECK-constraint 500 and lets a role change land on a row already in the trash bin

**الأثر المزعوم:** Two people hold `team.manage` on the same store and both are looking at a suspended member. One clicks «تفعيل» (PATCH isActive:true), the other confirms «إزالة من الفريق» (DELETE) a moment earlier. The re-activation writes is_active = true onto a row whose deleted_at is now set, Postgres refuses it with 23514, and the error handler is explicitly written to let that one through as a bare 500 — the merchant gets «خطأ في الخادم» with no explanation and a page in the error budget for a fault the server does not have. The quieter variant is worse: a PATCH carrying only `roleCode` does not trip the CHECK, so it succeeds against the trashed row and returns 200. The membership is now in the bin holding a role nobody granted it, and when the merchant presses «تراجع» on the undo bar the restore hands it back at that role (restoring `isActive` from deletedWasActive), while the `soft_deleted` audit metadata still names the old role and no event anywhere records the change — a member removed as موظف متجر can come back as مدير متجر with the activity log showing nothing.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:122 calls requireMembershipInStore, which at :116 does `findFirst({ where: { id: membershipId, storeId, ...LIVE } })` — a plain SELECT, no lock, outside the transaction that is only opened afterwards at :135. Inside that transaction, :136-144 runs `tx.storeStaffMembership.update({ where: { id: membershipId }, data: { ...isActive, ...roleCode, permissionVersion: { increment: 1 } } })`: the predicate is the primary key alone, and neither `storeId` nor `deletedAt: null` is restated, so the guarantee established by the read at :116 is not carried into the write. The concurrent writer is phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/soft-delete.service.ts:291-302, which sets `deletedAt`, `deletedEventId` and `isActive: false` and commits. The constraint that then fires is prisma/migrations/20260828090200_pg2c_soft_delete_trash/migration.sql:84-86 — `ALTER TABLE store_staff_memberships ADD CONSTRAINT ssm_deleted_implies_inactive CHECK (deleted_at IS NULL OR is_active = false)`. phase1-backend/src/http/errors.ts:216-219 states the handling explicitly: the category-cycle 23514 is mapped, and «every other 23514 in this schema (`deleted ⇒ inactive`, `purge_block_requires_deleted`) is a broken invariant that MUST stay a 500». The restore that honours the tampered role is phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/soft-delete.service.ts:554-557 (`isActive: membership.deletedWasActive ?? false`, role untouched), while the removal's audit metadata recorded the pre-change role at :285. The two client controls that produce the pair are one click apart on the same screen: «تفعيل» → PATCH `{isActive: true}` at client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:251, and DELETE at client/src/components/DeleteConfirmSheet.tsx:363. The same defect is in setMembershipOverrides, phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:175-179, which also updates `where: { id: membershipId }` only.

**العلاج المقترح:** Restate the read's predicate in the write. Replace the `update` at phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:136-144 with `updateMany({ where: { id: membershipId, storeId, deletedAt: null }, data: {...} })`, treat `count === 0` as the same 404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND requireMembershipInStore would have raised, and re-select the row for the payload. Apply the identical change to the `tx.storeStaffMembership.update` at :175-179.

### 11-15-58 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:154`

The effect is durable because resolvePermissionSet strips any code carrying a `deny` (phase1-backend/src/auth/staff-permissions.ts:66-71) from the store_staff base set defined at :45, and the override rows are ordinary persisted rows that only the reseed clears.

### 11-15-59 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:97`

**الادّعاء:** Re-adding a former or multi-store employee silently discards the temporary password the merchant just typed, while the screen tells him to send it

**الأثر المزعوم:** Turnover is normal in a shop, and this platform sells multi-store accounts. Both paths lead here: the merchant removes Ahmed in the spring, re-adds him in the autumn; or he adds his existing cashier to his second store. He fills the form including a fresh «كلمة مرور مؤقتة (8 خانات فأكثر)» with a strength meter that gives it every appearance of mattering, submits, and gets a GREEN success notice instructing him to send that password. The server threw it away — Ahmed's password is still whatever it was months ago, which he has forgotten. Nothing on the screen distinguishes this from a real creation, because the client discards the `created: "membership" | "staff"` discriminator the server returns precisely to mark the difference. Recovery then fails too: staff password reset is email-only, and a shop assistant's Staff record commonly carries an address the merchant invented for him because the form demands one. The merchant's only remaining move is to invent a second fake email and create a duplicate identity for the same human. Separately, if he retries the same email against a live membership he is told «هذا البريد محجوز لهوية أخرى في المنصة ولا يمكن استخدامه لموظف» — flatly false; the person is already on his own team — which pushes him toward exactly that duplicate.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:92 does `prisma.staff.findUnique({ where: { email } })`, matching globally because prisma/schema.prisma:950 declares `email String @unique` on Staff → phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:94-102 takes the existing-staff branch and creates the membership WITHOUT touching `input.temporaryPassword` (the comment at :95-96 states the intent), returning `{ created: "membership" }` → client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:161-172 awaits `api(teamUrl, …)` and ignores the returned body entirely, then always shows «أُنشئ الموظف وأُسندت عضويته للمتجر الحالي. أرسل له رابط الدخول أدناه مع بريده وكلمة المرور المؤقتة.» → the password field it collected is client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:195, a PasswordInputWithMeter marked required minLength 8 → recovery is closed at phase1-backend/src/services/password-reset.service.ts:439 (`if (!isEmailProviderConfigured()) return { delivery: "unavailable" }`) and :482 (`channel: "email", recipient: email`), with staff resolved by email at :243-244. For the duplicate-retry wording: prisma/migrations/20260828090200_pg2c_soft_delete_trash/migration.sql:132 creates `store_staff_memberships_staff_store_live_key … WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`, whose P2002 matches none of the targets in phase1-backend/src/http/errors.ts:193-208 and falls to :209 DUPLICATE_IDENTITY, which client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:66 maps to the «محجوز لهوية أخرى في المنصة» sentence.

**العلاج المقترح:** Read the discriminator the server already sends and say what actually happened: on `created === "membership"`, tell the merchant the person already has a Codiq account and keeps his existing password — and refuse the temporary-password field for that case rather than collecting a value that is dropped. Give the merchant a «إعادة تعيين كلمة مرور مؤقتة» action on an existing membership row (team.manage-gated, sessions revoked, permissionVersion bumped like updateStoreMembership) so a returning employee is recoverable without an inbox. Split the DUPLICATE_IDENTITY mapping in StoreTeamManager.tsx:66 so a duplicate live membership says «هذا الموظف موجود في فريقك بالفعل» instead of claiming the address belongs to someone else.

### 11-15-60 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/wishlist.service.ts:60`

**الادّعاء:** The wishlist and the back-in-stock scanner read raw `stockQuantity` while the storefront reads `stockQuantity − active reservations`, so «نبّهني عند التوفر» is hidden or burned exactly when a COD shop is sold out

**الأثر المزعوم:** Under cash on delivery, stock never leaves `stockQuantity` at checkout — it is only reserved, and decremented at completion. So the ordinary "sold out" state of this platform is `stockQuantity = 5, active reservations = 5`: the product page correctly says «نفد المخزون حالياً» and the button offers the shopper the alert. He opens his wishlist to arm it and the item is listed as in stock, so the «نبّهني عند التوفر» checkbox is not rendered at all — the very screen the product page pointed him to refuses him the switch. If he did arm the flag earlier (while the shelf was genuinely at zero), the scanner fires on the first pass after the shelf refills even when every returning unit is instantly re-reserved: he gets «عاد المنتج للتوفر» within ten minutes, lands on a page that still says «نفد المخزون», and the flag has already been cleared in the same transaction — a one-shot request, spent on a false signal, that he will never be told about again unless he notices and re-asks. The merchant loses the sale the feature exists to recover.

**الدليل المزعوم:** The authoritative rule is phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:274-276, `isAvailable(stockQuantity, reservedQuantity) => stockQuantity - (reservedQuantity ?? 0) > 0`, used for the catalogue card at :380 and for each variant on the product page at :442. It is subtractive because checkout writes reservations without touching stock (storefront.service.ts:1001) and stock is decremented only at completion (storefront.service.ts:1238). The wishlist ignores it: phase1-backend/src/services/wishlist.service.ts:60 returns `inStock: item.variant.stockQuantity > 0`, and client/src/pages/CustomerWishlistPage.tsx:111 renders the notify control only inside `if (!item.inStock)` (:116-121, «نبّهني عند التوفر») — while client/src/components/WishlistButton.tsx:53 has just told him «احفظه الآن، ويمكنك طلب تنبيه عند التوفر من قائمة رغباتك» off the reservation-aware `inStock={available}` passed at client/src/pages/StorefrontProductPage.tsx:159 (`available` = `selectedVariant.isAvailable`, :108). The scanner repeats the same blindness: phase1-backend/src/notifications/restock-scanner.ts:57 selects candidates with `variant: { stockQuantity: { gt: 0 }, ... }`, and :114 clears `notifyOnRestock` in the same transaction as the enqueue at :97-113, which is what makes the request one-shot.

**العلاج المقترح:** Use the storefront's own definition in both places. In `listWishlist`, group active reservations for the page's variants (`inventoryReservation.groupBy({ by: ["variantId"], where: { storeId, variantId: { in: ... }, status: "active" }, _sum: { quantity: true } })`, as storefront.service.ts:366 already does) and report `inStock` as `stockQuantity - reserved > 0`. In `scanRestocks`, apply the same subtraction to each candidate page before enqueueing and skip any variant whose free stock is still zero, leaving `notifyOnRestock` standing so the shopper is told when the units are actually buyable.
