### 16-20-42 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:456`

**الادّعاء:** An upgrade request disappears into a hole the merchant cannot see into, and the owner's approval queue hides the phone number he needs to collect the money

**الأثر المزعوم:** Payment is manual until phase 25, so an approved upgrade to growth (25,000 IQD/month) is a cash or wallet transfer the owner must arrange by calling the merchant. The queue card shows only businessName and an email address (dir=ltr) — in a market where the merchant is reached by phone — while the merchants directory already carries ownerName and phone since ق-167. The owner must leave the queue and search the directory for every approval. On the merchant's side, after «أُرسل طلبك وسيراجعه فريق المنصة» the panel never shows the request again: no pending badge, no approved/rejected outcome, no email or WhatsApp on decision. A merchant whose request was rejected sees his plan unchanged and nothing else; one whose request is still pending discovers it only by clicking again and reading a 409. The platform's first paying customers are the people who file this request, and both sides of it are blind.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:456-464 — upgradeRequestSelect selects merchant { id, email, businessName } only (no phoneNormalized, no ownerName), while listMerchantsWithPlans at 392-396 selects ownerName and phoneNormalized (ق-167) and client/src/components/MerchantsDirectory.tsx:124 renders them. client/src/components/UpgradeRequestsPanel.tsx:43-44 — card shows businessName and email; 57-58 approve/reject; no phone anywhere in the file. Merchant side: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/platform.ts:294-298 — the only merchant route is POST /merchant/subscription/upgrade-request; no GET of his own requests exists (grep planUpgradeRequest → only entitlement.service.ts). entitlement.service.ts:188-201 — merchantEntitlementsView returns plan, subscription, entitlements; nothing about requests. client/src/components/SubscriptionPanel.tsx:10-11,58,95-116 — View type carries no request state; success text is a one-shot notice; buttons remain enabled after reload. entitlement.service.ts:476-477 — a second click answers 409 «لديك طلب ترقية قيد المراجعة بالفعل…», the merchant's only signal that a request is pending. entitlement.service.ts:521-559 — decideUpgradeRequest writes the row and an audit event only; no notificationOutbox row (grep upgrade in phase1-backend/src/notifications → none). UpgradeRequestsPanel.tsx:104 — «اعتُمد الطلب وأُسندت الخطة الجديدة — الحدود نافذة فوراً» is shown to the owner, never to the merchant.

**العلاج المقترح:** (1) Add `ownerName: true, phoneNormalized: true` to upgradeRequestSelect.merchant (entitlement.service.ts:462) and render «الاسم · الهاتف» with a tel:/wa.me link on the queue card next to the email. (2) Return the merchant's latest request in merchantEntitlementsView (`prisma.planUpgradeRequest.findFirst({ where: { merchantId }, orderBy: { createdAt: "desc" } })`) and show it in SubscriptionPanel as «طلبك إلى خطة «النمو» قيد المراجعة منذ …» / «اعتُمد» / «رُفض»; disable the request buttons while one is pending. (3) Enqueue an outbox row on decision (channel email today, WhatsApp template when ق-131's channel lands) so the merchant is told the outcome.

### 16-20-43 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:58`

**الادّعاء:** When a trial's endsAt passes, the shopper at checkout is answered 403 «لا يوجد اشتراك نشط لهذا التاجر؛ راجِع إدارة المنصة» and the merchant cannot mark a cash order completed — for up to ten minutes per merchant, and for the whole time the worker is not running

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every merchant's free month ends between two scanner passes, so each one spends up to ten minutes in a state where his storefront checkout returns a 403 whose text is addressed to the platform's administration, and where a courier's «completed» (the moment cash changes hands) is refused with the same sentence. If the standalone worker is down — a deploy gap, a crash, a Railway restart — that window becomes hours, for every merchant whose trial ends in it, and the product's own header comment calls this «deleting the merchant's account by omission». The ق-216 split at checkout catches only PLAN_LIMIT_EXCEEDED, so this sibling code from the same resolver reaches the shopper raw. A read-side fallback would make the scanner a tidy-up instead of a load-bearing dependency.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:52-58 — activePlanForMerchant matches status=active AND (endsAt null OR endsAt > now) and otherwise throws 403 NO_ACTIVE_SUBSCRIPTION «لا يوجد اشتراك نشط لهذا التاجر؛ راجِع إدارة المنصة.». entitlement.service.ts:157-165 — every new merchant gets a growth row with endsAt = now + 30 days. phase1-backend/src/notifications/trial-expiry-scanner.ts:45,56-112,201-215 — the only writer that replaces a lapsed row runs on a 10-minute interval; phase1-backend/src/notifications/worker-entry.ts:55 — in the separate worker process; trial-expiry-scanner.ts:8-17 states the consequence («dead dashboard AND dead storefront checkout»). Shopper path: phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:860-867 — the catch converts only PLAN_LIMIT_EXCEEDED; NO_ACTIVE_SUBSCRIPTION is rethrown (line 866). client/src/lib/storefront-api.ts:335-373 — no mapping for NO_ACTIVE_SUBSCRIPTION, so the raw sentence is displayed. Cash path: phase1-backend/src/services/order-lifecycle.service.ts:271,287 → phase1-backend/src/services/finance.service.ts:52 activePlanForMerchant(tx, merchantId) inside the completion transaction, so the whole «completed» transition fails.

**العلاج المقترح:** In activePlanForMerchant, when no live row matches, fall back to the default plan for a merchant whose latest row is a lapsed trial (status active, endsAt ≤ now, plan.code === TRIAL_PLAN_CODE) — read-side, no write — so the scanner's later write only tidies what the resolver already answered; keep NO_ACTIVE_SUBSCRIPTION for the genuinely uncovered merchant. In storefront.service.ts:863 also map NO_ACTIVE_SUBSCRIPTION to STORE_NOT_ACCEPTING_ORDERS so an administrative sentence never reaches a shopper. Extend trial-expiry.integration.mjs with a checkout and a completion executed one minute after endsAt with the scanner not yet run.

### 16-20-44 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:113`

**الادّعاء:** The entitlement gate inside the serializable checkout was not lightened: assertWithinPlanLimit still computes every plan feature (eight aggregates plus a subscription read) to check one counter, and the checkout transaction still runs on Prisma's default timeout — only the 'move before the locks' half of D-17 was delivered.

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every COD checkout — guest checkouts included (guest-checkout.service.ts:88 funnels into the same function) — spends nine sequential round trips inside a SERIALIZABLE transaction on counts and byte-sums of the merchant's stores, products, staff, orders, images, prescriptions, banners and logo, to learn a single number. On Neon's pooled connections that is tens of milliseconds added to the hottest path per order, and under SSI those wide reads take predicate locks over the merchant's whole catalog and image tables, so an unrelated write (a product created, an image uploaded, another shopper's order this month) can abort a checkout with 40001 and burn one of its six retries. The transaction has no explicit timeout/maxWait, so the 5 s default that D-47 named is still the ceiling for a path that now carries roughly 28 statements rather than the ~20 the debt was filed against.

**الدليل المزعوم:** checkout() opens prisma.$transaction with only { isolationLevel: Serializable } (storefront.service.ts:808 → :1008 — no timeout, no maxWait; the only service that sets them is csv-transfer.service.ts:351). Inside it, after the idempotency, vacation, cart and store-owner reads (:809-847), it calls assertWithinPlanLimit(tx, merchantId, 'max_monthly_orders') (:861). That function always runs activePlanForMerchant (entitlement.service.ts:111 → :52-57, a subscription read) and then merchantUsage(client, merchantId) (:113), which fires eight aggregates unconditionally — store.count, product.count, storeStaffMembership.count, storeOrder.count, and four byte-sum aggregates (entitlement.service.ts:67-95) — before comparing usage[feature] (:114). Inside an interactive transaction the Promise.all is serialised on one connection. The same statement count then continues through address, delivery rate, payment method, coupon lock, the FOR UPDATE variant lock, reservations groupBy, customer read, nested order create, loyalty, coupon redemption, reservations createMany, cart update, checkout-request update and outbox enqueue (:868-1006). D-17's own mitigation reads «حساب عدّاد الميزة المطلوبة فقط + نقل فحص الطلبات الشهرية قبل الأقفال» (docs/history/reports/PHASE18_DEBT_REGISTER.md:43); D-47's reads «تقليص الاستعلامات داخل المعاملة (مع D-17) + timeout/maxWait صريحين» (:73). What shipped is the move-before-locks half (ق-222 comment, storefront.service.ts:837-845, which itself says the gate «fires six aggregates across every store the merchant owns» — it is now eight) and P2028 in the retry set (db/serializable-retry.ts:44-48). The register marks D-39, D-46 and D-49 ✅ (:65,72,75) and leaves D-17 and D-47 without that mark (:43,:73).

**العلاج المقترح:** Give assertWithinPlanLimit a per-feature meter: a usageFor(client, merchantId, feature) that runs the one query the feature needs (for max_monthly_orders, the single storeOrder.count at entitlement.service.ts:79) and only falls back to merchantUsage for callers that display the whole panel. Pass explicit { timeout, maxWait } on the checkout transaction (storefront.service.ts:1008) sized from a measured p99 of the ~28-statement path, and record the two numbers in the PR so D-17/D-47 can be closed with evidence rather than a note.

### 16-20-45 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/finance.service.ts:105`

**الادّعاء:** The ledger has no database rule of one reversal per order, so two concurrent manual adjustments (or any retried POST) both insert and permanently reverse an order twice

**الأثر المزعوم:** ADR-006 Decision 1 promises the manual adjustment is «append-only، ممنوع التكرار لنفس الطلب», but the only enforcement is an application read-then-insert. Two platform actors (owner + a staff member with finance.settle) submitting the same order id within the same transaction window, or a client retry after a lost response, produce two `manual_adjustment` rows. Every reader treats each non-completed entry as a subtraction, so the merchant's next settlement and wallet are reduced by a second full (subtotal + delivery − commission) — for a 60,000 IQD order that is roughly 58,000 IQD taken from him — and the platform's commission report undercounts. The trigger makes both rows permanent, and there is no positive entry type to compensate with: the CHECK allows only three types and all readers sign them completed=+, everything else=−. The same missing constraint is what backs `recordOrderReturn`'s guard at :77; that path happens to be protected by the order version CAS, this one is not.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1) Read-then-write with no lock or unique key: finance.service.ts:102 opens the transaction; :105 `findFirst({ where: { orderId, entryType: { in: ['order_returned','manual_adjustment'] } } })` sees only committed rows at READ COMMITTED; :109-122 `ledgerEntry.create({ entryType: 'manual_adjustment', ... })`. Two transactions interleaving :105 before either commits both pass and both insert. 2) No database uniqueness: prisma/migrations/20260825190000_phase17_finance_domains_registration/migration.sql:29-47 declares ledger_entries with CHECK on entry_type and bps and three plain indexes — no UNIQUE on (order_id, entry_type) or partial unique on order_id for reversal types; schema.prisma:1263-1287 likewise. 3) Permanent: migration :57-59 `ledger_entries_immutable BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE`. 4) No compensating type: migration :41 CHECK entry_type IN ('order_completed','order_returned','manual_adjustment'); readers sign every non-completed entry negative at finance.service.ts:181 and :195 and merchant-wallet.service.ts:55. 5) Only client-side double-click guard: FinanceCenter.tsx:189 `disabled={busy || ...}` — nothing server-side; the route platform.ts:215-220 has no idempotency key. 6) The suite tests only the sequential duplicate: phase17.finance.integration.ts:133-137 (second POST after the first committed → 409).

**العلاج المقترح:** Add a partial unique index in a migration: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ledger_entries_one_reversal_per_order ON ledger_entries(order_id) WHERE entry_type IN ('order_returned','manual_adjustment')` (and, for symmetry, `... one_completion_per_order ... WHERE entry_type = 'order_completed'`), then in createManualReturnAdjustment catch Prisma P2002 and rethrow as the existing 409 ALREADY_REVERSED so the second writer gets the same answer the sequential duplicate already gets.

### 16-20-46 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/finance.service.ts:333`

**الادّعاء:** createSettlement's timestamp-cutoff read cannot see an in-flight completion whose createdAt already falls inside the period, and the contiguity guard plus the wallet window then exclude that order from every future payout

**الأثر المزعوم:** An order the courier delivered is booked in the ledger with createdAt ≤ periodTo but commits a few milliseconds after the settlement's read; it is missing from that settlement, the next settlement must start ≥ periodTo, and the wallet's pending window is `gt: periodTo` — so the merchant is never paid for it and no screen ever shows it as owed again. Probability per settlement is low (the window is the completion transaction's create→commit latency overlapping the settlement's now→read gap), but the bulk sequencer runs fifty back-to-back completion transactions, the owner settles during business hours, and when it happens the loss is one full order payout, silent and unrepairable because settlements are immutable and the guard refuses any period starting before the latest periodTo.

**الدليل المزعوم:** Completion side: order-lifecycle.service.ts:243 opens the transaction; :256 CAS status update; :287 recordOrderCompletion → finance.service.ts:54-66 `ledgerEntry.create` with createdAt from `@default(now())` (schema.prisma:1279) assigned at insert time, before commit; :298-301 awardLoyaltyPoints and :330 a re-read with includes still run before commit. Bulk: order-bulk.service.ts:95-113 loops fifty such transactions. Settlement side: finance.service.ts:305 `settlementPeriod(body, new Date())` → :172 `to = now` (the ordinary «حتى اليوم» case per :163-166); :306 merchant lookup; :309 transaction; :325 findFirst; :333-336 `findMany({ createdAt: { gte: from, lte: to } })` at READ COMMITTED — an uncommitted entry with createdAt ≤ to is invisible; :338-353 writes periodTo = to. Afterwards: :330 refuses any `from < previous.periodTo`, so the missed entry (createdAt < periodTo) can never be inside a later settlement; merchant-wallet.service.ts:91,96 `gt: lastSettlement.periodTo` hides it from «محفظتي».

**العلاج المقترح:** Clamp the settlement ceiling to a safety margin rather than the instant: in settlementPeriod use `const ceiling = new Date(now.valueOf() - 60_000)` (or similar) as the clamp and as the refusal bound, so any completion transaction still in flight at the cutoff has committed long before the read. Alternatively serialise per merchant with `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(merchantId))` at the top of both createSettlement and the completed/returned branches of transitionOrderStatus.

### 16-20-47 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/list-page.ts:84`

**الادّعاء:** encodeListCursor serialises the ordering timestamp at millisecond precision while every createdAt column stores microseconds, so ascending keyset pages repeat their boundary row and descending pages silently drop same-millisecond rows

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every list paged on `createdAt` is affected (≈28 call sites). Ascending lists — order notes (order-notes.service.ts:74), pending merchant registrations (registration.service.ts:116), the merchant's return-request queue (return-request.service.ts:140) — serve the last row of page N again as the first row of page N+1 on essentially every boundary (whenever the stored value has a non-zero sub-millisecond part, i.e. ~999 times in 1000); with `limit=1` the cursor never advances at all. Descending lists — merchant/platform orders, products, customers, audit log, inventory movements, ledger, loyalty statement — silently omit any row that shares the boundary row's millisecond but was not on the page: rows written by one `createMany` (loyalty.service.ts:348 writes the reversed_earn and refunded_spend pair in one statement, paged at :465-468), bursts of concurrent checkouts on the platform-wide order list, and — worst — exportOrdersCsv (csv-transfer.service.ts:463-491) walks the whole order history with the same cursor, so an accountant's export can be short a few orders with no error and no way to notice. The repository already knows the precision mismatch exists: tests/pg3.integration.mjs:107-112 and tests/marketing-recipient-cap.integration.mjs:47-48 document a Prisma-created `@default(now())` row landing as `.494832` while JS holds `.494` — the cursor code never got the memo.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. The cursor value: list-page.ts:82-85 `encodeListCursor` → `value instanceof Date ? value.toISOString() : …` — a JS Date holds integer milliseconds, so `last.createdAt` read back from Postgres has already lost its microseconds before it is minted (pageOf at :236-241 mints from the last returned row). 2. The columns: every `createdAt` is `DateTime @default(now()) @map("created_at") @db.Timestamptz(6)` (prisma/schema.prisma:704 StoreOrder, :1421 OrderNote, :1624 LoyaltyEntry, and 50+ more) over DDL `created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` (e.g. prisma/migrations/20260816211500_store_scoped_catalog_and_orders/migration.sql:23). Repo-observed precision of such a value: tests/pg3.integration.mjs:107-112 («`next_attempt_at` defaults to now() at MICROSECOND precision while a JS Date is milliseconds … `.494000 >= .494832`») for a row created by `prisma.notificationOutbox.create` with no explicit value (tests/marketing-recipient-cap.integration.mjs:60) — `nextAttemptAt` is declared identically to every `createdAt` (schema.prisma:1133). 3. The predicate: list-page.ts:213-227 — asc: `{ createdAt: { gte: v } } AND ({ createdAt: { gt: v } } OR { id: { gt: i } })`. Boundary row L stored as …:00.494832, cursor v = …:00.494 → `gte` true, `gt` true → L is on the next page again; order-notes.service.ts:74-79 / registration.service.ts:116-122 / return-request.service.ts:140-149 use exactly this with `orderBy: [{ createdAt: "asc" }, { id: "asc" }]`. 4. desc: `{ createdAt: { lte: v } } AND ({ createdAt: { lt: v } } OR { id: { lt: i } })`. Any row with created_at in (…494000, …494832] that was not on the page fails `lte v` and is never served; a row with identical µs and smaller id (the very tie the id tiebreaker exists for, per the comment at :163-165) is dropped too. 5. Why CI is green: tests/contract.list-envelope.integration.mjs:167 creates its fixture orders with explicit `createdAt: new Date(Date.now() - (5 - index) * 60_000)` — ms-precision values 60 s apart, so the collision can never occur — and `walk()` (:76-97) is only applied to descending lists (:175-218); no suite walks notes, registrations or return requests page by page (grep for `(notes|registrations|return-requests)\?(limit|cursor)` in tests: no matches). tests/keyset-index.guard.mjs checks orderBy/keyset agreement textually (:267-277) and cannot see precision.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the two sides agree on precision. Smallest correct change: one migration `ALTER TABLE <t> ALTER COLUMN created_at TYPE timestamptz(3)` (plus `@db.Timestamptz(3)` in schema.prisma) for every column a `keysetWhere` orders on — Postgres rounds on write, so `now()` from either the engine or the DB default lands at the millisecond JS can carry, and the pg3/marketing «one second ahead» flakes disappear with it. If a rewrite is unwelcome now, carry the anchor at full precision instead: after the page read, `SELECT created_at::text FROM <t> WHERE id = $last` via $queryRaw and mint the cursor from that string. Either way add a `walk()` case that creates rows inside one `$transaction`/`createMany` and pages with `limit=1` in BOTH directions.

### 16-20-48 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/order-lifecycle.service.ts:303`

**الادّعاء:** A return after the 15-day window succeeds silently: the `outside_window` outcome is discarded at the transition, nobody is told, and the owner's only manual path is a text box demanding a raw order UUID with no list of the returned orders that need it.

**الأثر المزعوم:** In Iraq a return after delivery is normal: the shopper phones the shop, the shop presses «نقل إلى مُرجَع» on the order screen. Past day 15 that click flips the order to returned but books nothing (finance.service.ts:81 returns "outside_window"; :303 in order-lifecycle drops the value). The order_completed entry stays: the merchant keeps paying commission on a sale he refunded and his wallet/settlement keep subtotal+delivery for goods that came back (merchant-wallet.service.ts:52-63, 94-107; finance.service.ts:333-350). The merchant's screen shows no warning before the click and no notice after it (client/src/components/OrderWorkspace.tsx:643, and the transition response carries no ledger data — order-lifecycle.service.ts:330-332). The phase's own comment admits the cost: «leaving the merchant paying commission on a sale he refunded until the platform owner adjusts it by hand» (return-request.service.ts:18-20). The owner has no way to find such orders: the only surface is FinanceCenter.tsx:186-190, a text box labelled «معرّف الطلب (UUID)», and no query anywhere in src lists returned orders that lack a reversal. So the promised manual settlement path exists as an endpoint the owner cannot know to use, and the money stays wrong until a merchant notices his statement and phones with a UUID he has to dig out of a URL.

**الدليل المزعوم:** 1. order-lifecycle.service.ts:302-303 — `else if (toStatus === "returned") { await recordOrderReturn(tx, { storeId, orderId }); ... }` — the ReturnReversalOutcome is not captured; no audit, outbox or flag is written on the outside-window branch. 2. finance.service.ts:71-81 — `if (now - completion.createdAt > windowMs) return "outside_window";` with no side effect. 3. grep for outside_window across phase1-backend/src and client/src: only finance.service.ts:69,81 and the comment at return-request.service.ts:18. 4. The CD-3 gate that refuses a late request (return-request.service.ts:82-86, RETURN_WINDOW_CLOSED) applies only to the shopper's self-service request; the merchant's own transition from OrderWorkspace.tsx:622 → POST .../status → transitionOrderStatus bypasses it. 5. OrderWorkspace.tsx:643 renders «نقل إلى «مُرجَع»» with no window notice; :623 shows only the returned order payload. 6. FinanceCenter.tsx:101-116 adjust() posts whatever UUID is typed; :186-190 is the only entry point; createManualReturnAdjustment (finance.service.ts:100-108) then requires the order to already be `returned`. 7. The acceptance test proves only half: phase17.finance.integration.ts:131-135 shows a late return books nothing and that an owner handed the UUID can post the adjustment; nothing asserts anyone is notified or can discover the order.

**العلاج المقترح:** Capture the outcome at order-lifecycle.service.ts:303; when it is "outside_window", inside the same transaction write a platform audit event (recordPlatformAuditEvent entityType "ledger_reversal_skipped") and return `ledgerOutcome` in the transition payload so OrderWorkspace can show «الإرجاع خارج نافذة 15 يوماً — التسوية المالية بيد إدارة المنصة». Add GET /platform/finance/late-returns: store_orders.status='returned' joined to ledger_entries having an order_completed entry and no order_returned/manual_adjustment (both indexed: ledger_entries_order_idx), listed in FinanceCenter with a one-click «تسجيل قيد الإرجاع» that calls the existing endpoint — replacing the UUID box. Optionally warn in OrderWorkspace before the click when the completion entry is older than RETURN_REVERSAL_WINDOW_DAYS.

### 16-20-49 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/order-lifecycle.service.ts:166`

**الادّعاء:** Order, customer and product search silently keeps only 200 hits, and because whole-word exact matches all tie at rank 1.0 the 200 kept are chosen by uuid order rather than recency, so the newest order for a common name can be missing with no sign

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant with the shopper on the phone searches «محمد» or «علي» — a word in a large share of Iraqi full names — in a store past roughly a thousand orders (a normal year). More than 200 orders match as exact whole-word hits, all tie at rank 1.0, the engine keeps the 200 with the highest uuids, and the list then pages those by date. Yesterday's order from that customer is missing, nothing in the envelope says anything was dropped, and the merchant answers «ما عندك طلب» — a duplicate order or a lost sale at the door. The same happens for «حسين» in the customer directory and «قميص» in a 300-shirt catalogue. The comment at :135-136 promised 20-2 would replace this cap with a (rank, id) cursor; 20-2 shipped (services/list-page.ts) and the cap is still there, still unsignalled.

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/order-lifecycle.service.ts:138 SEARCH_ID_CAP = 200; :152-166 runs the three engine searches with `limit: 200` and returns `mergeSearchPages(pages, 200).hits`; :189 narrows `where.id IN (...)` to those ids; :191 applies the `(createdAt, id)` keyset over that subset; :205-210 returns `orders, nextCursor, availableTags, appliedTag` — no truncation field. Engine: phase1-backend/src/services/search/postgres-trigram.engine.ts:214-223 `ORDER BY exact DESC, rank DESC, id DESC LIMIT limit+1`; a whole-word exact hit scores word_similarity 1.0 (prisma/migrations/20260904090000_pg6_arabic_search/migration.sql:49-56), so ties among exact hits fall to `id DESC` = uuid order. phase1-backend/src/services/search/search.types.ts:94-108 merges the columns and `slice(0, limit)`; `compareHits` :112-116 also breaks ties on id; the merged `hasMore` is discarded by every list caller. Same shape in phase1-backend/src/services/customer-directory.service.ts:42,59-68,93,105-108 and phase1-backend/src/services/product-catalog.service.ts:231,251-261,268.

**العلاج المقترح:** Add `created_at` to the engine's ORDER BY (`exact DESC, rank DESC, created_at DESC, id DESC`) and to `SearchHit`/`compareHits`, so the 200 kept are the newest tied hits — the ones a merchant on the phone wants — and return `searchTruncated: mergeSearchPages(...).hasMore` from the three list services so the page can say «عُرضت أحدث ٢٠٠ نتيجة — ضيّق البحث برقم الطلب أو الهاتف».

### 16-20-50 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/owner-totp.service.ts:179`

**الادّعاء:** Second-factor consumption is a read-then-write with no compare-and-swap, so one TOTP code or one recovery code can be spent by two concurrent logins

**الأثر المزعوم:** The replay guard is the only thing that turns a shoulder-surfed, screen-shared or phishing-relayed code into a one-time credential. Under concurrency it fails silently in the worst direction: an attacker who submits the captured code in the same instant as the owner (a real-time relay does exactly this) gets a session AND the owner's own login succeeds, so the owner sees nothing wrong — whereas a sequential replay would have refused HIS login and alerted him. The same holds for the bcrypt-hashed recovery codes, which are meant to be single-use «one of my ten is gone». Platform staff have the identical shape.

**الدليل المزعوم:** owner-totp.service.ts:168-171 reads totpLastStep; :174-177 verifyTotp(..., { lastUsedStep }) — the only replay check is totp.ts:137 `step <= options.lastUsedStep` against that stale in-memory value; :179 unconditional `prisma.platformOwner.update({ where: { id }, data: { totpLastStep } })` with no `where totpLastStep < step`; :180 returns ok. Two requests carrying the same code both read the old step, both pass :137, both write :179, both return ok, and auth.service.ts:110-115 mints a session for each. Recovery path: :187-190 findMany unused codes; :192 bcrypt compare; :193 `platformOwnerRecoveryCode.update({ where: { id: candidate.id } })` with no `usedAt: null` predicate and no count check — two concurrent logins with the same code both stamp usedAt and both succeed. platform-staff-totp.service.ts:77-89 repeats the pattern (read :77-80, verify :83-86, unconditional update :88). routes/auth.ts:23 rate-limits the owner door at 30 per 10 min per IP, which does not stop a two-request race. tests/phase19b.owner-totp.integration.mjs:146-147 tests only a sequential replay.

**العلاج المقترح:** Make the consume the claim: replace :179 with `updateMany({ where: { id: ownerId, OR: [{ totpLastStep: null }, { totpLastStep: { lt: BigInt(check.step!) } }] }, data: { totpLastStep: BigInt(check.step!) } })` and return `{ ok: false }` when count !== 1; replace :193 with `updateMany({ where: { id: candidate.id, usedAt: null }, data: { usedAt: now } })` and only return ok when count === 1. ### 16-20-51 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/registration.service.ts:95`

**الادّعاء:** The 30-day trial clock starts at registration and keeps running while the merchant is locked out awaiting owner approval, so a manually-approved merchant receives a shorter «شهر مجاني» than promised (or none at all).

**الأثر المزعوم:** Every merchant who registers while `merchant_self_registration_mode` is `manual` loses one trial day for every day the owner takes to approve him. He cannot log in (auth.service.ts:159-161 refuses with «طلب تسجيلك قيد مراجعة إدارة المنصة؛ ستصلك الموافقة قريباً.») and his store is inactive (registration.service.ts:97), yet `merchant_subscriptions.ends_at` was stamped at signup. A week's delay costs him a quarter of the growth-plan month (25,000 IQD list price, 2% vs 3% commission on every completed COD order). If approval takes more than 30 days the scanner moves him to `free` before he has logged in once, and his first dashboard shows «المجانية» with no trial at all. The manual-provisioning path has the same shape (manual-provisioning.service.ts:42-45): the clock starts before the merchant is handed the temporary password.

**الدليل المزعوم:** registration.service.ts:82-83 reads the mode and sets `approved = mode === "auto"`; :91-92 creates the merchant with `approvalStatus: "pending"`; :95 calls `subscribeMerchantToDefaultPlan(tx, merchant.id)` unconditionally; :97 deactivates the store. entitlement.service.ts:157-165 creates the trial row with `endsAt: now + TRIAL_DAYS * 24h` with no regard to approval status. auth.service.ts:159-161 refuses login while pending. registration.service.ts:129-149 `decideMerchantApproval` updates `merchant.approvalStatus/isActive` (:136) and `store.isActive` (:137) and records the audit row (:139) — it never touches `merchant_subscriptions`, so `endsAt` is not re-stamped. trial-expiry-scanner.ts:69-70 selects `status: "active", endsAt: { lte: now }` with no approval filter and :83-98 moves the row to `expired` and creates a `free` row. entitlement.service.ts:181-186 `subscriptionSummary` then reports `daysLeft` from the original `endsAt`, so the merchant's first dashboard shows a countdown that already ran without him.

**العلاج المقترح:** In `decideMerchantApproval` (registration.service.ts:135), inside the same transaction as the approval, re-issue the trial: `updateMany({ where: { merchantId, status: "active" }, data: { status: "cancelled", endsAt: new Date() } })` then call `subscribeMerchantToDefaultPlan(tx, merchantId)` so the growth row's `endsAt` is stamped from the approval moment (this also re-grants a trial the scanner already expired). Alternatively skip `subscribeMerchantToDefaultPlan` at :95 when `!approved` and create the subscription at approval time — but then every reader of `activePlanForMerchant` for a pending merchant must tolerate NO_ACTIVE_SUBSCRIPTION, so the re-stamp is the smaller change.

### 16-20-52 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/registration.service.ts:79`

**الادّعاء:** POST /public/merchants/register answers, without writing anything, whether a given email is the platform owner's login and whether a given Iraqi phone is a merchant's login — the two facts the login doors were made constant-work to hide.

**الأثر المزعوم:** A stranger with an occupied store slug in hand (every live slug is published at /sitemap.xml, storefront-seo.service.ts:211) can send up to 6 probes per 10 minutes per IP and get three distinguishable, deterministic answers with no row ever written: «رقم الهاتف مسجَّل لحساب تاجر آخر على المنصة.» (this 07… number is a merchant login), «هذا البريد محجوز لهوية أخرى في المنصة.» (this email is the platform owner's login), or «رابط المتجر مستخدم بالفعل.» (neither). For the owner: TOTP is optional (auth.service.ts:102 `if (owner.totpEnabledAt)`), and /public/contact publishes a contact address (public.ts:74-78); one probe tells an attacker whether that public address is also the login of the one account with no tenant boundary above it — the address auth.service.ts:96-97 calls «the highest-value phishing target here». For merchants: the phone IS the login identifier (auth.service.ts:141-151), and in Iraq a shop's number is painted on the door, so one probe per shop confirms «this shop is on CODIQ and this number opens its dashboard» — the list that D-65 (docs/history/reports/PHASE18_DEBT_REGISTER.md:91) was closed to prevent. The landing page shows the sentence verbatim (client/src/pages/Home.tsx:267).

**الدليل المزعوم:** Route: public.ts:85-90 — requireTrustedWriteOrigin → consumeStorefrontRateLimit({scope:"merchant_self_register", rawKey: request.ip, limit: 6, windowMs: 10 min}) → registerMerchantSchema (strings only, registration.schema.ts:30-38) → registerMerchantWithStore. The limiter is IP-keyed under `trust proxy 1` (http/app.ts:46; storefront.service.ts:229-246), so it holds but bounds only the rate, not the answer. Service order, registration.service.ts: 60-67 shape checks; 72 assertStoreSlugNotReserved; 73 normalizeEmail; 76-77 ownerName/phone; 78 `await assertPhoneIsFree(phone)` → auth.service.ts:70-76 `merchant.findFirst({phoneNormalized})` → throws 409 DUPLICATE_MERCHANT_PHONE «رقم الهاتف مسجَّل لحساب تاجر آخر على المنصة.»; 79-80 `platformOwner.findUnique({email})` → throws 409 EMAIL_RESERVED_FOR_PLATFORM_OWNER «هذا البريد محجوز لهوية أخرى في المنصة.»; 81 createPasswordHash; 88-90 inside $transaction: `store.findUnique({slug})` → 409 DUPLICATE_STORE_SLUG «رابط المتجر مستخدم بالفعل.» — this runs BEFORE the first write at 91 (`merchant.create`), so with an occupied slug the transaction never inserts. Ordering makes the answers separable: phone is decided at 78 before email at 79 before slug at 89. A merchant's email is separable too: with a free slug, 91 hits the `merchants.email` unique index → P2002 target `email` → http/errors.ts:209 DUPLICATE_IDENTITY (transaction rolls back, nothing persists), whereas a store-staff/platform-staff email hits the cross-identity trigger (prisma/migrations/20260903120000_ps1_removed_staff_email_release/migration.sql:37-44, target-less 23505) → errors.ts:187-191 EMAIL_RESERVED_FOR_PLATFORM_OWNER with the English message — a third distinct answer. Contrast, same repo: loginPlatformOwner equalises work and message precisely to hide owner-email existence (auth.service.ts:93-100), loginMerchant does the same for phone/email (152-157), and password-reset.service.ts:240-241 returns null rather than a distinguishing code; this door reverses all three.

**العلاج المقترح:** Two small changes in registration.service.ts plus one mapping: (1) delete the pre-transaction owner lookup at lines 79-80 and let the existing DB trigger answer inside the transaction; then in http/errors.ts collapse the target-less P2002 branch (187-191) and the `email`-target P2002 for modelName "Merchant" into ONE code and ONE Arabic sentence (e.g. 409 DUPLICATE_IDENTITY «هذا البريد مستخدم بالفعل — سجّل الدخول أو استخدم بريداً آخر.») so owner, staff and merchant emails are indistinguishable from the outside. (2) For the phone, the registrant genuinely must be told his number is taken, so keep the message but price the probe: before assertPhoneIsFree add `await consumeStorefrontRateLimit({ scope: "merchant_self_register_phone", rawKey: phone, limit: 3, windowMs: 60*60*1000 })` keyed on the NORMALISED PHONE rather than the neighbourhood IP, and move the slug-occupied check (88-90) ahead of assertPhoneIsFree so an occupied slug cannot serve as a free «stop after the phone answer» switch. Add to phase17.finance.integration.ts a case that posts the seeded owner's email with an occupied slug and asserts the response code equals the one a duplicate merchant email receives.

### 16-20-53 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/soft-delete.service.ts:140`

**الادّعاء:** Removing a product is refused because of carts nobody is shopping from — 37 days for a guest who never returns, forever for a registered shopper — while the merchant is told it is «عملية شراء جارية» and steered to «أخفِه», which keeps spending his plan slot

**الأثر المزعوم:** In a cash-on-delivery storefront the ordinary shopper browses on a phone, adds to cart and closes the tab; a best-seller or a discontinued item is almost always sitting in some abandoned cart. The merchant who wants to remove it reads «هذا المنتج في سلة زبون نشطة الآن، فستُرفض الإزالة حتى تنتهي تلك السلة» — but a guest cart abandoned today keeps `status = 'active'` for 37 days unless that same guest returns, and a registered shopper's cart never ends. The only offered path is «أخفِه من متجرك بدلاً من ذلك», which still counts against `max_products`, so a merchant at his plan cap cannot free the slot to replace the item — exactly the «hide instead of remove» behaviour PG-2 says it exists to end (soft-delete.service.ts:46-49).

**الدليل المزعوم:** phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/soft-delete.service.ts:140-142 refuses when any `cartItem` exists with `cart: { status: "active" }`, message «… لا يمكن إزالته أثناء عملية شراء جارية», hint `deactivate`. Guest carts: lifetime 7 days (phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:26), re-extended on every touch (:477), flipped to `expired` only when THAT guest's cookie comes back (:250-251); otherwise the row stays `active` until the retention sweep deletes it 30 days after `expires_at` (phase1-backend/src/services/retention.ts:38 guestCarts: 30; phase1-backend/src/services/retention-job.ts:144-154). Customer carts: no expiry and nothing sweeps them (storefront.service.ts:948; phase1-backend/src/notifications/abandoned-cart-scanner.ts:20-21; retention-job.ts:132; the register's own D-85 row, docs/history/reports/PHASE18_DEBT_REGISTER.md:198: «سلة الزبون المسجَّل بلا expiresAt ولا ماسح ينهيها»). Hiding does not empty carts — checkout refuses the line (storefront.service.ts:922-930) but the cart_items row stays. The sheet: client/src/components/DeleteConfirmSheet.tsx:271 «حتى تنتهي تلك السلة», :210 «أخفِه من متجرك بدلاً من ذلك». Quota: phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:73 counts `...LIVE` products, hidden ones included.

**العلاج المقترح:** Count only carts a shopper can still act on — guest: `expiresAt > now`; customer: `lastActivityAt` within the same 7-day window — and treat older ones as stale, deleting their `cart_items` rows and bumping `version` inside the delete transaction exactly as :190-198 already does for expired/merged/converted carts. Reword the refusal and the sheet line to «في سلة زبون تحرّك خلال الأيام السبعة الماضية».

### 16-20-54 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:140`

**الأثر المزعوم:** The acceptance criterion «every limit refused SERVER-side even by a direct API call at the ceiling» is false for max_staff. At the ceiling a merchant suspends one member (usage drops by one), invites a new one (passes), then re-activates the suspended one (no check) — and repeats. It needs no script: the toggle is a button on the team screen. The platform's paid seat tiers (2 / 10 / 30) become advisory, and the usage bar reads 3/2 with nothing having refused anything.

Re-activation path: staff.service.ts:121-152 updateStoreMembership — :128 parses isActive, :135-144 writes `isActive: nextActive` (line 140) inside a transaction with no call to assertWithinPlanLimit; activeChanged === true && nextActive === true has no branch (:145 only handles the false direction for session revocation). Route: phase1-backend/src/http/routes/team.ts:27-32; schema accepts isActive: phase1-backend/src/http/schemas/team.schema.ts:41-44. 4. Reachable from the UI with a success toast «أُعيد تفعيل العضوية.»: client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:251 (PATCH { isActive: !membership.isActive }). 5. The platform already knows re-occupying a seat spends quota — the trash restore path gates it with «أو أوقف موظفاً آخر لإعادة هذه العضوية»: phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/soft-delete.service.ts:536-542 (checks only when deletedWasActive, i.e. exactly the isActive transition this PATCH performs without a check). 6. The acceptance test asserts only POST at the ceiling, never PATCH isActive:true: phase1-backend/tests/phase16.plans-entitlements.integration.ts:124-134.

**العلاج المقترح:** In updateStoreMembership (staff.service.ts:135), before the update and inside the same transaction: `if (activeChanged && nextActive === true) { const store = await tx.store.findUnique({ where: { id: storeId }, select: { merchantId: true } }); await assertWithinPlanLimit(tx, store!.merchantId, "max_staff", 1, "أو أوقف موظفاً آخر لإعادة تفعيل هذه العضوية"); }` — and add the PATCH-at-ceiling case to phase16.plans-entitlements.integration.ts beside lines 128-131.

### 16-20-55 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:136`

**الادّعاء:** Re-activating a suspended team membership («تفعيل») is never checked against the plan's max_staff limit, so a merchant on any plan can run an unlimited active team by cycling disable → add → re-enable.

**الأثر المزعوم:** The platform owner. Staff seats are the clearest upsell lever the plans carry (free 2 → growth 10 → pro 30, prisma/migrations/20260825150000_phase16_plans_entitlements/migration.sql:69,74,79) and in a COD shop the seats are the cashiers on the till and the people answering the WhatsApp thread. A free-plan merchant gets every seat he wants with two clicks per seat in the existing dashboard UI — no script, no race — and the phase-16 acceptance («every limit refused SERVER-side even by a direct API call at the ceiling») is false for this limit. It also invalidates the owner's directory picture of who is over quota.

**الدليل المزعوم:** Usage for max_staff is the count of ACTIVE, undeleted memberships only: phase1-backend/src/services/entitlement.service.ts:74 (`storeStaffMembership.count({ where: { isActive: true, store: { merchantId }, deletedAt: null } })`). Creation is gated on that count: phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:91 (`assertWithinPlanLimit(prisma, store.merchantId, "max_staff")`) before the inserts at :97 and :110. The suspend/re-activate route is `PATCH /merchant/stores/:storeSlug/team/:membershipId` → phase1-backend/src/http/routes/team.ts:27-32, whose schema accepts `isActive: boolean` (phase1-backend/src/http/schemas/team.schema.ts:41-44) and calls `updateStoreMembership`. That function reads `nextActive = input.isActive === true` at phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:128 and writes it unconditionally at :135-144 (`tx.storeStaffMembership.update({ data: { isActive: nextActive } })`) — there is no `assertWithinPlanLimit` call anywhere in :121-152. The restore-from-trash path proves the intent that re-occupying a seat must be gated: phase1-backend/src/services/soft-delete/soft-delete.service.ts:536-542 calls `assertWithinPlanLimit(tx, …, "max_staff", 1, "أو أوقف موظفاً آخر لإعادة هذه العضوية")` when `deletedWasActive`. The client sends exactly this write from the «تفعيل» button: client/src/components/StoreTeamManager.tsx:251-253 (`PATCH … { isActive: !membership.isActive }`). Sequence on the free plan (limit 2): memberships A and B active → PATCH A `{isActive:false}` (usage now 1) → POST team C (staff.service.ts:91 sees 1+1 ≤ 2, creates) → PATCH A `{isActive:true}` (staff.service.ts:136, no check) → 3 active seats. Repeat for every further seat.

**العلاج المقترح:** In `updateStoreMembership` (phase1-backend/src/services/staff.service.ts:135), when `activeChanged && nextActive === true`, resolve the store's `merchantId` inside the transaction and call `assertWithinPlanLimit(tx, merchantId, "max_staff", 1, "أو أوقف موظفاً آخر لإعادة تفعيل هذه العضوية")` before the update — the same guard `restoreMembership` already applies at soft-delete.service.ts:541.

### 16-20-56 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/src/services/storefront.service.ts:346`

**الادّعاء:** Public storefront search ranks hidden (is_active=false) products and drops them only after the page is sliced, so hidden product names are observable from outside and search pages inside a category or a store with hidden products are silently truncated

**الأثر المزعوم:** Two costs, both on the unauthenticated storefront. (1) Lost COD sales: a shopper who opens a category and types a word sees only the in-category products that happen to fall inside the top `limit+1` store-wide matches, and `nextCursor` comes back null even though more matching active products sit further down the ranked list; the same truncation happens in any store whose merchant hides products instead of deleting them (which the soft-delete work itself documents as the common pattern). With limit 24 and a store where a quarter of the matches are hidden or out-of-category, the shopper is shown roughly 18 products and told that is all. (2) A minor confidentiality leak: anyone can build the ranked-list cursor by hand (it is base64url JSON with a shape-only check) and page with `limit=1`; a page that comes back empty at rank k with a non-empty page at rank k+1 tells him a product the merchant has hidden — an unreleased or withdrawn item — has a name matching his needle.

**الدليل المزعوم:** The engine's products statement carries `deleted_at IS NULL` but no `is_active` predicate: phase1-backend/src/services/search/postgres-trigram.engine.ts:178-191 (the tenant/live filter is only lines 185-186). `storefrontSearchIds` collects up to 200 ranked ids from that statement, hidden ones included: storefront.service.ts:414-423. `publicProducts` turns the cursor into an offset (`searchOffset`, :397-404) and slices `limit+1` ids BEFORE any visibility filter: :345-346. The hydration query is where `isActive: true` and the category filter are first applied: :347-353. `orderProductsBySearch` explicitly treats a missing row as normal (:426-433), and `pageOf` derives `hasMore`/`nextCursor` from the count of SURVIVING rows (:358-361; list-page.ts:236-241), so a hidden or out-of-category id inside the slice shortens the page and can null the cursor while later slices still hold active matches. The route is unauthenticated: routes/storefront.ts:57-60 (only `resolveStorefrontStore`). The cursor is decodable and forgeable: list-page.ts:82-85 (mint) and :96-110 (decode checks only version, kind, string value and uuid shape).

**العلاج المقترح:** Filter the whole ranked list before slicing rather than after: after `rankedIds` is computed, run one `prisma.product.findMany({ where: { storeId, isActive: true, id: { in: rankedIds }, ...(categoryIds ? { categoryId: { in: categoryIds } } : {}) }, select: { id: true } })`, keep only ids that came back (in engine order), and slice THAT list with `offset`/`limit+1`. Hidden and out-of-category products then never occupy a rank, pages are full, `nextCursor` is truthful, and the gap oracle disappears. Optionally also add `AND is_active = TRUE` to the products statement via a `liveOnly` flag on `SearchRequest` so the storefront never pays for ranking rows it cannot show.

### 16-20-57 · متوسطة · `phase1-backend/tests/pg6.arabic-search.integration.mjs:174`

**الادّعاء:** The Arabic-search acceptance «كلمة بحرف مقلوب يجد الصحيحة» is asserted only with a last-letter substitution («احمر»), which is the single most favourable case; a transposed letter, or a wrong letter inside a 4–5-letter name, scores below the 0.4 word_similarity threshold and is not found.

**الأثر المزعوم:** A merchant confirming a COD order by phone types the customer's name from hearing it: «فاظمه» for «فاطمة» (ط/ظ is the commonest Arabic misspelling), «الجوبري» for «الجبوري», «محومد» for «محمود», «امحد» for «أحمد». None of these finds the row, and the empty result is indistinguishable from «this customer is not in our system» — the same silent miss the ق-213 phone fix was written to close, now on the name path. Any two-word needle («احمد الجبوري») gets no typo tolerance at all. The gate that promised typo tolerance passes because the suite asserts the one substitution pg_trgm scores highest.

**الدليل المزعوم:** The plan's acceptance line: «البحث عن «احمد» يجد «أحمد» وعن «فاطمه» يجد «فاطمة» وعن كلمة بحرف مقلوب يجد الصحيحة» (docs/CODIQ_FULL_PLAN_26_PHASES.md:206). The suite's only tolerance assertion is «احمر» → «أحمد» (pg6.arabic-search.integration.mjs:174-175), a substitution of the LAST letter. The engine ranks with word_similarity(needle, column) and matches with `needle <% column` on single-token needles only (postgres-trigram.engine.ts:170-171,183; FUZZY_TARGETS :138), at the database threshold 0.4 with `<%` comparing >= (migration 20260904090000_pg6_arabic_search/migration.sql:58-64). pg_trgm's documented model (two leading spaces, one trailing; word_similarity = common ÷ (|needle| + |extent| − common), maximised over contiguous extents) reproduces the four values the migration recorded — 0.6, 0, 0.4, 0.3125 (migration.sql:49-51) — exactly, which calibrates the hand computation. Under that model: «احمر»→«احمد» shares 3 of 5 trigrams contiguously → 0.6 (the asserted case); «الجوبري»→«الجبوري» → 0.375; «محومد»→«محمود» → 0.333; «فامطه»→«فاطمه» → 0.333; «امحد»→«احمد» → 0.2; middle substitutions «اخمد»→«احمد» → 0.25 and «فاظمه»→«فاطمه» → 0.333 — all below 0.4, so `<%` is false and the LIKE branch (exact substring) is false too. Only a swap of the last two letters of a 4-letter word lands exactly on 0.4. The multi-word exclusion is explicit at postgres-trigram.engine.ts:170 (`!needle.normalized.includes(' ')`). Not measured against the database in this run (read-only audit); computed from the documented algorithm and cross-checked against the migration's own probes.

**العلاج المقترح:** First make the suite say what the gate says: add assertions for a transposed letter («الجوبري»→«الجبوري») and a middle substitution in a five-letter name («فاظمه»→«فاطمة») so the gap is red instead of invisible. Then either close it — for single-token needles of ≤5 letters add a second branch on the same name columns (e.g. fuzzystrmatch's levenshtein_less_equal(needle, word, 1) over the normalised column, or a lower per-needle threshold via a length-aware OR of `%` at 0.3) and re-run the plan gate — or take the deviation to the owner and rewrite the acceptance line to the substitution the engine actually honours, recorded as a ق-decision.

**الادّعاء:** The register's status ledger asserts "open" or "scheduled" for at least 14 items the code has already closed, contradicts itself on three more, and its totals and top-10 list are stale — while its own header says the Phase-26 acceptance gate is run on it.

**الأثر المزعوم:** The owner is the person who pays: the header (line 16) declares the Phase-26 gate is measured on this document and (line 18) demands file:line evidence per row, yet a planner reading sections (ب)(ج)(د)(د-2)(هـ) today would re-schedule helmet/CSP into 19-د, logout into 19, upload rate-limits into 23, zod into 19-ج, structured logging into 24-أ and the settlement-overlap guard into 20 — all already shipped — and the gate cannot be passed or failed honestly against a count of "15 closed / 42 open" that is wrong by roughly a dozen rows.
